Since WebKit's state holds the forms pages sent, a password among them when a form's response showed without a redirect, `SessionStateSeal` seals each open tab's and favorite's tab's page state with AES-GCM under a 256-bit key in the login keychain (service `xyz.taeyoon.arch.session`, account `page-state`, accessible after the first unlock), which the first save creates and each launch reads once, and `session.json` is written readable by the user only (file version 13). State from an earlier build loads as it was and is sealed at the next save; without the key, as after a denied keychain prompt, the state is left out and the tab restores at its address without its back-forward list. A tab's address loses a user name and password, which WebKit keeps in the address it reports, before it reaches the sidebar, history, and `session.json`. The session keeps its files in the folder the app gives it, the app's Application Support folder (`userDataDirectory`): `session.json`, Chromium's profiles, the plugins, the browser import's `imports.json`, and, through the stores it makes there, `history.sqlite`, `permissions.json`, and `site-search.json`, which Settings, the History window, the palette, and imports reach through the session, and the live folders' `live-folders.json`. Spaces, favorites and their tabs, tabs, folders, split views, the closed list, and the main windows persist in `session.json`; quitting keeps the open tabs because the bridge does not report the browsers that close when the runtime shuts down. A `session.json` that does not decode moves to `session.unreadable.json`, so the fresh session never overwrites it, and a file from a newer build first leaves a copy, `session-v<version>.json`. A failed backup of an unreadable or newer session is retried before a save can replace it. A failed background save records the error in the system log. Arch saves before confirming quit. If the write fails, the user can keep working or quit without the latest changes.

#### Tabs

Tabs open, close, pin, reorder, switch, and reopen; unpinned tabs not activated for a configurable number of hours are archived to the closed list by a timer and at restore. Both count the tabs windows show as active: the timer when it runs, each save, which records them as active at that moment, and a window as it closes, which records its tabs as active then, so that closing the last main window to quit keeps them too. The closed list keeps the newest 100 records, without page state, activation dates, or hooks' names; a file from an earlier build loses them at launch. Popups become tabs, selected in the window that shows the page that opened them; `window.close()` closes the tab, or unloads a pinned one (Unloading), and the window then selects the tab of the page that opened it, as a sign-in popup closes once it has reported back, while that tab is listed and no other window shows it. A page cannot bring a tab it opened forward, as with `focus()` on the window `window.open` returned or `window.open` with that window's name: CEF's Alloy style ignores Chromium's `ActivateContents`, and `WKUIDelegate` has no such callback. A page whose process quits and does not come back (WebKit at once; Chromium after its automatic reload, which it leaves out when the page crashes again within a minute of the last one) is covered by a notice with a Reload button; the session is the engines' application delegate and keeps their crash count for the tab close log. Closing a tab ends its prompts first and reads the lists again afterwards, since ending a prompt runs the code that asked for it: a close of the same tab or of another one that finishes meanwhile is not repeated and does not shift which tab goes, and moving a tab to another space looks its place up again after it leaves its split view. A tab that opens straight onto a file closes, without a closed-list entry or a history visit, once the download its own page started appears, and a pinned one unloads to its saved page; another such tab waits for its own download, which closing it earlier would cancel. A restored tab like that does not download the file again.

#### Favorites

Favorites are shared by all spaces of their Persona. The first click on a favorite opens a tab of its own in the default engine; the tab stays out of every space's list and is never archived, and a dot below the tile marks the favorite as open; a favorite without its open tab, including one whose tab from the last session waits for its click, shows its icon dimmed to the same half opacity as an unopened tab's row. Later clicks, from any space, select that same tab, so its page keeps its state across space switches; switching spaces selects the space's own tab and leaves the favorite's tab open behind it. Links and popups that the favorite's page opens in new tabs go to a space of its Persona: the current space of the window that shows it, or the Persona's preferred space. Close in the tile's context menu, ⌘W, or the page's `window.close()` ends the tab, which then opens again at the favorite's address; ⌘Z does not reopen it. Remove from Favorites closes the tab first, and a page's `beforeunload` handler can keep both. While a favorite's tab is selected, Pin or Unpin Tab, Move Tab Up and Down, Next and Previous Tab, and Close Other Tabs are disabled. `session.json` keeps each favorite's tab (address, engine, and restorable page state) and whether it was selected: at launch the selected one opens, and the others keep their dots and load where they were on their first click. A favorite's tab that no window shows unloads on its own, and its tile then dims without its dot until a window shows the tab again (Unloading).

#### Saved pages

Pinned tabs and favorites are Arch's bookmarks (`docs/bookmarks.md`; built so far: saved pages, unloading, nested folders with their dragging, the Bookmarks menu, and live folders in the sidebar with their New Live Folder sheet and the `LiveFolders` module below). Each pinned tab keeps a saved page, an address and a title (`Tab.savedPage`, bookmark rule 2): pinning a tab, by Pin Tab or a drop among the pinned tabs or into a pinned folder, saves the page it shows, or the first address its page reports when it shows none yet, and unpinning forgets it; a saved page taken before its page showed a title takes the first title the page shows at that address. `session.json` keeps the saved page in open and closed records, so a restored or reopened pinned tab keeps it, and a file before version 14 gives each pinned record, open or closed, its last address and title. A favorite's address and title are its saved page. A tab is on its saved page while its address equals the saved one apart from the fragment, `http` against `https`, the host's case, a leading `www.`, and a trailing slash, and on its saved site while the hosts match that way (`URL.isSamePage(as:)` and `isSameSite(as:)`, bookmark rule 3). Under the pointer, a pinned tab's row shows a back button (`arrow.uturn.backward`) before the close button while the tab is off its saved site, with the saved address in its tooltip. The row's context menu offers, for a pinned tab, Go Back to Saved Page while the tab is off its saved page, Use Current Page as Saved Page, which takes the tab's address and page title, and Edit Saved Address…, a sheet on the window prefilled with the saved address, where text that the address field would not read as an address beeps and changes nothing, a new address keeps the saved title only when it names the same page, and a tab without its page moves its stand-in there; and, for every tab, Copy Address, which copies the tab's current address as a URL and as text, and Duplicate, which opens and selects a copy after the tab, in its list and folder, at its current address in its engine, a pinned copy keeping the original's saved page. A favorite tile's menu offers Go Back to Saved Page while the favorite's open tab is off the favorite's address, observing the tab, so it follows the tab's page while another tab is selected, Use Current Page as Saved Page, which gives the favorite its open tab's address and page title and is disabled while another favorite of the Persona has that address, Edit Favorite… with Name and Address, which refuses such an address too and keeps the name when it is left empty, and Copy Address for the favorite's address. A favorite's tab saved from the last session counts once its first click opens it. The Tab menu has Go Back to Saved Page, Use Current Page as Saved Page, and Edit Saved Address… for the sidebar target's selected tab, without default shortcuts: Go Back needs no internal page or preview over the tab, and Edit Saved Address… a pinned tab and no other sheet on the window. Go Back loads the saved address, and a tab without its page moves its stand-in there without loading.

#### Preview panel

Holding Shift alone for 0.25 seconds while the pointer rests on a favorite, or clicking a web link in a page with Shift, shows the page in a floating preview panel over the page area, with its title and site, Open in Tab, and Close above it. A favorite previews in the default engine and a link in its page's engine. The panel's web contents belongs to no tab: the page never enters the tab list, `session.json`, or history, cannot download, and asks nothing (dialogs and sign-in requests get the safe answers, and permission requests are denied, after which Chromium's kept answers return to the remembered ones). Open in Tab loads the page the panel shows in a new selected tab of the same engine; a page the preview opens, such as a link to a new window or a sign-in popup, becomes a selected tab in place of the panel, after the previewed page, which becomes a tab without loading again, so the opened page keeps its opener. Escape (unless a field of the app's own, such as the palette's or the find bar's, has focus), ⌘W, or a click anywhere outside the panel, which still reaches what it hit, closes the panel and its web contents, except a page that has just become a tab.

#### Share and site settings

The share button before the site settings button in the address pill and File ▸ Share… show the system share picker (`NSSharingServicePicker`) for the selected tab's address. Its preview shows the page's title and icon. Services that take a subject, such as Mail, get the title. The picker opens below the button, or from the top of the window while the sidebar or the library hides it. Internal pages and windows without a tab have nothing to share. The site settings button at the end of the address pill opens a popover that switches the current tab's engine, loading its page again in the other engine in the same place, and sets the engine for new tabs. Below the engine, on a Web Store page, the extensions section starts with the store's row: Install This Extension…, which closes the popover and opens the store sheet for the page's extension in Settings ▸ Plugins through `installFromWebStore`, which the app shell sets, with the tab's Persona and space, or the window's current space for a favorite's tab; or, while the Persona has a plugin of that extension from any source, "“1Password” is installed." with the pair's name for a version. It names no Persona. With no action to list, the section's note says "Opens the extension's review in Settings before it installs." or, for an installed extension, "Manage it for this Persona in Settings ▸ Plugins." Then the section lists the actions of the enabled web extensions of the tab's Persona with their icons, labels, and badges for the tab, a pair once with the action of the version that the tab's engine runs, dimmed on a tab of an engine without extensions, where the note says on which engines' tabs extensions run; an extension that Chromium kept from the tab's site offers Allow on This Site, which allows it there; on a tab whose engine runs extensions but none of a plugin's versions, the row says where the plugin runs and, while the plugin asks for that engine's version, offers Install \<Engine> Version…, which opens Settings ▸ Plugins at the plugin with the open panel through `installEngineVersion`, which the app shell sets, with the tab's space for the review, and Dismiss; a click closes the popover and runs the action, and an action's popup shows from the site settings button of the window that shows the tab, or from the top of the window while the sidebar or the library hides the button, through `ExtensionPopupPresenter`: WebKit's own popover, or Chromium's popup page in a popover of the presenter's, sized as the page asks between 25 × 25 and 800 × 600 points and closed with it.

#### Web extensions

Each Persona's plugin manager runs its web extensions through a `WebExtensionRuntime` that the session makes with the manager, keeps in the Persona's `PersonaEngines`, and drops with it; its native messaging host manifests are in `NativeMessagingHosts/` in the app's Application Support folder. `SessionWebExtensionHost` gives the runtime the Persona's profiles in the engines with the `extensions` capability, starting them if they have not started, and reports the Persona's spaces and small windows as the extensions' windows on the next turn of the run loop after the spaces, the tabs, a tab's page, engine, space, pinned state, or folder, or a window's space, selection, or order change: each space's tabs in sidebar order, after the open favorites' tabs that a window shows on it (a favorite's tab that no window shows is in the Persona's first space), leaving out a restored tab that no window has shown, which has no page for the engines to list until it loads; its active tab is the one a main window shows there, or else the one selected last; each small window is a window of its own with its one tab; and the window that last became main is focused when it is the Persona's, as the small window itself or a main window's space. An extension's `tabs.update` selects the tab in the window that shows it, or else in the front main window that shows its space, or else in the last main window, `tabs.remove` closes it as Close does (`TabCloseAction`): a pinned tab unloads, a favorite's tab ends, and any other tab joins the closed list, and its questions about access and the engines' prompts show as cards over the tab, or over the selected tab of the window that last became main for a question without a tab. A store install that Chromium's store button asks for on a store page opens the store sheet through `installFromWebStore` in the Persona with the tab's space, or for a favorite's tab the space of the window that shows it, as the site settings popover's Web Store row does. A tab that an extension opens, with `tabs.create`, an options page, or a link its popup opens, opens in the asking engine in the space of the window it names, or else of the Persona's focused window, or else the Persona's preferred space: before the tab at the asked index among that window's tabs of the engine while that one is an open tab outside folders, else after the window's active tab while that one is, else at the end of the space's open tabs, selected when asked; the host reports the windows at once. `windows.create` opens a small window with its page, or the new tab page for none, as its one tab, which belongs to the Persona's focused space or its preferred one, and leaves the window that was key in front when it does not ask for focus; extensions see a new window that lists only that tab, and a request for several pages opens none, since a small window holds one tab. A tab that an extension asks for in a small window opens in that window's space. A tab whose engine hands over a replacement for a page it cannot load, as a WebKit tab that navigates into or out of an extension's pages does, takes it as on an engine switch: the Persona's runtime owns it, the old page's prompts and find bar go, the window that selects the tab docks the new page's tools, the old web contents closes, the tab takes the replacement's address at once, and the extensions hear of a replaced tab with the next report. A tab that has closed drops it.

#### Command palette and site search

The command palette (⌘L edits the current address, ⌘T opens a new tab) accepts URLs and search queries, which go to the search engine chosen in Settings. An address without a scheme on this Mac, such as `localhost:3000`, `myapp.localhost`, `127.0.0.1:5000`, or `[::1]:8080`, opens over http, and a dotted host or another bracketed IPv6 address over https; a single name with a number after a colon, as in `python:3`, is a search. A tab opened without an address, as at a launch without tabs or for an extension's `tabs.create({})` and `windows.create({})`, loads the home page of that search engine. A file address loads as typed, so Return on the address of a file dropped on the tab list reloads the file instead of searching for its path. Site search keeps keyword shortcuts in `site-search.json` in Application Support. It writes four defaults (`yt`, `w`, `gh`, and `m`) when the file is missing. A file that does not decode leaves the defaults in memory and moves to `site-search.unreadable.json` at the first write, so that neither Settings nor an import overwrites it. Palette text that starts with a keyword, ignoring case, and a space shows a Search \<site> for "…" row. Return opens the site's results page with the percent-encoded query in place of `%s`. With only the keyword and space, it opens the search page without a query. A keyword whose template, edited by hand in the file, makes no web address searches the web for the whole text instead. The palette also lists Ask Agent and matching agent sessions (with the agent panel, below), matching plugin commands and open tabs of the window's Persona, and history suggestions. Text that names an extension, a Web Store page's address with or without `https://` or a bare identifier, adds Install from the Web Store right after the primary row, which closes the palette and opens the store sheet for it in Settings ▸ Plugins through `installFromWebStore` with the window's Persona and current space. While the selected tab shows a store page that no internal page or preview panel covers, Install Extension from This Page follows Ask About This Page once the typed words begin its name or the text names the page's extension, as the page's address that ⌘L starts with does, and starts the same install with the tab's Persona and space, in place of Install from the Web Store for that extension (`PaletteWebStoreRows`). Neither row names a Persona, and neither shows while the app shell has not set `installFromWebStore`. Up to five commands of the enabled plugins of the window's Persona (its current space's) follow the primary row, each with its symbol and its plugin's name. A command matches when every word of the typed text starts a word of its title or keywords, ignoring case and diacritics. Clicking one closes the palette and runs it on the selected tab, or on no tab while an internal page or the preview panel covers it or the tab belongs to another Persona, as a favorite's tab can. The palette has a glass search field shaped as a rounded rectangle and, once the field has text, a glass results panel below it. Before macOS 26, both use the system material. The address pill, clicked or through Open Location (⌘L), sends the palette out along the line from the pill in 0.32 seconds. Meanwhile, the whole pill moves the same way and fades out. It returns when the palette closes. The New Tab row slides the palette in from the sidebar's side in 0.32 seconds; another shortcut, and ⌘L where no pill shows (a hidden sidebar or a small window), raises it from below in 0.16 seconds. It animates its own arrival so its contents move with it; the palette and its dimming fade out together in 0.2 seconds when it closes. Closing it gives the keys back to the page's view that takes focus: WebKit's page view, which passes them to its web view, or on Chromium the current page's view within CEF's view, the view CEF focuses, since CEF's view takes none and a page left for another site keeps its hidden view above it while in the back-forward cache. The find bar, the question popover, the tab switcher, a new small window, and Move to Main Window focus the page the same way. Selecting another tab closes it, as when a link from another app or Move to Main Window selects a tab. Each window builds a hidden palette as it opens so the first opening animates like later openings.

#### Plugins in the sidebar, menus, and pages

Below the favorites, the sidebar shows the sidebar items of the enabled plugins of the window's Persona, each with its symbol, its title, the badge its plugin sets, and its plugin's name in its tooltip; a click runs its command on the selected tab or opens its plugin's panel in a popover beside the row, which lets the panel go when it closes, and a second click closes it. A tab row's context menu and the space menu end with the plugins' items for them, one section per plugin under its name; a tab row's item runs on that tab, and a space menu item runs with the space, on the selected tab while it belongs to the space's Persona. The page context menu adds, after the app's own items, the items of the tab's Persona's plugins for what the user clicked on, which they receive with the tab. Each tab's pages run the page scripts of its Persona's enabled plugins: the session passes them to every web contents it creates for a tab, a tab takes them again when it joins the session, and every tab of the Persona takes new ones when a plugin is switched on or off, from its next document on. The values the scripts send reach the plugin manager through their tab.

#### Internal pages, find, and page fullscreen

Typing `flags://` in the palette opens an internal page over the page area. It shows the switch for all private APIs, whether this system has each API, and whether each feature is active. Close, Escape, ⌘W, selecting a tab, Back, Forward, or going to another address leaves it. While it is open, the address pill shows its address, keys do not reach the page under it, and Reload does nothing. The find bar floats over the top trailing corner of the selected tab's page (⌘F, ⌘G, ⌘⇧G, Escape). Only the window's latest search shows its result, so a late result from the tab selected before does not replace the current tab's count. A Chromium page that enters fullscreen fills the window without the sidebar or margins and takes the window into full screen. Escape, selecting another tab, closing the tab, or leaving the window's full screen ends it. The window leaves full screen with the page if it entered for that page; a page that ends fullscreen while the window is still entering takes the window out once it is in.

#### Library and downloads

The library button (⌘⇧J) pushes the whole sidebar aside. The column widens by 76 points, and the library takes the sidebar's width, with downloads at the top, followed by Reopen Closed Tab and Settings. A line sits at the center of a 16-point gap between the library and the sidebar. The sidebar's first 60 points remain visible, dimmed and still. The sidebar's own library button fades out as the library comes in, also during a swipe, so only the library's button shows in that place. Clicking this part or swiping brings the sidebar back; swiping past the first space brings the library in. In the floating card, the library fills the card, uses the same swipes, and closes when the card hides. While files download, the button shows a download symbol over their combined progress, and afterwards a badge counts the downloads that finished while the library was closed. That progress and the Clear button's state come from the session's `DownloadSummary`, so a download's progress redraws only those controls and the download's row, not the views that follow the session. A Chromium download stops when the page that started it closes, because CEF then stops reporting it and the bridge cancels it, so a Chromium page whose tab closes or switches engines while one of its downloads is in progress, paused, or interrupted stays open on a blank page, without the tab, until its own downloads no longer need it. A Chromium page that closes itself with `window.close()` during such a download closes its tab and stays open the same way; neither the page nor the page that opened it can close it while it waits, but the page that opened it can still navigate it, and the page it loads then runs unseen. A paused WebKit download resumes from WebKit's resume data, and a failed one from its resume data or, without any, from the start, through its own web view, or through a hidden web view in the same data store once the tab has closed; a second Resume before WebKit restarts it does nothing, Cancel also ends a failed download, and a download that finishes while Pause stops it stays finished; files save with an unused name to the downloads folder chosen in Settings, or the user's Downloads folder if none is chosen or the chosen folder is gone. When Settings asks where to save each download, a save panel opens in that folder. A page's first download goes on without a question, and so does one after a click in the page, in any pane on the window's screen, and one that an address Arch loads in the tab for the user starts, such as an address typed in the palette, Home, Back, Forward, or Reload, even after a refusal; any other download waits for a card over its tab that asks whether the page may download multiple files, with Don't Allow and Allow, which waits to arm; a card that ends without the user, as when the tab closes, answers no, and the answer decides the page's later downloads (`PageDownloadGate`). The gate follows the documents the main frame commits (`webContents(_:didCommitDocumentAt:)`), not the ends of their loads, so that an answer does not carry into the next document while it loads. An Allow ends when the tab commits another document. A refusal and the used first download last until the gate starts over, at a document on another site or one the user asked for, with a click in the page or through Arch; a document that the page loads on its own on its site, as by `location.reload()`, an address change, or a refresh, keeps them, so that a page cannot reload itself for another first download. A refused download ends and leaves the downloads list, so that a page that keeps trying adds no rows; one that waits for the card shows in the list meanwhile. The card's first row names the page's host, a file's name for a file page, or else this page, never the page's title. A tab that only pointed at the file asks nothing, and neither does an agent's tab, whose downloads go on only after the user's answer on the agent's card (rule P6, `docs/agents.md`). The session copies both settings into each engine's profile preferences when the engine starts and whenever the defaults change. When a download finishes while the app is not active, a local notification through `UserNotifications` names the file, and clicking it reveals the file in the Finder. Each finished download checks Arch's notification permission through the session's `NotificationAuthorization` (Permissions, sheets, and dialogs), which asks macOS only while macOS has not asked; launching does not ask. A bare build has no app bundle and posts no notifications. `DownloadNotifier` becomes the notification center's delegate once at launch, before any engine starts, and Chromium's notification service passes the calls for Arch's own notifications on to it (Permissions, sheets, and dialogs).

#### Permissions, sheets, and dialogs

Every question a tab asks shows as Arch's own card over that tab's pane (`PanePromptLayer`): a page's `alert`, `confirm`, and `prompt`, a website's permission request, an HTTP login, a leave-page check, a link for another app, a web extension's question, Clear Site Data, a page's download after its first, and a notice about the page, such as a failed capture. Each tab's prompts wait in order in the session's `TabPromptQueue`, and a pane shows its tab's oldest one while the window shows the tab and neither an internal page nor the preview panel covers it, so a prompt never covers another tab's page, each pane of a split view shows its own tab's card, and cards of different tabs show at once in different panes and windows. A prompt of a tab that no window shows waits; the tab's sidebar row and favorite tile mark it, shown or waiting (`exclamationmark.bubble.fill`, "Waiting for your answer"). The card hangs from 3 points above the page frame, in the window's margin, which a page cannot draw in, and comes down from behind that line. It is as tall as its content, up to the pane's height less 16 points, and a taller one scrolls between its first row and its buttons; in a split view it leaves room for the pane's close control. Its first row names who asks, from the tab's address, the address of the page's document for its dialogs, the protection space, or the extension, never from page text, with the site's icon, a lock for a site reached over https, and where a frame from another site is. A page's own words sit in a box apart from Arch's words. The card dims the page and takes its clicks, scrolls, and gestures, while the sidebar, the palette, other panes, other windows, the menus, and docked developer tools go on working; its surface is the opaque window background, as the find bar's is, because a material over the engine's view cannot sample it. The card takes the keyboard focus as it shows in the focused pane while nothing has it, or the pane's page or a field over the page has it, the find bar's or the question popover's, which report their focus (`PageField`). A field in the sidebar, the palette, the preview panel, the floating sidebar, another pane, or another window keeps it, also where it lies over the pane, and closing the palette gives it to the card. Otherwise the card takes the focus when the user clicks it or its dimming, selects its tab, or the page would take the focus, and focus that moves into the page or those fields returns to it. Selecting another pane's tab from the sidebar moves the focus off a card to that tab's page or card, so only the focused pane's card takes the keys. Only while it has the focus, Return presses its default button, which it draws as the prominent one, and Escape or ⌘. its cancel button; Return never presses a destructive button, so Clear Site Data's Return and Escape both press Cancel. A button that grants something (a permission's Allow, Open for another app, an extension's Allow, a page's Allow for more downloads) stays dimmed for half a second after the card shows, its tab comes back, or its window becomes key, and on such a card Escape waits as long, since its cancel answer is remembered too. While the selected tab has a prompt, find, the question popover, printing, Export as PDF, and Clear Site Data wait. VoiceOver reads the card as "\<kind>, \<who asks>" and not the page under it, and announces a card that shows without the focus. A site permission card names the request's site and the permission's symbol, and says that Arch remembers the answer. Arch remembers Allow and Don't Allow per origin (scheme, host, and a port other than the default) and permission type in `permissions.json`; the system's own camera and microphone prompt follows Allow. Arch grants a request without a card only when every type in it has a remembered Allow. One remembered Don't Allow denies it without a card. Screen recording and pages without an http or https host always ask. Don't Allow, clicked or through Escape, is remembered; a card that ends without the user, as when its tab closes, is not. Identical requests from one tab that arrive while their card waits share its answer. The session's `SitePermissionAnswers` copies the remembered answers into each engine's profile when the engine starts, after every change, and after each answer, so an answer the user did not give does not stay with the engine. `permissions.json` also lists the answers removed in Settings ▸ Websites until a new answer replaces them, and a profile that starts takes those away too, so a profile that was not running at a removal asks again. The Agents Persona, which the session takes from `agent.json` when it is created, before any engine starts, is the exception to Persona rule 6 (`docs/agents.md`, agent decision 9): its profiles take no remembered answers, and its requests are denied without a card, remembered nowhere, and cleared from the profile again; once `agent.json` no longer names it, its running profiles follow the remembered answers. The default Persona is never agent-only. JavaScript `alert`, `confirm`, and `prompt` cards are headed by the page's host on both engines: that of the document the main frame last committed (`webContents(_:didCommitDocumentAt:)`), or before the first one, that of the frame that shows the dialog, not the tab's address, which on WebKit is already that of a navigation the page started, so that a page cannot ask under the site of an address it starts to load. Both engines name the origin of the frame that shows the dialog (`frameOrigin`). Words from a frame on another host than the page's carry the caption "An embedded page on “example.com” says" with that host, as a login's realm carries "The site says". Words from a frame whose origin the engine does not name carry "An embedded page says" on a page with a host: a frame with an opaque origin, or on Chromium, which names a frame by its address, an `about:blank`, `about:srcdoc`, `data:`, or `blob:` frame, which any frame of the page can add and script; on a page without a host they count as the page's. A WebKit page whose own origin is opaque, as under a `sandbox` Content Security Policy, gets that caption too, since neither engine says whether the frame is the main one. VoiceOver reads an embedded page's host, or an embedded page, as the author of its words, also in the announcement of a card that shows without the focus. When the user reloads, goes back or forward, or loads another address while a page dialog is open, the dialog gets its safe answer and closes, and the page cannot open another until that load ends; Back or Forward without an entry leaves the dialog open. Such a cancellation ends only the page's own dialogs: a permission, login, leave-page, or other-app question stays, and the tab's next prompt follows. Closing a tab or switching its engine ends its prompts without the user. Closing a tab on the Chromium engine, from its row, Close Tab, or a favorite's Close or Remove from Favorites, asks with a leave-page card when the page's `beforeunload` handler wants to stay, unless one of its downloads is in progress, paused, or interrupted, when the tab closes at once. While that card waits, closing the tab again only selects the tab to show the card, since the card is the question; any other close request first ends the tab's prompts, so that a page that waits on its own dialog can answer its `beforeunload` check. WebKit has no public `beforeunload` callback, so its tabs close at once. A card for the tab whose page fills the window in fullscreen, as Chromium's pages do, ends that fullscreen as it first shows; WebKit's own fullscreen window stays in front of the card, as it stayed in front of a sheet, since ending it would also end the page's other media presentations. Quitting ends every tab's prompts without the user, each tab's oldest first, before the engines shut down, so no card holds the app open; a quit that a window's own sheet stops lets the prompts wait for answers again. When a site or proxy asks for HTTP authentication, a card asks for a user name and password on both engines. It names the server, and a proxy as such, shows the site's realm as the server's words, says when the last attempt failed and then shakes once, warns when the password would go unencrypted, and keeps the credential in memory for the session only; a proxy's card shows no lock, since the protection space does not say whether the connection to the proxy is encrypted. Its password field is secure, and both fields name their content types for password managers. For a server other than the tab's site, as for an image from another site, the focus goes to the card itself, not to a field, so that keys meant for the page never reach a field whose text goes to that server; the user clicks or tabs into User Name. A `prompt` field starts with the page's default text selected, which AppKit does as a field takes the focus. A card that leaves after its prompt ended keeps showing what its fields held until it has gone, and then lets the text go. A page's file chooser opens Arch's own panel on both engines (`FileChooserPanel`, through `Tab.chooseFiles`, which the session sets). It waits among the tab's prompts as a `fileChooser` prompt with its panel (`PromptState.panel`) and shows, in place of a card and without the dimming, as a sheet on the window that shows the tab once no other sheet is on that window (`PanePromptLayer`): an open panel for one file, several files, or a folder, offering only the page's accepted types when each has a declared file type and otherwise every file, or a save panel with the suggested name, each with a message that names who asks from the engine's report (`FileChooserPanel.asker(of:)`), never from the tab's address, which can be one that the page started loading: the asking frame's host, with the page's when they differ, such as "Choose a file for “example.com”." or "Choose a file for “widget.example” in “example.com”.", or on Chromium, which names no frame, the page's committed host, as in "Choose a file for a page on “example.com”.", and a folder's adds that the page receives every file in it. Cancel, closing the tab, switching its engine, and quitting give the page no file; quitting ends a panel that shows with Cancel before AppKit decides whether to quit, since AppKit stops a quit for a sheet with more than one button. A panel whose tab leaves the window's screen, as when another window takes the tab, ends with Cancel instead of showing again, and the page can ask again. The preview panel's page gets no file. Window-level questions stay the system's sheets and alerts: Delete Persona, a Persona's name, the quit confirmations, History's Clear History, the imports, and the print, save, and open panels.

Arch's own notification permission from macOS is one for the app bundle, which the session's `NotificationAuthorization` reads at launch, whenever Arch becomes active, as after a visit to System Settings, and after each request; the download notifier, the permission card, and Settings ▸ Websites share it. On Chromium, Arch's engine posts websites' notifications through `UserNotifications` from the app's process, the client macOS lists for the bundle, so they follow Arch's permission and the user chooses banners or alerts for Arch in System Settings ▸ Notifications (`arch-8078-6`, engine changes table). Chromium 156 itself posts every browser notification through the deprecated `NSUserNotificationCenter`, banners from the app's process and notifications that stay until clicked from `Arch Helper (Alerts)`; macOS refused the app process's posts because the bundle also uses `UserNotifications` (`usernoted` logged "Legacy client xyz.taeyoon.arch connecting to modern client. You can't mix modern clients with legacy clients." at every launch from 2026-11-13), and the engine no longer starts the Alerts helper, which `scripts/bundle_app.sh` still builds. The engine's service makes itself the notification center's delegate while it runs, passes the calls for Arch's own notifications to the delegate it replaced, and puts that delegate back when it ends, so `DownloadNotifier` becomes the delegate once at launch, before any engine starts, and never takes the role back or passes anything on. Chromium replaces the app's notification categories with its own, so Arch's own notifications use none. A website's notification has no Settings action, Chromium removes websites' notifications when Arch quits, unless the process exits before macOS answers its request for them, and download notifications stay; without Chromium's private calls, which the engine leaves out, a click is expected to remove the notification and bring Arch forward, macOS's default, and a notification that replaces one with the same tag presents again. A card for a website's notifications takes its note and buttons from the permission each time it shows, since it can wait in its tab's queue while macOS's answer to another tab's card changes the permission (`PromptState.makeContent`), and again when the permission changes while it shows, as after a visit to System Settings, and its answer acts on the permission it showed: before macOS has asked, it says that macOS asks after Allow; while Arch's notifications are off, that the website's notifications won't show, with a third button, Open System Settings, apart from the answers at the card's leading edge, which opens Arch's entry in System Settings ▸ Notifications (an undocumented address; without its anchor, Notifications opens), denies the page, and remembers nothing, so the website can ask again; in a bare build, that it can't show notifications. Allow answers the page at once, because Chromium holds the tab's next permission request until this one is answered and macOS's question can stay on screen for long, and then asks macOS on a card that said it would; a refusal there leaves the website's Allow in place. A remembered Allow asks neither. The engine asks macOS for nothing before it posts, so a notification that the page shows while macOS's question is unanswered, as a page that shows one as soon as its request resolves does, is not shown, and nothing shows it again once macOS allows. Switching a site's Notifications to Allow in Settings ▸ Websites asks macOS as well, and while a site is allowed and macOS has not asked or keeps Arch's notifications off, a group at the top of Settings ▸ Websites offers Turn On Notifications… or Open System Settings. A page's `window.focus()` or its service worker's `client.focus()`, as after a click on its notification, reaches the bridge through `CefLifeSpanHandler::OnActivate`, and the session selects the page's tab and brings its window and Arch forward, as for the return button of a picture-in-picture window: a tab that no window shows goes to the front main window on its space, or else to the front main window of its Persona, and only without either to the sidebar target, so that a window of another Persona keeps its Persona / Space. A service worker's `clients.openWindow()` reaches it through `CefRequestContextHandler::OnOpenTab` and opens the address in a new selected tab of the profile's Persona, in the current space of the Persona's front main window, or without one in the Persona's preferred space, whose browser the bridge hands to Chromium as soon as CEF has created it, so that the worker's promise resolves with the tab's window; CEF makes the global context without a handler, so the bridge gives its storage one through a context that shares it. None of this has run in the bundle yet (regression check 29, VI.7 and VI.8). On the development Mac, Arch's first request on 2026-11-13 ended without a grant, and macOS keeps Arch's notifications off. WebKit refuses a page's request without asking the app, so the site settings popover's note on a WebKit tab says that websites can't show notifications there.

#### Links for other apps and the page context menu

A link with a scheme neither engine loads, such as `mailto:`, does not navigate the page and opens no tab: when macOS has an app for it, a card with that app's name and icon asks before the link opens there, and its Open waits to arm. A tab asks one such question at a time. After the user chooses Cancel, clicked or through Escape, the site (its host, or its scheme for a page without one) asks no more on its own until the tab moves to another site; a link the user clicks or an address the user types still asks. Right-clicking a page shows the same menu on both engines, built by the tab: up to five spelling suggestions or No Guesses Found (on Chromium, the app asks the system spell checker for suggestions in the system's preferred languages if the profile's one spelling language has none for a marked word), then Open Link in New Tab (web links only, as a background tab in the page's space and engine) and Copy Link, Open Image in New Tab and Copy Image Address, then Cut, Copy, Paste, and Select All in a field, or Copy for a selection that is not on a link, image, or media; Back, Forward, and Reload show only when none of those apply. AppKit then adds its own items on both engines: AutoFill in a field, Services in a field with a selection and on a link, and Writing Tools where macOS offers it, and none elsewhere. On Chromium, a password field gets AutoFill and neither Services nor Writing Tools: the bridge turns on Arch's engine's `SetContextMenuPasswordAutoFill` for a field's menu, from right before the presenter chooses the menu's view until the menu closes, and meanwhile Chromium's page view gives a password field a text input context and offers its text to no service; whether the password that AppKit fills after the menu has closed reaches the field is unverified (VI.9). Writing Tools uses AppKit's panel rather than WebKit's inline editing (VI.10), and a right click on a field's caret keeps the caret where WebKit selects the word before it; regression check 61 covers these in the app.

#### Zoom, printing, and PDF

Zoom steps through the same factors on both engines (0.5 to 3). Arch remembers each host's factor in the `ARCHZoomFactorsByHost` defaults dictionary. A tab takes its host's factor, or 1, after each load. Changes apply to every open tab of that host. A page without a host keeps a zoom change only until its next load. File ▸ Print… (⌘P) and File ▸ Export as PDF… work the same on both engines. The tab's engine renders the page as PDF. Print runs PDFKit's print operation as a sheet on the window. Export as PDF saves through a save panel sheet named after the tab. Both are disabled without a selected tab, under an internal page, while the tab has a prompt, while a PDF renders, and while the window shows a sheet. Leaving a tab while its PDF renders drops the request. A failure shows a notice card on the tab, with a file's path truncated in the middle and whole in its tooltip.

#### Developer tools

⌥⌘I toggles the selected Chromium tab's developer tools. They dock inside the page area, to the right of the page by default, or below or to the left from the Developer menu. The page narrows by their size, and the engine's view of the tools fills the space beside it in the page's rounded frame, so the browser window stays the only window and the sidebar card, popovers, and menus draw over the tools. Dragging the margin between the page and the tools resizes them. The tools show at the saved size, made smaller where it would leave the page less than 200 points but not below 200 points of their own, and never larger than the page area; a drag stays within the same limits, so a size saved in a wide window or dragged past the window's edge never pushes the sidebar, the page, or the handle out of a smaller window. Undock moves the same view, and with it the tools' state, into a window of their own named "Developer Tools – " followed by the tab's title, at a saved frame and cascaded from another such window; choosing a side again brings the view back into the page area, and the window's close button closes the tools. The side and sizes persist in `UserDefaults` and apply to every window. A tab's docked tools hide while another tab is selected and while a page fills the window in fullscreen, and dock in the window the tab moves to; floating tools keep their window. While the window of floating tools is key, the Developer menu acts on those tools, whichever tab the active window shows: ⌥⌘I closes them, and a side other than Undock docks them, so they show beside their tab's page once a window selects it. Every window's dock and the tools' window hear each tools object's opening and closing (`DevToolsUndockedWindows`). Closing a tab closes its tools, also those of a page kept open for its downloads.

#### Unloading

Closing an unkept agent tab, including through Delete Persona, discards it without adding a closed tab or an undo entry. The tab and its window selection stay out of the saved session. Automatic unloading skips agent tabs. Agent folders stay at the top of the open list and refuse nested folders or incoming tabs. Moving a tab out or choosing Keep Tabs returns it to ordinary browsing.

Closing a pinned tab unloads it (`docs/bookmarks.md`, Unloading; bookmark rule 4): once its page agrees to leave, the page closes as a closed tab's does and the tab keeps its place, folder, and ID with an `UnloadedWebContents` at its saved page, without page state, whose row shows the site's icon; nothing joins the closed list or the tab close log, and ⌘Z then reopens none of the tabs closed before it. `TabCloseAction.of(_:reason:)` decides this on every path that closes a tab (`BrowserSession.closeTab(_:reason:)` and `requestCloseTab(_:)`): ⌘W, the row's button, Close Tab, an extension's `tabs.remove`, the page's `window.close()`, a crashed page that the engine closes, and a tab that only pointed at a file unload a pinned tab, which then downloads the file when opened again; a favorite's tab ends; a live folder item's tab is dismissed, apart from one that only pointed at a file or whose page crashed, which unloads (Live folders below); any other tab closes into the closed list, except one that only pointed at a file; and Delete Persona closes its pinned and open tabs into the closed list (reason Persona in the tab close log) and its item tabs without it. Delete Tab in a pinned tab's context menu closes it into the closed list, and ⌘Z reopens it pinned in its folder with its saved page. Under the pointer, a pinned row's close button is a minus with the tooltip Unload, shown only while the tab has its page, and rows of tabs without pages show their icons and titles dimmed (`SidebarDimming.unopened`), as rows of tabs restored from the last session do, unless they are selected. The selection after any close or unload passes over pinned tabs without their page (`BrowserSession.tabToSelect`), so ⌘W loads no pinned page the user closed and two pinned tabs whose saved pages are files cannot download each other in turn; with only such tabs left, the window shows the empty page. Pinned tabs and favorites' tabs that no window shows unload on the archive timer after the hours of Settings ▸ Sidebar (`ARCHUnloadAfterHours`, 12 by default, 0 for never), and, while that setting is not 0, every tab that no window shows unloads when a `DispatchSource` memory pressure source reports a warning, after 30 minutes unshown, or critical pressure, at once (`TabUnloading.tabsToUnload`, bookmark rule 5). A tab's last activation is when a window last selected it or stopped showing it. An automatic unload keeps the tab's place, a favorite's tab in its favorite, and a stand-in with its address, title, icon, and engine page state, so a WebKit tab returns with its back list and a Chromium tab loads its last address. It skips a tab that plays sound or media through its documents' audio and video elements (Web Audio and elements outside the document go unseen, which the Settings footer says), shows a video in picture in picture, also paused or muted (`EngineWebContents.isInPictureInPicture`), uses the camera or microphone (`EngineWebContents.isCapturingMedia`), holds text the user typed since its main frame's document loaded (`TabUnloading.inputScript`, a page script in the world `arch.unloading` that runs first in every frame), shows a site allowed to post notifications, has a prompt waiting, downloads that need its page, or open developer tools, opened a page that a window shows, as a sign-in popup, or is in a small window. While the delay is Never, the footer says that tabs keep their pages under memory pressure too. It answers a Chromium page's leave-page question with Stay without a card, which keeps the page loaded until the next try; a close the user asks for meanwhile shows the question. A Chromium tab that a window shows while its page has not answered yet still unloads if the page agrees, and loads again at its last address (`docs/bookmarks.md`, risk 9). None of this has run in the app (regression check 69).

#### Bookmarks menu

The Bookmarks menu, between Spaces and History, lists the bookmarks for the sidebar target (`docs/bookmarks.md`, Bookmarks menu; bookmark rule 7). `BookmarkMenu.entries(for:personas:spaces:favorites:tree:unreadCounts:)` gives its contents as values, which the app turns into menu items each time the menu opens: the favorites of the sidebar target's Persona in their grid order, headed by the Persona's name with two or more Personas, then, for each Persona in order and apart from the other Personas' by a divider, each space whose pinned list holds a tab as a submenu titled with the space's name, or "Work / Research" with two or more Personas (Persona rule 7). A space's submenu holds its pinned folders as nested submenus, each with its folders before its tabs, then its pinned tabs outside folders; folders and spaces that hold no pinned tab at any depth are left out, and collapsed folders list what they hold. A tab is named as its row names it and a favorite by its title, or its host without one, each with its address as the tooltip. Without favorites and pinned tabs the menu shows a disabled No Bookmarks. While no main window is open, the menu lists the favorites of the Persona of the window that would reopen (`BrowserSession.sidebarTargetPersonaID`) without opening it. Choosing a pinned tab selects it in the sidebar target as a click on its row does (window rule 2), which takes the window to the tab's space of any Persona; choosing a favorite opens it as a click on its tile does; the window then comes forward, out of the Dock if it is minimized (`BrowserSession.openBookmark(_:)`). The menu's items have no shortcuts, and Settings ▸ Shortcuts lists none of them. A live folder's submenu shows its unread count as a badge, and its unread items carry a dot.

#### Live folders

Live folders in the sidebar and the New Live Folder sheet that makes them are built (`docs/bookmarks.md`, Live folders ▸ The sidebar and The sheet; live folder rules 1 to 8). `TabFolder.live` keeps a live folder's source, item limit, and refresh interval, a private feed's address without its query, and `TabRecord.liveItemID` each item tab's item (`session.json` version 16); loading pins a live folder, lifts a folder out of one, takes records without an item, or of another space, out of one, and drops items outside live folders. The session registers its live folders with `LiveFolderService` at launch and whenever their configurations change, which removes the store entries and Keychain items of folders, spaces, and Personas that went, apart from those of folders that a set-aside `session.unreadable.json` may hold, and a live folder configuration that does not decode loads its folder as an ordinary one; it plans each folder's tabs from its stored items at launch and at each start and end of a refresh, passes Arch's activation and a main window showing a space as triggers, and stops the service at quit. A plan adds a pinned tab without a page for each new item, saved at the item's address and title in the default engine, retitles, reorders by the source, and removes tabs without pages that no window shows, or will show once the saved session's windows reopen, for items the source dropped, without the closed list or the tab close log. Item tabs are never restored tabs, post no `tabOpened` to hooks, take no Tab Names, and show their item's title while on their saved page; their stand-ins request no site icon, and their rows show the feed site's `/favicon.ico`, loaded once per folder and launch, or a GitHub item's state symbol, with the item's details in the tooltip. Closing an item tab by ⌘W, the row's Dismiss button, Close Tab, the page's `window.close()`, or an extension's `tabs.remove` dismisses its item without the closed list; Show Dismissed Items brings dismissed items back. A tab moved out of its folder in any way becomes an ordinary tab, pinned with its saved page or open without one, and the folder gets a new tab for the item at once, marked read; nothing else enters a live folder (`FolderTree` refuses it as a target of tabs and folders), and its tabs keep the source's order. A window showing an item tab, or stopping showing it, marks its item viewed, apart from an item marked unread while shown; the tab's dot follows its item, also when the tab is selected again, and the folder's row shows its source's symbol, the unread count, a progress indicator while it refreshes or waits for a slot, and, after a failure, a warning symbol with the error and the next attempt. The folder's menu has Refresh Now, Mark All as Read, Show Dismissed Items, Edit Live Folder…, Rename Folder, Convert to Folder, which keeps the tabs as ordinary pinned tabs and forgets the source, and Delete Folder, which closes the item tabs without closed records once their pages agree; an item tab's has Mark as Read or Mark as Unread, Dismiss, Rename Tab, Copy Address, Go Back to Saved Page while off it, Open in Split View, Add to Favorites, and Move to Folder, and no Duplicate, Delete Tab, or saved page edits. Browser import makes a folder named like a live folder beside it and counts no item tab's address as pinned. `swift test --filter LiveFolderTabsTests` and the live folder tests of `SessionSnapshotTests`, `TabUnloadingTests`, `BookmarkMenuTests`, and `SidebarImportTests` cover the parts without a session; the rest has not run in the app (regression check 72).

New Live Folder…, after New Folder in the space menu, opens a sheet on the window titled "New Live Folder in \<space>", or "New Live Folder in Work / Research" with two or more Personas (Persona rule 7), and Edit Live Folder… in a live folder's menu opens it with the folder's values, titled "Edit Live Folder in \<space>" the same way, once it has read a private feed's whole address from the Keychain. `LiveFolderSheetModel` holds the sheet's state and decisions, `LiveFolderSheetView` draws it as a SwiftUI form in an AppKit sheet window, and the window model presents it. Feed takes an address, prefilled with the selected tab's when it shows a web page, read as `https` without a scheme, and refused with the reason when it is not `http` or `https` or holds a user name or password; an address with a query shows the Keychain note. Add loads it through `FeedFetcher.discover(_:)`: a feed is used directly, a page's only feed loads as a feed at once, a page's several feeds show as choices, and a page without feeds, a failed load, or a link that is no feed shows why. GitHub takes a host (github.com by default; a pasted address gives its host and port, and a change that names the same host keeps `gh`'s state) and My Pull Requests, Review Requests, Assigned Issues, or Search with its text, with a status line from `GitHubCLI.shared`'s `status(host:)`, checked 0.3 seconds after the host or the source changes, Open Terminal (`BrowserSession.openTerminal`, which the app shell sets), and Check Again, which reads the login shell again; Add waits until `gh` is signed in to the host. An empty name takes the feed's title, its link's, or its host, or the search's name; Items is 10, 25, or 50, and Refresh 15 minutes, 30 minutes, 1 hour, or 3 hours, by default 30 minutes for a feed and 15 for GitHub. With 20 live folders, Add is disabled and the sheet says "Arch keeps up to 20 live folders." Add writes a private feed's whole address to the Keychain through `LiveFolderService.setPrivateAddress(_:for:folderName:)`, then puts the folder after the space's folders among its pinned tabs, expanded, and shows the space; registering it refreshes it (`BrowserSession.addLiveFolder(_:toSpace:)`). Save writes a changed private address the same way and changes the folder's name and configuration (`editLiveFolder(_:with:)`), and a private feed's new name, from Save or Rename Folder, relabels its Keychain item, so registering starts the folder over when its source changed and refreshes it when its item limit changed; an Address field that keeps its first text needs no load and no Keychain write, and an edit that keeps a GitHub search needs no signed-in `gh`. A failure stays in the sheet. `swift test --filter LiveFolderSheetTests` covers the sheet's model; the sheet on a window has not run in the app (regression check 73).

#### Tab switcher and tab close log

⌃Tab opens the tab switcher, a glass panel centered over the window. It lists the current space's tabs and the open tabs of its Persona's favorites, most recently selected first. Tabs not selected since launch follow by their last activation. While Control stays down, Tab moves the highlight to the next less recent tab and ⇧Tab moves it back, wrapping at either end. Releasing Control selects the highlighted tab and focuses its page, closing any open command palette. Escape, another key, a click, or leaving the app closes the switcher without switching. Local key monitors take these keys before a focused page on either engine sees them. The session logs each tab close during the current run with its reason (the user, the page itself, the page after its process quit, archiving by the timer or at restore, a tab that only pointed at a file, a web extension, or Delete Persona), time, title, address, engine, the tab's crash count, and the engines' crash total. View ▸ Developer ▸ Show Tab Close Log shows the newest 500 in a table window that ⌘W closes. The log is kept in memory only.

#### Captures and site data

View ▸ Capture Viewport, Capture Full Page, and Capture Region… save a PNG file at the screen's pixels per point to `~/Pictures/Arch Captures`. They name it after the tab and time (for example `Title 2026-10-25 at 14.03.09.png`, with a number added when the name is taken) and select it in the Finder. Capture Region… dims the page and crops the viewport capture to the rectangle dragged over it. Escape cancels; selecting another tab or opening an internal page ends it. A failed capture or write shows a notice card on the tab. History ▸ Clear Site Data… and the Site data section of the site settings popover ask with a card over the tab, whose Clear is destructive and takes no key, and which with several Personas names the Persona and space, as "Work / Research". They then remove, in the tab's Persona, cookies from both engines together: cookies of the selected page's host and its subdomains, and domain cookies of its parent domains. An engine that has not started yet starts first. They clear each engine's other data for the host's origins and those of the site's open tabs in that Persona (WebKit for the whole registrable domain, the unit of its data records; Chromium per origin, keeping its HTTP cache), then reload the site's tabs without the cache. Cookie copies wait while the removal runs, and the removal waits for copies already running, so no copy brings a removed cookie back.

#### Now Playing

Now Playing shows the selected tab's media while it plays sound. Otherwise, it shows the tab that played sound last (tabs still playing first). It shows the page's Media Session title, artist, album, and largest artwork, or the page's title and site, plus its playback state, duration, and position. The artwork loads as site icons do, outside every Persona's store and without cookies or stored credentials, within 30 seconds and up to 5 MB. Play, pause, play/pause, and, when the page handles them, next and previous track, from the media keys or Now Playing, go to that tab: the page's handler runs, or the media itself plays or pauses. Now Playing clears once no tab has media. The app leaves Now Playing and the media keys to other apps until a tab first plays sound.

#### Page reader, page questions, and hooks

While a provider can serve them, the page reader and page questions (`PageAssistant`) read pages through `AIPageSource` and send them only through `AIService`; a page read ends at once when the question or hook that made it is cancelled, and the engine's late answer is dropped. View ▸ Show Page Reader opens a 340-point panel beside the page, which narrows; opening it summarizes the selected tab's page unless its conversation has begun or AI is off for its site, and the field below it asks follow-up questions that carry the page's current selection, unless it lies in or reaches a field or an editable area. Replies stream with Stop and show the model, tokens, and estimated cost under each answer, and the AI module's errors show with Open AI Settings, Allow AI on This Site, Start Over, or Retry: a question that went out with earlier ones and was too long for the model offers Start Over, which clears the conversation and asks it again on its own, and a question too long on its own, or for a command-line tool that gets no page data, offers Open AI Settings. Each tab keeps its conversation in memory until it closes or moves to another site; another page of the same site joins the conversation with the next question. Typing the start of "Ask About This Page" in the palette offers that command while neither an internal page nor the preview panel covers the tab, and Ask About Selection in the page context menu asks about selected text outside fields; both open a popover over the page's top trailing corner, below the find bar, that answers one question at a time and closes on Escape, when another tab is selected, when the preview panel opens, or when the tab loads another page. Replies render inline Markdown without images; each link shows its full address, and a web link opens only when clicked, as a page the tab's page opened: in a new tab of the tab's Persona, selected in the window that shows the tab, or in a small window of its own beside a small window. On a site where AI is off, the panel and the popover say so and offer Allow AI on This Site instead of reading the page, and the site settings popover has the same switch. `BrowserHooks` turns the session's browser events (Events module) into the AI interface's hook events under each bus event's identifier: finished page loads, new tabs other than restored ones and the pinned tabs a browser import opens, and finished downloads, never from agent tabs. It ends a tab's hooks when the tab commits another document, ignoring the fragment, and when it closes, and a download's when the user removes it from the list, and applies the actions that hooks return while they still fit the event. Its built-in hooks are automatic features, off until the user turns them on and gives them a model in Settings ▸ AI. Tab Names reads a space's tab's page 1.5 seconds after its main frame loads a document, skipping the first load of a restored tab and of a pinned tab a browser import opened, a tab the user named, a page it cannot read, and a page it named already or whose name the user turned down, and asks for a name of at most four words in the page's language; the name shows as the tab's suggested title, at most 60 characters, until the tab moves to another address or the user names the tab; renaming it to nothing or to the page's title shows the page's title, and hooks leave the tab until it moves to another address. Download Names skips a file whose name the user chose in a save panel, sends a finished download's name, media type, size, source address, and page address, skips a download whose source or page is on an excluded site, and renames the file without `/`, `:`, control characters, or dots at its ends, with its own extension, in at most 80 characters and an unused name; the row then offers Restore Original Name, and the download's notification opens the file's folder once the file is renamed. New Agent Task and View ▸ Show Agent Panel show the agent panel (`AgentPanelColumn`, `BrowserWindowModel.agentPanel`) in the trailing column of the active window, a small window included, in place of the page reader, at its width, with the `Agent` module's composer, which `agentComposerContext(text:)` fills with the window's current space as "Persona / Space" (for a small window, the space Move to Main Window would use) and the selected tab unless an internal page or the preview covers it, or one session's conversation, whose replies render through `PageReply.text`. A link in the panel opens as a page the window opened: a tab of a main window, or a small window of its own beside a small window. A step's tab button selects the tab in a main window; a small window shows only its own tab, so the main window selects any other tab and comes forward. A small window's tab brings its small window forward instead from either window, and a closed tab's button does nothing. Only one of the two panels shows. The palette's Ask Agent row (`AskAgentOffer`) follows the primary row once the text has two or more words while a Claude Code or Codex model can run a session. A click on it starts a session at once with the text as its task, in the window's place, with the selected tab unless it shows no web page or its site is excluded (`AgentComposerContext.sessionStart(task:mode:allowsAI:)`), in Settings' mode for new sessions and on the `agent` route's model, or the first preset model when the route has none; a start the service refuses shows the composer with the task and the refusal's sentence instead. Rows for live sessions whose titles contain the text, with their "Persona / Space", show those sessions in the panel. Continue as Agent Task, beside Ask in the page reader and the question popover while a model can run a session, closes the popover and opens the composer with the question typed in the field, or else the last question the user asked (a summary gives none), and the selected tab; the selected text and the replies stay out of the task. The sidebar's bottom bar shows the agents indicator (`AgentsIndicator`) at its trailing end while a session's turn runs or an outside client is connected, with a menu of the acting sessions and clients and Stop All Agents, and a dot on the symbol of another space while a session there waits for the user (`AgentWaitingDot`), which shows once VII.17.1 sets that state.

### History module

`history.sqlite` records one visit per page change per tab and serves palette suggestions ordered by visit count. The palette reads them off the main thread each time its text changes, so neither typing nor its other updates wait for the query. The History window (⌘Y, History ▸ Show All History) lists each page once under the day of its last visit, newest first, with its title, address, and time. Its toolbar search field filters by title or address. Double-clicking, Return, or Open in New Tab opens the selected pages in new tabs of the last main window, or of a new one while none is open, and brings it forward. Copy Link copies their addresses. Delete or the context menu removes them, in one transaction. Clear History…, in the window's toolbar or the History menu, which brings the window forward, removes the pages last visited in the last hour, today, today and yesterday, or all history. The window reloads from the store each time it opens or becomes key, reading off the main thread; a read followed by a later reload or deletion is dropped. ⌘W closes the window, and while its sheet shows, Close Tab is disabled. See Decisions. Imported pages arrive through `importEntries` in batches of 1,000 rows, each committed separately and queued only once the one before has committed, so that visits, suggestions, and the window's reads wait for one batch at most; an update applies only when it changes the page, and the caller can learn how many pages the import added or changed, each address once. A batch whose statement fails rolls back and ends the import. A deletion during an import, of the window's rows or through Clear History…, also holds for the batches the import has not written: they leave out the pages it removed, so the result is as if the deletion came after the import. Failed statements go to the system log under the subsystem `xyz.taeyoon.arch`, category History. At quit the app waits until every requested import and visit is written (`finishWrites()`). `HistoryTests` checks the suggestions, reads between import batches, the wait at quit, and removal on temporary stores.

### Import module

File ▸ Import Bookmarks and History… opens an open panel sheet on the last main window for one or more files. It recognizes each by its contents: HTML bookmark files, Chromium-format Bookmarks JSON, a WebKit-format Bookmarks.plist (binary or XML), or a Chromium-format History SQLite database. It reads the database from a copy in a temporary folder with its journal or write-ahead log, since the owning browser may hold it open and locked, copies the files again while any changed during a copy and reports the database as unreadable after three such copies, fails with the copy's error when a journal or log that did not change does not copy, as when macOS lets Arch read only the chosen database, copies a symbolic link's target rather than the link, reports a database it cannot read to its last row as unreadable, and deletes the copy once read; each import first deletes copies left when Arch quit during an import's read. Bookmarks with web addresses become favorites in file order, with folders flattened. Arch skips addresses already in favorites and excludes the WebKit-format reading list. History rows with web addresses that the file does not mark hidden go into `HistoryStore` with their titles, visit counts, and last visits. A page already in the store keeps its title and takes the later last visit and the larger visit count. Importing a file again changes nothing. Arch reads the files off the main thread. Once the history store has taken the pages, a sheet reports the favorites added, bookmarks already in favorites, the number of distinct history pages added or updated in committed batches, and files that could not be read. If the user closed the window during the import, the summary shows on the last main window, or as an alert of its own when that window shows a sheet or none is open. The item is disabled while an import runs or the window shows a sheet. The Passwords app handles passwords, as the panel says. File ▸ Import from Browser…, above it, imports from other browsers as `docs/import-browsers.md` designs it, under the same rules. Its sheet on the last main window says it is looking for browsers while a background queue finds the browsers by their key files through `access(2)`, which tells privacy denials (`EPERM`) from file permissions (`EACCES`), and reads each once: the profiles a Chromium-format `Local State` lists, with each profile's bookmarks, merged from `Bookmarks` and `AccountBookmarks` and split into bar bookmarks and folders named by their paths, its history, and the search shortcuts and default engine in its `Web Data` and `Preferences`; the spaces, pinned tabs and folders, and favorites of a sidebar file by profile folder, with a row for each folder that only the sidebar file names; and a WebKit-format browser's bookmarks and `history_items` database. The sheet has a section per browser with its app icon and a row per profile with something to bring: a checkbox, a destination menu with the current Persona, the other Personas, and a new Persona named after the profile, and a checkbox per item with its count, for favorites, spaces with pinned tabs or folders, bookmark folders, history, and search shortcuts. An item that cannot be read shows why and stays unchecked. The items that bring pinned tabs start checked in sheet order while they add at most 50 pinned tabs, and the sheet says how many load at each launch while more are checked. A browser whose files macOS blocks names its privacy setting, with Open System Settings, after which the sheet reads that browser again when Arch becomes active, and Choose Exported File…, which opens the file import. Without any browser, the sheet says that none was found. Below the sections, a browser whose last used profile has a default engine other than Arch's offers it. Import applies everything in one turn of the run loop: `BrowserSession.addImport(_:to:record:)` for each profile that brings favorites or spaces, `SiteSearchStore.add(_:)` with every profile's shortcuts, `HistoryStore.importEntries` with every profile's history, the engine, and then `imports.json`. Once the history store has taken the pages, a summary sheet with lines shared by the file import reports the Personas created, spaces and pinned tabs added, favorites added and already present, the history pages added or changed, shortcuts added and skipped, the engine, and, once per reason, the parts that could not be read in profiles with checked items. When nothing came in, its title says so and the sheet says everything checked was already in Arch. `BrowserSession.addImport(_:to:record:)` adds what one profile brings, its favorites and its spaces with pinned tabs and folders, to an existing Persona or a new one named after the profile, which gets the imported spaces or else one named Personal. Each pinned tab opens in the default engine as a restored tab does, saved at its imported address and title. A pure `SidebarImport.Plan` first resolves the row against the sidebar and the profile's entry in `SidebarImport.Record`, which keeps in `imports.json` the Arch IDs of what earlier imports brought, by the source's IDs, drops those of items that are gone, and moves a file that does not decode to `imports.unreadable.json` at its first write. A recorded item keeps its source item out wherever the user moved or renamed it; without one, spaces and folders merge by name, and favorites and pinned tabs come once per address in the Persona's favorites or the space's pinned tabs, where a pinned tab's saved page counts as well as its current address. A space or folder is created only when a pinned tab or folder goes into it, so a recorded space the user deleted does not come back empty. A row's default destination is the Persona the record names, else one named like the profile, else the current Persona for the browser's first profile. `SiteSearchStore.add(_:)` adds the shortcuts that Settings would accept, and `SpaceTint.nearest(red:green:blue:)` maps a theme color to the tint of the nearest hue, or gray. `ImportTests` checks the readers on synthetic files and the sheet's model and the summary lines on values; `BrowsingTests` checks the plan, the record, and the site search and tint additions on values and temporary files. The note's Checks with the app have not run from the bundle (I.17). `BrowserSource.nativeMessagingHostFolders` lists the eight folders where native app vendors install host manifests for other browsers, the system folders first (`docs/import-browsers.md`, Native messaging hosts), which the app shell passes to the session for the native app links; the import does not use it.

### Plugins module

`Plugins` holds the parts of the plugin system in `docs/plugins.md` built so far.
