A built-in plugin is a Swift type that conforms to `BuiltInPlugin` and declares a `PluginManifest`: a reverse-DNS `PluginID` (at least two dot-separated labels of ASCII letters, digits, and `-`, at most 128 characters, lowercased so that identifiers that differ only in case name one plugin and one folder), a name, a version, a summary, the permissions it asks for (`tabs`, `tabs.rename`, and `clipboard` so far), the sites it may reach as match patterns, its contributions: commands, menu items, sidebar items, settings, and page scripts, and the browser event kinds it listens for (`events`). A `MatchPattern` is `scheme://host/path` or `<all_urls>` and covers http and https addresses only: the host is `*`, a name, or `*.` and a name for its subdomains too, where `*.` and a single label, such as `*.com`, counts as every website for the confirmation, a pattern covers every port, and each `*` in the path matches any run of characters of the address's path and query. A command can have a `PluginShortcut`, written like `ctrl+opt+r`: the modifiers `ctrl`, `opt`, `shift`, and `cmd`, each at most once, and a key (a printable ASCII character other than `+`, `plus`, `space`, an arrow, or `f1` to `f20`), with `cmd` or `ctrl` unless the key is a function key.

The app passes its built-in plugin types to the session, which creates a `PluginManager` for each Persona when a tab or window of the Persona first needs it and keeps it in the Persona's `PersonaEngines`, with its files in `Plugins/<persona id>/` in the app's Application Support folder. The manager gives built-in plugins everything their manifests ask for and enables those that are on by default, except the plugins the user switched on or off in that Persona, which `plugins.json` records (a file that does not read or decode moves to `plugins.unreadable.json`). Its `PluginContributionIndex` holds the enabled plugins' palette commands, their menu items for each location in one section per plugin, with titles falling back to their commands' (a command with a shortcut and no item in the Plugins menu gets one there, and only the first of its items there has the shortcut; a page context item shows when the click has one of its contexts, where `page` means no link, image, media, selection, or field), their sidebar items, and their page scripts. The manager starts a plugin's code on its first command, panel, page script value, or event: it creates the plugin and activates it with a `PluginContext` scoped to the plugin, its grant, its storage, and its secrets. Uses that arrive during activation wait for it, and a failed activation starts again at the next use. Switching a plugin off drops its contributions and badges and calls its `deactivate()` once its activation ends; switching it on again starts a new instance. An instance's context stops acting when the plugin is switched off, its Persona is deleted, or the activation fails: its storage and secrets only read, its other actions throw `stopped`, and its badges do nothing. Deleting a Persona closes its manager (`close()`): its plugins leave the list, their running instances stop, and storage changes not yet written to the files are dropped; from then on it stages, installs, and stores nothing, and throws `PluginError.closed` for such calls, as for a review whose install waited for Touch ID. The next launch's removal pass calls `PluginManager.removeData(ofPersona:in:)`, which deletes the Keychain items of every plugin whose account starts with `<persona id>/` and the Persona's `Plugins/<persona id>/` folder before any manager of the Persona exists. The invocation's tab carries its title and address only when the grant has `tabs` or its sites cover the page; a page context menu item's link, image, and selected text and a space menu item's space pass as they are. The context throws `permissionDenied` for calls the grant does not cover, reads the plugin's settings (the user's value, or the default for one the user has not changed or whose stored value no longer suits its field), and sets badges on the plugin's own sidebar items. A failed command beeps. `Plugin.panel(for:)` gives a sidebar item's panel each time the item opens it. The session gives each manager its bus (`docs/hooks.md`, Plugins) and its `SessionPluginBrowserHost`: the manager subscribes for its Persona, without agent tabs, and hands each event of a kind an enabled plugin's manifest lists to `Plugin.handle(_:)` as `PluginEvent.browser` in the script view its grant allows (addresses with `tabs` or on the pages its granted sites cover, a download's identifiers only), starting the plugin first, as events arrive and outside any hook chain, never an event its own calls caused, such as its rename's title change, and at most 10 events for one subject and 60 in all within a minute (`BrowserEventRateLimiter` in `Events`, which the hook registry's rate uses too); what a listener throws goes nowhere. The context's `tabs()` and `tab(_:)` give the Persona's open tabs in sidebar order through the host, never agent tabs, each with its kind and redacted for the grant, and `renameTab(_:to:)` needs `tabs.rename`, sets a tab's suggested name for the document it shows when the call arrives, with the plugin as the change's cause, under the rules of `SessionPluginBrowserHost` (Hooks module), returns whether the tab took it, and throws `notGranted` for a tab outside the Persona.

Each plugin's storage keeps JSON values (`PluginValue`) by key in `Plugins/<persona id>/<plugin id>/storage.json`, up to 5 MB counted as the bytes of each key plus the JSON of its value; a write that grows it past the limit throws `quotaExceeded`, and a value JSON cannot hold, such as one nested more than 510 levels deep, throws an encoding error. Changes reach the file atomically 0.5 seconds after the last one, or 5 seconds after the first one that has not reached it while changes keep coming, and at quit. A file that does not read or decode moves to `storage.unreadable.json`, and the plugin starts empty. Setting values live in the same file under keys that start with `arch.setting:`, which the plugin's own storage refuses with `reservedKey` and leaves out. Secrets, secret settings among them, are generic passwords in the login keychain with the service `xyz.taeyoon.arch.plugin.<plugin id>` and the account `<persona id>/<key>`, up to 64 for a plugin in a Persona and 16 KB each; a longer secret or a new key past the limit throws `quotaExceeded`. Reading and writing a secret runs one call at a time on a queue of its own off the main thread, as for AI keys, since macOS may ask for the login keychain password first; the bridge replies to `arch.secrets` calls once they are done.

The built-in Page Address plugin adds Copy Page Address, which copies a tab's address, with ⇧⌘C, in the Plugins menu, in the page context menu on the page itself, and as Copy Address in a tab row's context menu for that row's tab, and a setting that copies a Markdown link of the title and address instead.

A built-in plugin's page scripts (`PluginPageScript`) carry their JavaScript, their match patterns, when they run, and whether they run in every frame. The index gives the engines the scripts of the enabled plugins, each plugin's in its world `ARCHPlugin.<plugin id>`. Each script's function first checks the document's `location` against the script's patterns and the plugin's granted sites with a JavaScript copy of `MatchPattern.matches(_:)`, which calls no built-in function and reads only own properties, so built-ins a page replaced cannot change its answer. Then it runs the script as the body of a function with the world's channel as `arch`. A script whose source does not parse as a function body on its own, which JavaScriptCore checks through the `Function` constructor without running it, is left out, so no source can close that function and run outside the check. `PluginManager.receive(_:from:)` passes a value a page script sent to `Plugin.receive(_:)`, starting the plugin first if it has not started, while the plugin is enabled and its granted sites cover the sending frame's document, with the tab redacted for the grant, and returns the reply as JSON. A page script can also list `excludes`, patterns it skips although its `matches` cover them, which the guard and `pageScriptsCover(_:of:)` apply.

Script plugins are packages with `plugin.json` (`docs/plugins.md`, Keys the script runtime reads). `PluginManager.stage(_:)` copies a folder, or extracts a `.zip` archive with `ditto`, or the `.zip` archive after the header of a `.crx` file of format version 3, whose public key it writes into the `manifest.json`, over a `key` the manifest has, as Chromium does, once the package has passed the check that refuses symbolic links, so that the write never follows a link out of it (`CRXArchive`), into `Plugins/<persona id>/Staging/<UUID>/` off the main thread and reads it as a `PluginPackage`: its manifest, its background script's path, its page scripts from their files, and its files. It refuses, with `PluginError.invalidPackage` and a sentence that names the field, a package without `plugin.json` at its top or in an archive's only folder, invalid JSON, a key the runtime does not read, a permission `PluginPermission` lacks, an event kind the bus does not have, commands or events without a background script, menu or sidebar items that name unlisted commands, repeated identifiers, settings whose defaults do not suit them, paths that leave the package, page scripts that are not function bodies on their own, symbolic links, a built-in plugin's identifier, an identifier under `xyz.taeyoon.arch.` other than `xyz.taeyoon.arch.generated.`, which Arch keeps for its built-in plugins, an identifier that starts with `local.arch.browser.` or `local.arch.generated.`, which `PluginIdentifierMigration` renames at launch, and `plugins.json`, `plugins.unreadable.json`, or a runtime's file such as `webExtension.json` as an identifier, in any letter case, whose folder would take that file's name; a newer `apiVersion` throws `unsupportedAPIVersion`. A `.crx` file stages only when its signatures hold, as Chromium's verifier requires (`docs/web-store.md`, Package proof): every proof in its header holds, RSA through Security and P-256 through CryptoKit over one SHA-256 digest of what they sign, and one is by the key whose digest begins with the identifier in the signed header, which becomes the manifest's `key`; otherwise staging refuses it with "The signature of “…” does not match its contents." or "… has no key for its extension's identifier." A header that holds the signature of a `.zip` archive's end record (`PK` 05 06, 06 07, or 06 06), which Chromium's verifier refuses before it reads the header, is refused with "The header of … does not read.", and a file whose package is not the web extension of its identifier, such as a script plugin with `plugin.json`, with "… holds no web extension.", since the signatures prove no other identifier. Its package's `proof` is `webStore` when a proof is by the Web Store's publisher key, whose SHA-256 digest `CRXArchive.webStorePublisherKeyDigest` copies from `kPublisherKeyHash` in Chromium 156.0.8078.48, else `developer`; folders, `.zip` archives, and packages that AI writes have none. `stage(_:origin:)` stages a downloaded `.crx` file with its origin and, for `PluginOrigin.webStore(<identifier>)`, refuses a package without the store's proof, one for another identifier, and a theme. `install(_:grant:isEnabled:)` moves the package to `<plugin id>/package/`, limits the grant to what the manifest asks for, and records the install (its identifier, origin, proof, grant, and date) after the choices in `plugins.json`, where a proven `.crx` install names its file with `signedSource` and a store install its identifier with `webStore`, each with `proof`, in place of `source`, so that builds before package proofs keep such entries as entries they cannot read; an entry with `source` has no proof, and a proof value this build does not know counts as none and stays in the file. `InstalledPlugin.predatesProof` marks an entry that names a `.crx` file with `source`, for the native app gate's sentence that asks for a reinstall; the gate reaches the manager from the app as `nativeAppGate` (`PluginNativeAppGate`; see the Extensions module). `InstalledPlugin.proof` holds the entry's proof, and the review warns "The installed version is signed by its developer; this package is not." when an unsigned package updates a plugin with a proof. In `plugins.json`, an install entry this build cannot read stays as it is; installing an installed identifier again replaces the package, keeps the plugin's place, storage, and secrets, and stops its running instance. An install that `plugins.json` cannot record, as on a full disk, throws and undoes itself: the staged package goes back to `Staging/`, the package it replaced, kept as `<plugin id>/package.replaced` until the file records the install, back to `package/`, and the manager's lists back to how they were, so that the review can install again or discard the package. The other changes to the file, switches, joins, separations, dismissed notes, removals, and the engines' answers, keep their effect for the run when its write fails: `listWriteFailure` says why, Settings ▸ Plugins shows it with Try Again, and the next write that succeeds saves them all (`docs/plugins.md`, Storage). `discard(_:)` deletes a staged package. `remove(_:)` stops the plugin and deletes its folder and its Keychain items in the Persona, whose accounts start with `<persona id>/`. At launch the manager lists the installed plugins after the built-in ones, each read from its package, and clears `Staging/` (`stagingDirectory`), where the Web Store's downloads also wait.

A script plugin's instance (`ScriptPlugin`) loads its background script into a hidden `WKWebView` of its own: a `PluginSchemeHandler` serves the package at `arch-plugin://<plugin id>/`, with a document at the root that runs the background script, every response carries the content security policy `default-src 'self'`, a compiled content rule list blocks every other load, Lockdown Mode takes away WebRTC, which neither covers, and the data store is non-persistent. A user script defines `arch` in the page's world, and `ScriptPluginBridge`, the web view's message handler with replies, performs its calls through the instance's context: storage, secrets, settings, badges, copying, and `arch.tabs.list()`, `get(id)` (null for a tab the plugin does not reach), and `rename(id, name)`. Commands, page script values, and events reach the listeners that the background script set (`arch.commands.onRun`, `arch.pages.onMessage`, and `arch.events.on(kind, listener)`, whose listeners for a kind run one after another, each event's JSON with ISO 8601 dates) through `callAsyncJavaScript`; the delivery function reports an event's missing listener and what a listener threw as JSON, which `ScriptPlugin.handle(_:)` throws as `PluginError.noListener` and `listenerFailed`, so nothing reads WebKit's exception details. `ScriptPlugin`, `ScriptPluginLimits`, `ScriptPlugin.syntaxError(in:)`, which the composer uses too, and `PluginPackage.init(manifest:background:source:rootURL:)` are public for hook scripts, whose package the scheme handler serves from memory. Its start, a command, or a page script value left unanswered for 10 seconds, or the end of its web content process, fails the instance: it releases its web view and fails the waiting calls, and the next use starts a new instance; at the third failure within five minutes the manager switches the plugin off with the reason, which Settings shows for the run. An instance without calls for 30 seconds stops the same way, unless its owner holds it (`isHeld`, which the hook runner sets during a run), and badges outlive it.

`PluginComposer` writes script plugins through the AI interface as the `pluginComposer` feature (Plugin Writer), which the user starts and which sends text only and needs a model with JSON output (`docs/ai.md`, Features that AI adds at run time). It sends the user's description with Arch's plugin reference (`PluginComposerInstructions.swift`) and takes a JSON reply of the manifest and the files. It refuses `sites`, `contributes.pageScripts`, files other than the background script, which alone runs, file names that are not plain `.js` names, files that do not parse as classic scripts (`JSCheckScriptSyntax`), and more than 256 KB of files, writes `plugin.json` with the current API version and the identifier `xyz.taeyoon.arch.generated.<UUID>`, which every version from one composer keeps, and stages the files with `stage(_:writtenBy:)`, which writes them into `Staging/<UUID>/` and reads them as it reads any package. A refused reply goes back to the model with the problem at most twice, a reply is requested again if it is not JSON, and one over 256 KB or one that the output limit of 8,192 tokens cut (`AIError.replyTooLong`) ends the request at once; then the composer throws `PluginComposerError.unusableReply`. A change sends the request, the last version's reply, and the change. The package's origin is `PluginOrigin.generated` with the requests and the model's name, which `plugins.json` records as `generated` in place of `source`.

Every installed plugin has a `PluginKind`: the manager runs script plugins itself, and the app gives it a `PluginRuntime` for each other kind, so far web extensions (`Extensions` module). Staging tells a package's kind by the manifest file at its top level or in an archive's only folder, `plugin.json` or a runtime's such as `manifest.json`, and reads the package through that runtime; an install with the identifier of an installed plugin of another kind is refused. `plugins.json` and the packages in `<plugin id>/package/` keep every kind alike, so the installed list does not depend on an engine. The manager gives each runtime a file of its own in the Persona's folder, `<kind>.json` (`PluginRuntime.runtimeFile`), tells it the enabled plugins of its kind each time they change, makes their instances through it, has it delete what it keeps of a removed plugin, and switches a plugin off with the reasons the runtime reports once the plugin has failed to start on every engine it ran on, which Settings shows for the run. A runtime that cannot run on this Mac gives its plugins an `unavailability`, which keeps their contributions out and appears in Settings. `PluginPermission.nativeMessaging` is for web extensions only, and a `plugin.json` that asks for it is refused; `PluginManifest.extensionPermissions` holds a web extension's API permissions that no Arch permission stands for, which come with its install.

Site access modes, page script styles, the `settingChanged` event, the tab members of `PluginContext` other than `tabs()`, `tab(_:)`, and `renameTab(_:to:)`, its page, history, download, network, and notification members and its `setEnabled(_:command:)` and `showPanel(_:)`, `PluginInvocation.source`, commands that take an argument, settings pages, script plugin panels, panels in the sidebar's place, revoking grants, Install in Another Persona, the `ai` permission, plugin hooks, and changing an installed plugin with AI are not built yet.

### Extensions module

`Extensions` runs Manifest V3 web extensions as plugins of the `webExtension` kind through `WebExtensionRuntime`, one per Persona. It reads `manifest.json` into a `PluginManifest` without any engine: the name, version, and description, a whole `__MSG_<name>__` text taken from `_locales/<locale>/messages.json` in the user's preferred languages and then the default locale; `tabs`, `clipboardWrite`, and `nativeMessaging` as Arch's permissions and the other API permissions as `extensionPermissions`; as sites, the patterns of `host_permissions` and of the content scripts' `matches` that Arch's match patterns hold (web pages on every port); and, for an `action`, an `action` command titled with its default title or the extension's name, which the palette lists. It refuses invalid JSON, a `manifest_version` other than 3, a missing name or version, a `key` that is not Base64, a message that the default locale lacks, and `permissions` or `optional_permissions` that are not lists of names and objects with one key, which Chromium refuses too; such an object counts by its key, as a permission with arguments. The extension's identifier is Chromium's: the first 128 bits of the SHA-256 digest of the public key in `key`, or else of the path the user installs it from, each hexadecimal digit as a letter from `a` to `p`, so installing from the same place again updates it; its plugin identifier is `extension.<identifier>`. The engines that run extensions, WebKit from macOS 15.4 and Chromium, are the host's `extensionRuntimeVersions`, less an engine whose application or the Persona's profile does not start, which never lends another engine's profile, and web extensions are unavailable only while there is none ("Web extensions need macOS 15.4 or later." below it). Each of them checks a package at staging without running it (`EngineExtensions.check(_:)`, which on WebKit reads it with `WKWebExtension(resourceBaseURL:)` and on Chromium reads its files), as well as the installed plugin it would update where an engine has no current answer; a package that every engine refuses is refused with the first engine's reason, such as "WebKit cannot run this extension: …". `plugins.json` keeps each answer in the install entry's `engineChecks` with the version of the engine's extension runtime, WebKit's framework version or the CEF version, so an entry without answers, as earlier builds wrote it, or with one from another version, as after a macOS update, is checked again in those engines, on or off, once the engines run for an enabled extension or a staging, and before its next load, and the names of engines this build does not know stay in the file. An enabled extension that no engine runs any more is switched off with the first engine's reason, and switching it on again checks it again. The runtime loads each enabled extension into the Persona's profiles only in the engines whose current answer runs it (`InstalledPlugin.engines`), with the API permissions its grant covers, those that come with the install, and its granted sites, every web page as `<all_urls>`, which extensions check for with `permissions.contains`. Each change to the engines waits for the previous change and applies per engine: an extension whose install changed loads again, while a recorded answer leaves a running copy alone; one switched off or removed unloads, and a removal also deletes its storage in the engines, except where a counterpart with the same identifier keeps it: an engine that does not run for the Persona then, as when its application or the Persona's profile did not start, or every engine while none runs extensions, gets an entry with the engine identifier in the runtime's file, `webExtension.json` in the Persona's folder, and the first pass after that engine attaches in a later launch, which a waiting entry for an engine the host lists makes run even with no extension enabled, deletes the storage there before it loads anything and drops the entry (`docs/plugins.md`, Storage); an extension that an engine cannot start leaves that engine, which keeps the reason for the run (`engineFailures`) until the plugin is switched on again, and is switched off with each engine's reason only once it has failed on every engine it ran on. Each load passes the install's date, and once a pass's loads have ended, each engine may stop what it runs from an earlier launch and the app did not load (`stopUnloadedExtensions()`). No pass runs while no extension is enabled and no removal waits for an engine, so a profile that starts then stops them at once: the session reports each of the Persona's profiles as it starts and, when it makes the runtime, those that started before (`profileDidStart(with:of:)`), which also makes the runtime the profile's delegate before any extension attaches the engines, so that the store's own install button reaches it in a Persona without extensions. Its plugin instance runs the action on the invocation's tab and fails without a tab whose engine runs the extension. The runtime passes the windows that Browsing reports to the engines, lists the enabled extensions' actions for a tab, a pair under its version installed first and an extension without an action while the tab's engine keeps it from the tab's site, and answers the engines: popups go to the host; access that an extension requests while it runs asks, as a `WebExtensionQuestion` without any interface that the host shows as a card over the tab, in the install sheet's sentences, which for a version of a pair names the pair and the asking version, never grants sites other than web pages or `nativeMessaging`, and needs Touch ID or the login password for every website, by the rule the install sheet also follows (`PluginGrant.confirmationReason(for:)`); native messages and ports need the extension's `nativeMessaging` grant from its install. `NativeMessaging` finds an app through its host manifest in Chromium's format, `<name>.json` in `NativeMessagingHosts/`, whose `name` matches, whose `type` is `stdio`, whose `path` is absolute, and whose `allowed_origins` lists `chrome-extension://<identifier>/`; a name is dot-separated runs of lowercase letters, digits, and `_`. It starts the app with that origin as its argument, writes without SIGPIPE, and exchanges messages as a 32-bit length in the Mac's byte order followed by UTF-8 JSON, up to 1 MB from the app: a single message ends the app after its first reply, and a port lasts until either side ends it. `NativeMessagingHostLinks`, one for the app (`BrowserSession.nativeMessagingHosts`), links into that folder the host manifests that native app vendors install only for other browsers, and keeps the proof gate on them (`docs/password-managers.md`, Host discovery and Proof gate). A pass reads the folders that the app shell passes (`BrowserSource.nativeMessagingHostFolders`) in order, off the main thread and without changing them, keeps for each name the first manifest of at most 64 KB whose `name` is its file's and follows the naming rule, whose `type` is `stdio`, whose `path` names an executable file by its absolute path, and whose `allowed_origins` read, and lists the folders that a permission error keeps from it (`unreadableFolders`). In Arch's folder it links each winner's name to its manifest unless the name is the user's, a regular file or a link outside the listed folders, replaces its own link when the winner moves, and removes its own links whose names have no winner, each new link renamed into place. A host whose native app macOS does not start for Arch is held and gets no link: for `com.apple.passwordmanager`, the pass asks `HelperLaunchConstraints` at most once per run, which reads the helper's parent launch constraints from `codesign -d -vvvv` (the kinds on its standard error, their trees on its standard output), matches their pairs of signing identifier and team and their entitlement condition against Arch's signature from `SecCodeCopySigningInformation`, keeps the team only in memory, and holds nothing when they do not read. `update()` runs a pass once per run and `update(force: true)` every time: each runtime asks before its first load of an enabled extension whose grant has `nativeMessaging` and again after an install or update of one in the run, staging before it checks a web extension that asks for it, and Settings ▸ Plugins as it first shows in the run the section of an extension whose grant has it, on or off (`NativeAppGuide`, below). Before a pass creates a link or changes one, the gate counts the links it will make, and the session has every Persona's runtime take the versions those links gate out of Chromium and stop what Chromium runs that the runtime has not loaded, as a kept copy of an extension that the list lacks, waiting for a load in Chromium under way and then until Chromium has switched them off (`takeGatedVersionsOutOfChromium()`, through `gateWidens`, and `EngineExtensions.waitForUnloads()`); after the change, the pass asks again when the manifests behind Arch's links or in Chromium's system folder refuse a caller that the gate did not count, as when a vendor adds an origin behind a link that stays, and removing a link asks nothing. The gate covers the names under Arch's own links (`linkedHosts`, read from Arch's folder and the manifests behind its links until the run's first pass, a manifest that does not read counting as allowing every caller until a pass reads it) and every manifest in the system folder that Chromium reads itself for a name that Arch's folder lacks (`ChromiumEngine.systemNativeMessagingHosts`, which the session passes as `chromiumFolder`), whether a pass finds it valid or not, except under a name where the user's file in Arch's folder is what Chromium reads: a version whose grant has `nativeMessaging`, whose identifier no `.crx` signature proved, and which such a manifest allows, gets "Arch cannot prove that this extension is the one that 1Password BrowserSupport expects. Install it from the Web Store or from its signed .crx file.", with the manifest's `description`, or, for an install entry that names a `.crx` file with `source` (`InstalledPlugin.predatesProof`), the sentence that asks for a reinstall. On WebKit, `NativeMessaging` refuses its messages and ports to a name under one of Arch's links at the time, or one that a pass is about to make, with the sentence, by the manifest it reads for them, so that a vendor's change during the run counts at once. On Chromium, which reads the links and its system folder itself, the load pass does not load it, unloads it where it runs, and stops a copy that Chromium kept; a Chromium profile's start unloads it before any pass of loads (`profileDidStart(with:of:)`); and the manager keeps the sentence for the run as its failure on Chromium, so it keeps its other engines and goes off when it ran on Chromium alone. The review shows the sentence as the package's Chromium line (`PluginManager.engineNotes(of:)`), and a version without proof cannot join a proven counterpart under an identifier that the gate covers, at staging, in Install \<Engine> Version…, or with Join With… ("Arch can prove the identifier of only one of the two, and a native app on this Mac accepts the other's, so they cannot be one plugin."), while each version of a pair is gated on its own. Two web extension plugins of a Persona can be versions of one extension, a pair (`PluginEngineVersions`, `engineVersions` in `plugins.json` as objects from engine name to plugin identifier, which keep the records and engine names the build ignores): each version runs only on the engine its record gives it, the runtime loads one extension per engine identifier in each engine, and each engine's calls reach that engine's version. At staging, a package matches an installed plugin outside a pair that has its identifier, or, while none has it, the first installed with the same name and a matching `author` or `homepage_url` and none that differs; the engines check the match and the plugins the package updates where they have no current answer, and the package can join the match for each engine that runs it while the match runs on the other. A version that joins a plugin with the same identifier gets the entry identifier `extension.<identifier>.webkit` or `.chromium`, which no package may have as its own. The engines know it by its package's identifier, so on its engine it takes over the data of the plugin's copy, which the review warns of when the package comes from another place and the plugin's package runs there, and a version whose record a build before engine versions dropped pairs again from its identifier. An update with an installed identifier goes to the version from the same source, else the one whose answers match, else the one the review chooses, keeps the pair and the dismissed notes, and cannot leave a version of a pair on no engine; a new plugin takes nothing from an entry or a record with its identifier that the list leaves out, as a removal whose write failed leaves them. Join With…, offered only while both engines run extensions, pairs two plugins whose current answers run each on its engine, starting with the one installed first on WebKit, and takes the switch of the one installed first; Separate Versions, refused for versions that share an identifier, and installing a package that could have joined as a separate plugin dismiss each plugin's note for the other's engines. The pair switches together, while a version that fails switches off alone; removing one version leaves the other in the pair's place with its grant, storage, and switch, and deletes the removed version's engine storage except in the engine its counterpart with the same identifier keeps. A pair contributes once, through its version installed first while it is on, which also brings the commands of the other version that its own lacks while that one is on (`InstalledPlugin.commands(among:)`, which `PluginManager.run(_:_:)` also accepts), so the palette lists an action that only the version installed second has under the first, and running it runs the action of the version for the tab's engine. An unpaired plugin offers a version for each engine that runs extensions while it runs on another, and asks for one where an engine with a current answer does not run it, a failure to start counting, until the user dismisses the note for that engine (`dismissedVersionNotes`); before Chromium runs extensions, nothing asks and Join With… does not show. Before the engines check or load an extension installed from a file without a `key`, the runtime writes into the installed copy's manifest a key whose bytes are the source's path, once per run; Chromium names an extension by the digest of its key, and otherwise by the folder it loads from, which is Arch's copy, so it then knows the extension by Arch's identifier, as WebKit does. A `.crx` file stages with the key of its header (Plugins module), and Chromium's package types for Install Chromium Version… add `.crx` files. The tabs and windows that extensions ask for open through the host in the asking engine. Allow on This Site reaches the engine that runs the tab's version. A question in the engine's own sentences, Chromium's for `permissions.request()`, asks with a question that names the pair and the asking version and says that the engine keeps the access until the extension is removed, though access to websites can end sooner, as after an update that leaves some of the extension's websites out (`docs/plugins.md`, Requests at run time), needs Touch ID or the login password when the manifest's optional sites, or the sites it asks for that the install left out, which Chromium withholds and lets `permissions.request()` ask for again, include every website, and is refused without asking for an extension whose manifest lists `nativeMessaging` among its optional permissions without the install's grant, or whose optional lists do not read; a reload asks with Reload and Cancel; a store install is refused, so that Chromium installs nothing outside the plugin list, and one from a tab on a store page (`WebStore.extensionID(in:)`) whose identifier reads as one starts Arch's store install of it through the host in the Persona with the tab (`WebExtensionHost.installFromWebStore(_:persona:tab:)`). `commands`, an options entry in Settings, and run-time grants that last across launches on WebKit are not built yet.

`WebStore`, `WebStoreClient`, and `WebStoreInstaller` install extensions from the Web Store as `docs/web-store.md` designs it, started from Settings ▸ Plugins, a store page in a tab, and the palette (Preferences module; Share and site settings; Command palette and site search). `WebStore.swift` is the only file with the store's addresses: the update endpoint, `https://clients2.google.com/service/update2/crx`, and the store's two page hosts, the current one and an older one whose pages redirect there (named in `docs/web-store.md`, as `AGENTS.md` allows); `extensionID(in:)` reads a store page's address on either host, and `extensionID(from:)` reads a bare identifier, 32 letters from `a` to `p` in any case with spaces at either end, or a store page's address with or without `https://`. `WebStoreClient` asks the endpoint about at most 25 identifiers a request, one request after another, with `response=updatecheck`, `os=mac`, `arch`, `prodversion`, the Chromium version in the CEF version of Arch's engine (`chromiumVersion(inCEFVersion:)`), `acceptformat=crx3`, and for each identifier an `x` of `id`, `v` (the installed version, or `0.0.0.0`), `installsource=ondemand`, and `uc`, through an ephemeral session without cookies, cache, or stored credentials and with an empty `Accept-Language`. It reads the update protocol's XML with `XMLParser` into a release, `noupdate`, an unknown identifier, or another status, and downloads a package only over HTTPS, following redirects only to HTTPS addresses, stopping past 256 MB, and keeping it only when its size and SHA-256 digest match the answer's. `WebStoreInstaller.stage(_:in:)` asks for the identifier's current release, refuses with "“1Password” 8.12.44.33 is installed, the Web Store's current version." without downloading when the Persona has that version installed from the store, downloads into `PluginManager.stagingDirectory`, stages the file with `stage(_:origin:)` and the origin `webStore(<identifier>)`, which checks the store's publisher proof and the identifier, and deletes the download in every case; cancelling its task throws `CancellationError` at any step, stopping the request or the download and discarding a package that staging finished meanwhile, and for a Persona that the user deleted (`PluginManager.isClosed`) it throws `PluginError.closed` without asking or downloading. Its failures are `WebStoreError`s with the install flow's messages. `WebStoreInstaller.installedPlugin(of:among:)` finds a Persona's plugin of an extension identifier, from the store or not, which a store page's popover row names. The session owns one installer (`BrowserSession.webStore`), whose client takes the Chromium version from the CEF version that the app shell reads from the Chromium engine, or has none without that engine, so that every request fails with a message. Chromium's store button on a store page starts the same install (Extensions module). The installer is an `ObservableObject` that also checks for updates (`docs/web-store.md`, Updates): `checkForUpdates(in:onlyOncePerRun:)` asks the store about the Persona's installs with the origin `webStore`, each under its origin's identifier with its installed version, one query per identifier with the older version where two versions share it, and nothing for a Persona without one; it keeps the versions newer by Chromium's version order (`version(_:isNewerThan:)`: one to four dot-separated numbers, compared as numbers, a missing number as 0) in `updates`, by Persona and plugin, and the check in `updateChecks` (`WebStoreUpdateCheck`: checking, checked at a date, or failed with the message), keyed by `PluginManager.persona`. With `onlyOncePerRun` it skips a Persona already checked in the run, and it never starts a second check for a Persona under way; a failure keeps the versions found before, a cancelled check leaves the Persona's last check as it was, and a check whose manager closed records nothing and throws `PluginError.closed`. `update(of:in:)` gives a plugin's newer version while it is installed at an older one, and `stageUpdate(of:in:)` downloads that version without asking the store again and stages it as an update of that plugin, or throws the installed message without one. The session's `deletePersona` calls `forget(_:)`, which drops the Persona's results. No timer or launch check runs.

`NativeAppGuide` gives the native app lines of a web extension's section in Settings ▸ Plugins while a version's grant has `nativeMessaging` (`docs/password-managers.md`, Status and guidance in Settings ▸ Plugins), naming no Persona and no folder of another browser: "Looking for native apps…" until a pass ends in the run, which `look(for:in:)` starts for the section, on or off; then, by host name, each manifest that allows a version's identifier, from Arch's links (`linkedHosts`), the user's files in Arch's folder (`userHosts`, which passes read by the discovery's rule), and the discovered hosts, as "\<description>, set up for Arch", "\<description>, set up for another browser" for a discovered one whose name Arch's folder gives to a manifest that does not allow it, or, for a held host, "\<description>, which macOS does not start for Arch on this Mac"; without one, "No native app on this Mac accepts this extension.", with "macOS kept Arch from reading another browser's native app setup." when a folder did not read. While an app accepts it, the lines add the conditions that `ArchInstallation` finds unmet, a bundle that is not directly in `/Applications` or is a link ("Arch runs from \<folder>; …", the folder after links) and a signature without a team ("Arch is not signed by a developer team; …"), read once per run from `SecCodeCopySigningInformation` without keeping the team; the user's step for `com.1password.1password` and `com.apple.passwordmanager`, the latter with "Arch is among them on this Mac." or "Arch is not among them on this Mac." from `NativeMessagingHostLinks.launchConstraintAnswers`, by the helper's path, which passes also fill for a helper that a user's manifest names, and neither for constraints that do not read; and last what the plugin's latest message or port met on WebKit tabs in the run for its current install (`NativeAppOutcomes`, which `WebExtensionRuntime.nativeAppOutcomes` gives): the app answered, ended without answering, was not started because its host is held, which the extension also gets as "Arch did not start \<description>, which macOS does not start for Arch.", or the failure message, such as the gate's sentence. A port that the extension ends before the app answers records nothing.

### AI module

`AI` is the provider-neutral AI interface designed in `docs/ai.md`. `AIClient.stream` takes a typed `AIRequest` (text, page, selection, file, download, tool call, and tool result parts) from a registered feature and streams `waiting`, `started`, `text`, `toolCall`, `usage`, and `finished` events; `reply` collects them, `run` performs tool rounds, and canceling the reading task or dropping the stream cancels the request. `AIService.shared` resolves the feature's route (a primary model and up to two fallbacks; features the user starts fall back to the default route, automatic ones never do), checks the daily automatic-request and monthly spending limits, checks every part against the feature's declared data kinds and the excluded sites (`file:` and `local-network` by default, the latter with the unspecified addresses `0.0.0.0` and `::`; internal pages always; hosts compare without the root's trailing dot, so `localhost.` is the local network), strips queries, fragments, and user names and passwords, cuts page text to the scope and the model's input limit, waits in the provider's rate limiter (concurrent slots with user requests first, an optional per-minute bucket, and a cool-down after a 429, at most a day from `Retry-After`, set before the freed slot goes to a waiting request, which requests answered 429 together start once; an automatic request gives up after 15 seconds), checks the excluded sites and the spending limit again once it has its slot and key, records an attempt's usage before it frees the slot, and moves to the next model only when a model fails before any text reached the caller. HTTP providers speak the `chatCompletions` or `messages` HTTP format against a configurable base URL (https, or http on a loopback host) through an ephemeral `URLSession` that refuses redirects to other hosts, with a 60-second first-byte and a 30-second chunk timeout. A `chatCompletions` request limits the reply with `max_tokens`; a 400 that refuses it (its error's `param` names `max_tokens`, or its message asks for `max_completion_tokens`) sends the request once more with `max_completion_tokens`, which later requests to that model send at once until quit. That field also counts a reasoning model's thinking, so a reply that reaches its limit before any text or tool call ends with `replyTooLong` rather than as an empty answer, and its tokens count as an error's. Keys live in the login keychain (service `xyz.taeyoon.arch.ai`), are read once per launch, and never reach `ai.json` or an error message; every Keychain call runs one at a time on a queue of its own off the main thread, a key the Keychain does not give makes the provider unavailable with the Keychain's reason, a saved key goes only to the base URL it was saved for, so a request that waited while the user moved its provider to another base URL ends with the provider unavailable rather than sending that server's key to the old one, and Refresh and Check with an edited base URL use only a typed key, and removing a provider removes its key first. `ai.json` holds providers, routes, exclusions, and limits, and fields a hand-written file lacks take their defaults where they have one; `ai-usage.json` keeps 13 months of per-feature and per-model totals with costs estimated from user-entered prices under their currency codes, which the spending limit compares with its own trimmed and in uppercase (a file that does not decode is renamed to `ai-usage.json.unreadable`), and the last 50 requests stay in memory. `AIPageSource` is the protocol through which features read open tabs' pages. `AISettingsStore.allowsAI(on:)` and `setAllowsAI(_:on:)` back the site switch: allowing removes every exclusion that matches the address, and excluding adds its host, or `file:` for a local file. `title(of:)` and `estimatedCost(of:on:)` describe the model under an answer. Command-line providers run a tool the user installed and signed in to in the built-in terminal. Each request starts the tool with `posix_spawn` in its own process group, with pipes instead of a terminal, a new empty folder under `TMPDIR` as its working directory (removed once the tool has ended), and only `HOME`, `USER`, `LOGNAME`, `SHELL`, `PATH`, `LANG`, `TMPDIR`, `TERM=dumb`, and the configured variables; `PATH` is the executable's folder followed by the login shell's PATH, which `$SHELL -l -i -c` prints once per launch and again after `LoginShell.reload()`. The shell prints its exported environment between markers. Arch keeps the tool search path and the configuration paths used by Claude Code, Codex, and the GitHub CLI. Only live folders pass absolute `GH_CONFIG_DIR` and `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` values to `gh`. The prompt goes to standard input: the instructions (with the schema when a JSON reply is asked for), then each message labeled `User:` or `Assistant:` with its parts as delimited blocks; tools get only text files. `{model}` in the arguments becomes the model's name, and an argument that contains it is left out for the tool's own default model, which has an empty name. Standard output is read as plain text a line at a time or as JSON lines whose dotted paths give text deltas, a final text, token counts, and an error, split into lines before UTF-8 decoding. A nonzero exit reports the last line of standard error, an exit without a reply is a malformed reply, output must start within 120 seconds and continue within 60 and ends a second after the tool exits even if a background process it started keeps standard output open, and cancellation sends SIGTERM to the process group and SIGKILL two seconds later. Requests with page, selection, file, or download parts skip command-line tools that the user has not marked as running without their own tools, and throw `blockedByPolicy(.commandLineToolWithPageData)` when only that rule left no model. `AIService.hooks`, an `AIHookCenter`, runs hooks as steps of the shared hook scheduler it owns, `AIHookCenter.scheduler` (`docs/hooks.md`, The shared scheduler), which the hook registry shares and plugin hooks are to share. `AIHookScheduler.post(_:kind:subject:event:document:urls:)` adds an owner's `AIHookStep`s to the chain of a kind and subject; steps of one event join one chain in ascending `order`, ties in posting order, and run one at a time, each after its delay from the event's first post and its owner's `mayRun` check; a post with another event replaces the chain, whose waiting steps skip and whose running step is canceled, and starts once that step has stopped, never past its timeout. `cancel(subject:except:)` keeps a close's own chain, `cancel(subject:otherThan:)` ends the subject's chains for other documents, ignoring the fragment, and keeps chains without one, `cancel(owner:)` ends an owner's steps, and quitting cancels every chain. Each step ends exactly once, as finished, failed, timed out, stopped, or skipped with its reason (`AIHookStepOutcome`), and its owner hears of it before the next step starts. A step with an AI feature skips while its owner is paused, when an address of its event is excluded from AI (at once, so a post left without steps changes no chain), and unless its feature is a registered automatic feature that is on with a usable model of its own and within the daily limit for automatic requests and the monthly spending limit; a `perRequest` feature, such as an agent hook's, has a usable model only through the step's route (`AIHookStep.route`), which the check passes to `AIService.isAvailable(_:route:)`. Three consecutive `authentication` or `providerUnavailable` request errors that a step reports pause its owner until `resume(owner:)` or a change to one of the routes its steps name, which also cancels its running steps. The center posts its hooks as steps under their features with `post(_:)` or, under a browser event's identifier so other owners' steps for that event join its chain, `post(_:event:)`: features subscribe to `pageLoaded`, `tabOpened`, and `downloadFinished` events with a priority, a delay, a timeout (30 seconds by default), and the kinds of action they may return; the hooks for one event run by priority and then feature ID, each seeing the actions applied before it, and a hook's `stop` skips the center's later hooks only. Ending a subscription ends its feature's steps, and the center drops the actions of a hook that did not finish. Its requests go out under its own feature whatever feature they name. The center keeps each feature's last hook error, a reached budget or `AIHookError.timedOut` among them, which a request that succeeds clears, a hook that sends no request leaves, and a change to the feature's route clears; its `pausedFeatures` and `resume(_:)` read and resume the scheduler's owners, so Resume in a Settings ▸ AI row or a change to the feature's route resumes a feature paused after three failed requests; a hook's own error does not end the count.

Sessions on the user's CLI (`docs/agents.md`, Sessions on the user's CLI) run in `AI`, and `AgentService` starts them for in-app sessions (Agent module). The Claude Code and Codex presets are command-line providers whose `AICommandLineConfiguration.agent` is `.claudeCode` or `.codex` (`AIAgentKind`): Arch builds every argument, the editor keeps only the environment, models, and limits (two concurrent requests), and the environment never carries `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` or `OPENAI_API_KEY`. `AIAgentToolStatus` reads the version from `--version` and the sign-in from `claude auth status --json` (only `loggedIn`) or the exit status of `codex --no-daemon login status`. The probes run no model and are kept per tool version under the executable's resolved path, size, and modification time (`AIAgentProbeStore`), which a `stat` compares before every process start: for Claude Code, `--version` and whether `--help` lists every flag Arch passes; for Codex, `--version`, `features list`, `debug models --bundled`, and the configuration probe (`--strict-config` with every `-c` and `--disable` of a session, then each setting alone, with the server's settings as one group, to name the one refused), all with `CODEX_HOME` set to a new empty folder. The feature derivation disables every listed feature except those at stage `removed`, deprecated ones listed as off, and the keep list (`code_mode_host`), and refuses a list without `shell_tool` or `unified_exec`. `AIModelCapability.agent` is derived (a preset's models and HTTP models with `tools`), and overrides cannot change it. A preset's ordinary requests run with Arch's arguments for them after the probes, with every tool off, so they accept page data; Claude Code's text comes from its text deltas, and an `init` that lists a tool or server ends the request with `blockedByPolicy(.agentUsedOwnTools(_:))`; Codex's comes from completed `agent_message` items, with the code-mode error item skipped and the stream watcher applied. Check on a preset runs the status command and the probes, never a model. `AIService.startAgentSession(_:)` starts an `AIAgentSession` on the chosen model or else the `agent` feature's preset models, or the first preset model when the route has none (`agentSessionCandidates(named:for:)`), in `$TMPDIR/Arch-Agent-<UUID>/` (mode 0700, removed at the end, and `removeLeftoverFolders()` for launch): Claude Code as one process whose standard input stays open (`CommandLineProcess`'s open input mode) with `mcp.json` (mode 0600) holding the ticket, Codex as one process per turn with `--no-daemon` before `exec` and `exec resume <thread>` with `-m` on follow-ups. Before a model's first turn, a signed-out tool fails with `authentication`; a model that fails before `ready` (its first output after the start check: Claude Code's `message_start` after an `init` that passed, or Codex's first item other than an error), as at a usage limit, which maps to `rateLimited` with the tool's reset time as the provider's cool-down, gives way to the route's next, as does a provider in a cool-down longer than 10 seconds, and a Codex thread the failed model began is deleted; after `ready` the conversation stays on its model, with `--fallback-model` never passed. The start check runs on every `init` (tools, the `arch` server connected, permission mode, skills, commands, the reviewed built-in plugins and agent types, memory, telemetry), a later `init` may list any `tools/list` answer the host reported (`toolListDidChange(_:)`), and with a session skill folder (`AIAgentSkills`) the session passes it with `--plugin-dir`, the skill tool, `plugin-authoring` off through `--settings`, and the bundled and policy skills, `doctor`, the extra usage commands, and fast mode off, so `init` may list only the folder's skills and 28 reviewed built-in commands, and `send(_:)` throws `blockedByPolicy(.agentMessageIsCommand)` for a message that starts with `/`, which Claude Code would run as one of those commands; the watcher accepts only Codex's `agent_message`, `reasoning`, `todo_list`, `error`, and `mcp_tool_call` items to `arch`. A refusal ends the process group and the session. Stop sends Claude Code's interrupt and ends the process group 3 seconds later if the turn has not ended, after which the next message starts a new process with the earlier conversation as text, and ends Codex's process group at once. Output must start within 120 seconds and continue within 180, a timer that pauses while the host runs one of the session's calls (`toolCallDidStart()`, `toolCallDidEnd()`). Each turn holds a slot of the provider's rate limiter and counts once under `agent`, an interrupted Claude Code turn with each request's last stream usage and a Codex turn with what it added to the thread's running total; Stop and `end(keepingThread:)` end a turn that still waits for its slot or its probes at once. `end(keepingThread:)` ends the processes, removes the folder, and runs `codex --no-daemon delete --force <thread>` unless told to keep it, and `endProcesses()` ends the process group at once, as at quit. `processDidStart` reports each process right after `posix_spawn`, for the ticket check's start time, and `checkMessage(_:)` throws what `send(_:)` would throw without sending, so a caller that queues messages keeps a refused one. `agentSessionModels()` lists the `agent` route's usable preset models, then every other one, for the composer. `Packages/ARCH/Tests/AITests/Fixtures/Agent` holds the recorded streams the decoders are tested against. One-time runs pass the requested turn limit to Claude Code and treat reaching it as completing the run. Codex starts with `--ephemeral`. Either preset accepts one message, closes its process and removes its private folder after the turn. The normal session path still supports follow-ups.

API-key sessions (`docs/agents.md`, API-key sessions) run on `AIClient.run(_:tools:maxRounds:conversationDidChange:)`. `AIRequest.pinnedModel` serves a request on that one model with no fallback, also one its route does not list, if it still exists and meets the feature's requirements; `run` pins every round after the first to the model that served the first, and a request with tools goes only to HTTP models with `tools`, never to a command-line tool, also one with a Tool calls override. Before each round `AIToolHistory` sends the results of rounds more than eight back as their one-line notes (`AIToolResult.note`, or a line that names the tool) and each image in a result only until a reply follows a request that carried it; while a request is over its model's input limit, the service sends whole results as notes too, oldest round first and never the newest, before it cuts page text. The service sends a tool result that its part checks refuse, for a page or an image on a site excluded from AI, also one excluded after the result arrived, or a kind the feature does not declare, as an error result that says why without naming the site, so the loop goes on without the data; a model without `images`, and any `chatCompletions` model, gets a note for each image in a result. `conversationDidChange` gets the conversation after each round whose calls all ran and after the final reply or the round limit, with no tool call left without its result.

Model tiers and request routes (`docs/hooks.md`, Model choice and tiers) are built, though no caller names a route yet; agent runs (VII.27) are the first. `AIModelTier` (Fast, Standard, Deep) names routes in `ai.json` under `tier.fast`, `tier.standard`, and `tier.deep` (`AIFeatureID.tier(_:)`), each with a model and up to two fallbacks; their `isEnabled` is written true and not read, and a hand-written route without it takes its feature's default. `AIRouteChoice` (`{"tier": "fast"}` or `{"models": [...]}` in JSON) lets `AIRequest.route` and `AIAgentSessionRequest.route` (used when the session names no `model`, which resolves as a route of that one model) name a route; a value with both keys does not decode. `AIFeature.routing` is `stored` by default or `perRequest`: a `perRequest` feature takes the request's route and throws `notConfigured(.noModel)` without one, a stored feature the user starts may use a request's route under its own switch, and a stored automatic feature refuses one with `blockedByPolicy(.routeNotAllowed)` before any request, whatever its switch, with a sentence that blames no provider and no action. An empty tier throws `notConfigured(.emptyTier(_:))`, "Assign a model to the Fast tier in Settings ▸ AI.", whose action is Open AI Settings as for every `notConfigured`, and never falls back to another tier or the default route; models without their provider or a required capability drop after the tier as for any route, and budgets, rate limits, part checks, and usage apply as before, so a `perRequest` automatic feature counts toward both limits, on a CLI session too, whose turns of an automatic feature are each checked against both limits, counted as one automatic request, and given a provider slot behind the user's requests. `AIService.resolvedModels(for:route:)` returns the models a choice reaches, and `isAvailable(_:route:)` says whether it reaches one. `AIRequest.recordsContent` false leaves the Activity entry without content.

### AgentControl module and arch-cli

`AgentControl` holds what both ends of the agents' control channel share (`docs/agents.md`, Local control channel; Tools; arch-cli). `AgentFrame` and `AgentFrameReader` frame messages as a 4-byte big-endian length and one UTF-8 JSON-RPC message of at most 16 MiB, and refuse a larger announced length before its payload arrives. `AgentRPCMessage` reads and writes JSON-RPC 2.0 requests, notifications, results, and errors with the standard error codes, and no batches. `AgentHello` and `AgentHelloAnswer` are `hello` at protocol version 1 and its flat answer: `bound`, `granted` with the level, Persona, and expiry, `none`, or `refused` with a reason code. `AgentSocketLocation` puts the socket at `~/Library/Application Support/Arch/Agent/control.sock`, with the home folder from the account database, or at `xyz.taeyoon.arch/control.sock` in the per-user temporary folder when that path does not fit `sun_path`'s 104 bytes; `forClient()` gives a client outside an app bundle, which fails its check of any listener, the path in `ARCH_AGENT_TEST_SOCKET` when that is set. `AgentPeerCheck` reads the peer's user (`LOCAL_PEERCRED`) and audit token (`LOCAL_PEERTOKEN`) and compares its running code's validity and code directory hash with an expected hash through Security, returning the peer it checked; since the token names the process that last used the peer's end, both ends read it again before each frame and require the same process ID and version, so a peer that hands the socket to another process or `exec`s is refused. `AgentBundleLayout` names the files each end expects (`Contents/MacOS/Arch` and `Contents/Helpers/arch-cli`). `AgentToolCatalog` lists the design's 24 tools with descriptions, classes, traits, and parameters, from which it makes their JSON Schemas and MCP annotations; `AgentLimits` holds the limits. `AgentUntrusted` wraps page text in `<untrusted source="page" url="…">` blocks with the closing tag escaped and the address without query, fragment, or credentials. `AgentExitCode` maps result statuses and client errors to `arch-cli`'s exit codes. `AgentHarness` holds the harness text: static instructions ending with the guide's topics and summaries; the topics `browsing`, `forms`, `research`, `downloads`, `purchases-and-messages`, `troubleshooting`, and `cli`, the last generated from `AgentCommandLine`'s command table and exit codes (`AgentGuideTopic`); `mcpInstructions`; `guideResult(arguments:)`, the `guide` tool's result for the tool host (the guide, a topic, or `error` with the code `invalidArguments`); and skills from the same text (`AgentSkill`: a `SKILL.md` with only `name` and `description` in its front matter, the description as a YAML double-quoted string, and the version line `arch-skill-version: 1` with the SHA-256 digest of the text at the start of its body): `entrySkill(cliPath:)`, the `arch-browser` skill for outside agents, which names the bundle's `arch-cli` by its absolute path and directs the agent to the guide, and `sessionSkills`, one `arch-<topic>` skill with the topic's text for each topic except `cli`, in an in-app session's own skill folder (agent decision 16). `AgentSkillInstaller` resolves the targets `claude` (`${CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.claude}/skills`), `codex` (`$HOME/.agents/skills`), and `codex-home` (`${CODEX_HOME:-$HOME/.codex}/skills`), with `HOME` from the same environment as the tools read it and the account database's home folder when it is unset, and `--path` folders. It refuses folders inside app bundles and variables that are not absolute paths, `~` included, copies the skill, and reports each place as absent, current, outdated (Arch's copy with different text), modified (Arch's copy whose text no longer matches its digest), or foreign. It replaces and removes only Arch's unmodified copies: folders, not links, containing only a `SKILL.md` with the version line and a matching digest and an optional `.DS_Store`. It checks every place before writing any, treats an empty folder as absent, and writes a session's skill folder with `write(_:into:)`. `AgentCommandLine` dispatches by `argv[0]` and parses the commands. `AgentControlConnection` is the client: it connects, checks that the listener runs the `Contents/MacOS/Arch` of the bundle around the client before it sends a byte, waits for Arch's `ready` notification and confirms it came from the checked process, and only then says `hello`, so a program that took the socket's path and handed it off collects no ticket. After `hello`, `call` sends a method and waits for its result, printing nothing itself: `progress` notifications go to a closure, error -32001 (`AgentControlErrorCode.noGrant`) throws `noGrant` and a `revoked` notification `revoked`, both exit 6, and `callTool` sends a `tools/call` (`AgentToolCall`, a name and arguments) and decodes its `AgentToolResult`. `AgentControlMethod` names `tools/list`, `tools/call`, `tools/cancel`, `grant/show`, `grant/release`, `progress`, `tools/changed`, and `revoked`, and `hello`'s refusals gain reason `unidentified`. `AgentPeerCheck.verify(_:)` checks a peer already read, so that the endpoint's checks read and verify in two steps. `AgentToolMessages.swift` holds the control protocol's tool messages: `tools/list`, answered with the names of the connection's tools (`AgentToolList`), `tools/call` with `AgentToolCall` and `AgentToolResult` (the status, the error code or rule, text and image content in MCP's shape, the structured facts, and the approval an `approvalPending` answer waits on), and the params of `tools/cancel` and `progress`; a call's ID is its request's integer ID. `AgentMCPServer` is arch-mcp's MCP core without I/O: it takes the client's lines, Arch's messages, and each connection's outcome, and returns the replies, control messages, connection attempts, log lines, and exit to perform. It answers `initialize` with the client's version among 2024-11-05, 2025-03-26, 2025-06-18, and 2025-11-25, otherwise the newest, with `serverInfo` `arch`, `tools.listChanged`, and `AgentHarness.mcpInstructions`; `ping` at any time; -32601 for any other method, before `initialize` too; and -32600 for `tools/list` and `tools/call` before it. It refuses an unknown tool or a malformed call with -32602, describes the tools Arch names from its own catalog (the whole catalog while no connection exists), maps Arch's results to MCP's (`isError` unless `ok` or `approvalPending`, `structuredContent` with the status, code, and approval from 2025-06-18, annotations from 2025-03-26, and "Waiting for the user in Arch; call approval_wait with this ID: \<id>" before an `approvalPending` answer's content), forwards progress to calls with a progress token and `notifications/cancelled` as `tools/cancel`, and sends `notifications/tools/list_changed` after `tools/changed` and when a new connection follows a list the client already has. `AgentLineReader` splits standard input into lines of at most 16 MiB. `AgentMCPConfiguration` writes the Claude Code command, Codex's `[mcp_servers.arch]` table with `tool_timeout_sec = 120`, and the `mcpServers` JSON, each naming the bundle's `arch-mcp` by its absolute path, and `setup`'s argument arrays. `AgentLinkInstaller` links `arch-cli` and `arch-mcp` into the folder `--dir` names or the first writable PATH folder inside the home folder, `~/.local/bin` first, never uses a folder inside an app bundle, so a bundle's own `Contents/Helpers` on PATH keeps its `arch-mcp` link, checks both places before writing either, replaces only links into an Arch bundle's `Contents/Helpers`, and removes only those.

The `ArchCLI` target builds `arch-cli` (`.build/<configuration>/arch-cli`), which `scripts/bundle_app.sh` copies into `Contents/Helpers` with `arch-mcp` as a relative link to it. It answers `status`, which exits 69 while nothing listens at the socket, as while Allow Outside Clients is off, and 77 when the listener is not the bundle's Arch or the client runs outside an app bundle, and otherwise reports Arch's version and this client's grant; `spaces` (`list_spaces`) and `tabs [--shared]` (`list_tabs`), which print the result's text, or its structured value with `--json`, and exit with its status's code, and whose first call without a grant waits for the grant sheet and says so on standard error, exiting 6 when the user does not allow the client; `release` (`grant/release`); `open <url>` (`open_tab`), `navigate <tab> <url>`, `back`, `forward`, and `reload <tab>` (`navigate`), `close <tab>` (`close_tab`), `text <tab> [--offset n]` (`read_text`), `wait <tab> --load|--text t|--url u [--timeout ms]` (`wait_for`), `downloads` (`list_downloads`), `history <query>` (`search_history`), `snapshot <tab> [--full] [--ref r] [--diff]`, `click` and `hover <tab> <ref>`, `type <tab> <ref> (<text>|--text -) [--replace] [--submit]`, which reads the text from standard input for `--text -`, `press <tab> <keys> [--ref r]`, `select <tab> <ref> <value>...` (`select_option`), `scroll <tab> (<ref>|up|down|left|right) [--amount n]`, and `call <tool> [json]` for any tool, which likewise print the result's text, events included, or its structured value, and which say "Waiting for your approval in Arch…" on standard error and keep calling `approval_wait` while a step waits for the user; `screenshot <tab> [--full|--ref r] [-o file]`, which writes the image only to the file, or to `arch-screenshot-<time>.jpg` in the current folder; `guide [topic]`; `tools`, with `--json` for MCP's `tools/list` form; `--help`, which prints the `cli` topic; `mcp-config [claude|codex|json]`; `setup claude|codex`, which shows the tool's own `mcp add` command, asks y/N, and runs it, exiting 3 on No and 1 when the tool is not on PATH or its command fails; `task "<task>" [--space name] [--mode read|ask|act] [--model name] [--deny-approvals]` (`task/start`), which asks for a grant first as a tool call does, prints the session's place on standard error, the agent's reply as it streams, and each step as an `[Arch]` line until the turn ends, or the summary and each `task/event` as JSON lines with `--json`, exits 0 at the turn's end, 1 when it fails, 4 for a space or model Arch refuses, and 6 for a grant without Start Agent Tasks; `sessions [list|show id|stop id]` (`sessions/list`, `task/stop`) for the client's own sessions; `links install|remove [--dir folder]`, with 2 for a named folder outside the home folder or inside an app bundle, 1 when no folder is writable, and 4 for a file in the way that is not Arch's; and `skills list|install|remove` with `--target claude|codex|codex-home` and `--path <folder>`, both repeatable. `skills list` without a place lists the three targets with their paths and states. `install` and `remove` require a place. `install` prints each `SKILL.md` path before writing. Both exit 4 for a folder that is not Arch's or holds a modified copy: `install` before printing or writing any path, and `remove` after processing the others. Both exit 2 for a folder inside an app bundle, a `--path` that is not a folder, or a `HOME`, `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, or `CODEX_HOME` that a named place needs and that is not absolute; `remove` processes the other places first and exits 4 when both problems occur. `mcp-config`, `setup`, `links install`, `skills list`, and `skills install` exit 77 outside an app bundle. Under the name `arch-mcp`, or as `arch-cli mcp`, it runs `AgentMCPServer` on standard input and output (`MCPServerLoop`): one queue owns the server, threads read standard input and the socket, a connection is made on the queue when a request first needs Arch, so the client's later lines wait for its outcome, and it exits 0 when standard input closes. With Arch not running, lists come from the catalog and calls end in the error result "Arch is not running, or agent access is off. Open Arch and turn on Allow Outside Clients in Settings ▸ AI ▸ Agents." `arch-mcp --session` exits 2 without `ARCH_AGENT_TICKET`, connects at once with the ticket, and exits with its connection's exit code, 69 while nothing listens.

### Agent module

`Agent` is the agents' service in the app (`docs/agents.md`, Local control channel; Personas, history, and permissions; Storage). `AgentService`, which the app shell creates with the session before the windows open, reads `agent.json` (`AgentSettingsFile`, over `SealedSettingsFile`, which `hooks.json` uses too with a Keychain item of its own): `{"seal", "settings"}`, the seal an HMAC-SHA256 over the settings' JSON with sorted keys, its 32-byte key in the login keychain (service `xyz.taeyoon.arch.agent`, `AgentKeychainSealKey`, read once per launch on its own queue, and only when a file exists or Arch first writes one). The settings carry a generation that each write raises, and the Keychain item keeps the last write's generation after the key, updated on the same queue after each write, in order; Settings says so when an update fails. A file whose seal does not match, that does not read, whose key the Keychain no longer has, or whose generation is below the item's, as an earlier copy put back after a Revoke, is renamed `agent.json.unreadable`, and Arch starts with outside access off and no grants; a key the Keychain does not give leaves the file untouched, keeps outside access off and every grant unused, and keeps the Persona the file names agent-only until Try Again. Until the seal is checked, a new session starts in Read Only (`AgentService.newSessionMode`, which the composer, the palette, and `task/start` read) and a session's end keeps Codex's copy of its thread (`keepsCodexCopy`). `AgentSettings` holds Allow Outside Clients, the grants for 24 hours or until revoked, the Agents Persona's ID, and the sessions' settings (`newSessionMode`, `inAppAgentsSearchHistory`, `keepsCodexCopy`); grants until Arch quits stay in memory. The service listens through `AgentEndpoint` only while Allow Outside Clients is on or an in-app session lives, and with outside access off it closes every connection but the sessions': the `Agent` folder must be a folder of this user's of mode 0700, not a link, which Arch makes and never changes; the socket is bound with mode 0600; a socket file is removed only when a `connect` to it fails, so a second Arch reports the channel in use; and stopping removes only the file it bound. Each connection passes the peer checks before a byte is read (`LOCAL_PEERCRED`, `LOCAL_PEERTOKEN`, and the running code's validity and code directory hash against the bundled `arch-cli`'s, read at launch), gets `ready` first, and before each frame must still be held by the process with the checked process ID and version; a connection that fails is closed and logged with the reason and the process's name. Each connection reads and writes on a queue of its own, with a 10-second write timeout. `hello` is refused for another protocol version, a ticket that binds no session, outside access off, and a client Arch cannot identify; otherwise its answer names the session (`bound`), or the client's grant or none. `AgentClientIdentifier` names the client: the nearest ancestor of the peer through `sysctl` that is not one of Arch's tools, by code directory hash, or a shell, `env`, `login`, or `sudo`, by path (`/etc/shells` and the system's tools), with its path and signature from Security and its name from the path it was started with (`KERN_PROCARGS2`), so `claude` rather than its version's file name; a client reached through a shell is marked, since every program in that shell can `exec` `arch-cli` and use its grant; a team signature that passes `anchor apple generic and certificate leaf[subject.OU] = "<team>"` is its identifier and team, any other signature its path and hash, and a chain that reaches launchd or Arch binds the process just below them by its process ID and start time; the start time is read again afterward. A client's first `tools/call` without a grant shows the grant sheet (`AgentGrantSheet`) on the front main window, after a `progress` notification, with a notification without content while Arch is in the background; the calls of one client share one sheet, which closes as Don't Allow after five minutes or at quit. The sheet names the client and its path, says when the client is an interpreter, bound to a process, or reached through a shell, and offers the Persona (Agents, or one of the user's), the space, the access level, the history and agent task scopes, and the duration, Read Only in the Agents Persona until Arch quits first, with sentences on what the client can read; neither button takes the keyboard focus, Escape is Don't Allow, and Allow takes only a pointer click. Every grant but Read Only in the Agents Persona with no scope until quit asks for Touch ID or the login password (`AgentGrantChoice.needsAuthentication`). Don't Allow remembers nothing, and the same client is asked again only after a minute. Allow with the Agents Persona creates it through `BrowserAgentHost` with one space and records it before any of its profiles starts. At the next launch the app reads the recorded ID without the key and passes it to the session when it creates it, so a favorite's tab that the session restores before the service exists starts that Persona's profile agent-only. Revoke, Revoke All, `grant/release`, expiry, a grant's process ending, and its Persona's deletion end a grant: its connections get `revoked` and close. Turning outside access off closes every connection and keeps the grants. `AgentToolHost` runs, for an outside client's grant, every catalog tool over `AgentBrowserHost`, among them `session_info`, `list_spaces`, `list_tabs`, `open_tab` and `navigate` with an address, a ref, or back, forward, and reload, `close_tab`, `read_text`, `wait_for` with `load`, `text`, `url`, or `ref`, `list_downloads`, and `search_history` behind the grant's history scope, with handles `t1`, `s1`, and so on per grant for the launch (`AgentGrantState`), page text and titles as untrusted blocks, and pages agents do not read listed by handle only. It checks arguments against the catalog's parameters. `open_tab` and `navigate` wait up to 30 seconds for the document; `read_text` reads at most 400,000 characters and returns 20,000 at a time within the 48 KB result limit; a page that does not answer a read in 10 seconds gives `timeout`; a wait or read ends with P17 once the user takes the tab over. A result shows a title, a link's scheme, or a download's name, all of which the page chooses, only inside an untrusted block and cut at 300 characters, and names a sign-in's host only when it is the page's own; `list_tabs` and `search_history` leave out their last rows to stay within 48 KB and say how many, and any other result that would pass it answers `limitReached` instead. Each result carries the events since the client's previous call, as sentences after its text and as `events` in its structured value, and the limits that applied: 20 agent tabs, 2,000 calls a day per grant counted from the activity log, and the cuts. `AgentPolicy` holds the rules this build applies: P16 refuses sites excluded from AI, internal pages, files, and other schemes at every address an agent writes, at each main frame navigation, redirect hop, and popup of an agent tab, whoever started it, and at every read and every title a result shows; P17 refuses a tab that is not the client's, as one the user took over; P6 and P11 ask about downloads and links to other apps on cards; and P8, P9, and P10 become events.
