The activity log records each call's site, the host its address names, and the refusing rule. It answers `guide` with `AgentHarness.guideResult(arguments:)`, which checks the arguments itself, so a null topic gives the guide, and an unknown topic, a topic that is not text, or another argument gets `invalidArguments` with the topic names. `tools/list` names the tools it runs that the grant's level and scopes allow, and before a grant all of them, since the grant sheet may allow any; a grant sends `tools/changed` to the client's connections, so `arch-mcp` tells its client to list again. A client's `tools/cancel` from the connection that sent a call ends the call at once with status `error` and code `cancelled` and frees its slot: a call that waits on its card withdraws the card, an `approval_wait` also cancels the call it waits on and withdraws that call's card, one that waits on the grant sheet stops waiting while the sheet stays open for the client's next call and runs nothing once the user answers, and an in-app session's call that waits at its gates runs nothing. A grant the user allows shows its client as connected at once, also when the only call that waited on the sheet was cancelled. `AgentActivityLog` keeps `agent-activity.json`: 90 days of tool calls and grant events (granted, first use in a launch, revoked, released, expired, declined, refused peer) with the client's name, the session's or grant's ID, the Persona, the tool and its class, the site at most, the decision, and the outcome, and renames a file that does not read. `BrowserAgentHost` in `Browsing` gives the session's Personas, spaces, and tabs in sidebar order, adds the Agents Persona, makes a Persona agent-only, gives the search engine's results address, and runs the agent tabs: an agent folder is a `TabFolder` whose `agentSessionID`, the grant's ID, is never encoded; the host opens an agent tab in the background in the default engine or the one asked for, in the client's folder, named after the client and its app ("claude · Terminal") among the open tabs of the grant's space and made with its first tab; it navigates, reads, and closes it; it passes on what `Tab` reports from it (`AgentTabReport`) and asks the service about each of its main frame navigations, redirect hops, and popups. A tab is an agent's while an agent folder holds it (`Tab.agentOwnerID`), so dragging it out, deleting the folder, or the folder's Keep Tabs makes it the user's, which the host reports as a take-over. An agent tab records no visits, denies permission requests without asking (P8), dismisses alerts (P10), leaves `confirm`, `prompt`, leave-page, and sign-in questions to the user without bringing the tab forward (P9), and stops a download the user has not allowed (P6) and a link to another app (P11), reporting each, so that the service asks on a card; alerts, confirms, and prompts carry the origin of the frame that shows them, which both engines give the UI delegate, and the tool host gives the agent a dialog's text only from a frame on the page's site that AI may read. A page it opens joins its folder in its engine. `session.json` leaves agent folders and their tabs out (`SessionSnapshot.keptSidebar`), so unkept agent tabs are gone after quit; closing one adds it to neither the closed list nor the tab close log (`BrowserSession.closeKeeps`), so ⌘Z after one closes reopens no older tab, and a folder goes when its last tab closes or leaves it, by a drag, a pin, or a move to another folder or space. Close Other Tabs and the archive timer pass over agent tabs. A grant's end leaves its folder and tabs to the user until they close them, keep them, or quit. `AgentRun.swift` holds the agent run's plain values (`docs/hooks.md`, The call), which hooks keep and VII.27's runner is to run; nothing runs an agent yet: `AgentRunOptions` (the prompt of at most 4,000 characters, tab access, an `AIRouteChoice`, the record level, tools, limits, a close run's closed tab, and dry run, whose JSON needs `prompt`, `model`, `tabAccess`, and `tools` and defaults the rest), `AgentRunGrant` (tools, written sorted, tab access, the record's floor and ceiling, runs per day, the limits' ceilings, and `returnsReply`), `AgentRunTabAccess` and `AgentRunRecord`, each ordered from less to more, `AgentRunLimits` (timeout in seconds, rounds, and tool calls, each defaulting when omitted), `AgentRunOrigin`, `AgentRunPromptSource`, `AgentRunRequest`, `AgentRunClosedTab`, `AgentRunResult`, `AgentRunAction` (`renamedTab`, which the run log counts as `renameTab`), and `AIFeatureID.agentHook(_:)`.

The agents' page script (`AgentPageScripts`) is one function in the world `arch.agent`, which the host runs through `executeJavaScript(_:inWorld:completion:)` with an operation, `snapshot`, `element`, `boxes`, or `act`, and the policy's word lists, and which answers with JSON text and keeps nothing in the page (`docs/agents.md`, Page snapshots and refs). On WebKit, `executeJavaScript` runs it without a user gesture through `_evaluateJavaScript:withSourceURL:inFrame:inContentWorld:withUserGesture:completionHandler:` while the private API flags allow it (`WebKitUserGestureAPI`), so the page's handlers for the events it dispatches get no user activation, and with the public evaluation's gesture otherwise. Its walk counts the visible interactive elements of the main frame and of each same-origin frame from 1, follows open shadow roots and slots, lists other sites' frames, and leaves out extension frames, hidden elements, `aria-hidden` and `inert` subtrees, and scripts; names follow reduced accessible name rules at most 80 characters, and a field's value never joins a name. A password or hidden input, a field whose `autocomplete` names a credential, a one-time code, or payment (`cc-*`), and one whose label, name, ID, or placeholder names a secret in English or Korean give no value (`value hidden`); email, telephone, address, postal code, and birthday fields show `<personal>`; a tab the user shared shows no values. `AgentPageTools.swift` holds `snapshot`, whose outline the host writes with the policy's annotations and keeps per tab with each ref's fingerprint (tag, role, input type, and the name's first 40 characters) and each link's full address, against the document's address and the tab's count of pages (`AgentTabActivity.pages`), which grows at each change of the tab's address other than in its fragment, reported as `AgentTabReport.addressChanged` from `Tab` before the document finishes loading, and at each finished document, so that a ref whose element differs or whose tab shows another page is `stale`; `diff` compares the lines with the tab's last snapshot through `CollectionDifference`, and a cut lands at a line's end within `max_chars` and the result limit. `screenshot` reads the boxes of hidden and personal fields, other sites' frames, and extension frames, from every subtree that shows, `aria-hidden` and `inert` ones too, and in a shared tab of every field, before and after the capture, which has 10 seconds to answer (`timeout`), captures once more if they moved and then refuses, paints them over in sRGB gray on white, cuts a full page at 8,000 CSS pixels, gives no full page of a Chromium tab with boxes, since Chromium's full-page capture lays the page out again where they can move (`notAvailable`), crops to an element, scales the longest side to at most 1,568 pixels, and encodes JPEG at 0.8, lower in quality and then in size until it is at most 1 MB, as an image part (`AgentToolContent.image`); an empty capture answers `notVisible`. The actions resolve their ref, read the element's facts (role, name, input type, `autocomplete`, value class, whether it is editable, a file input, or a select, whether it submits its form, the kinds of the fields a person fills in its form, the host the submission goes to with the submitter's `formaction`, and the default button's name and value for Enter) and whether it is connected, visible, enabled, and on top at its center, naming a cover, after scrolling it into view in an agent tab and without scrolling a shared tab, whose cover check comes with the action once allowed, then act through DOM events with `inputKind: dom`: pointer and mouse events and `click()`, focus and the prototype's value setter with `input` and `change`, or `insertText` for editable areas, keyboard events with Enter in a form field calling `requestSubmit()` with the form's default button, or on a submit input with that input, `selected` with `change`, scrolling, and hovering; afterward the host waits up to 3 seconds for a load and 300 milliseconds more, and keeps to 10 actions a second. The host gives a tab that no window shows the front window's content size before scripts and captures (`BrowserAgentHost.giveViewport`). `AgentPolicy` holds P1 to P18 as one table (`AgentPolicy.table`) and decides each step (`AgentPolicyStep`) from the mode, the client's kind (in-app or outside), the tab's access, the element's facts, and the allowances: refusals first (P1, P7, P16, P17, P18, and Read Only's), then sensitive cards (P2 to P5, P11, P12, P13 for in-app written addresses, and P15), which no allowance covers, then the mode's cards, which Allow on This Page and Allow on \<site> cover; P13 and P14 apply to in-app sessions with the scope facts that VII.17 computes. `press` with Enter or Space on a button, a submit, reset, image, or button input, a link, a checkbox, a radio button, or a switch counts as a click on it for P3, P4, and P5 (`AgentPolicy.pressActivates`), since a page's key handlers act on untrusted events too, and P5 compares registrable domains as the site scope counts them (`AgentPolicy.site(ofHost:)` through `AgentSite`). `AgentApprovals` keeps the cards (`AgentApproval`) that wait for the user: each waits until an answer, its deadline (an outside client's after 5 minutes), its tab's next page or its close or take-over (withdrawn, so the call is `stale`), or an end, as Stop All Agents (`AgentService.stopAllAgents()`), a client's Stop or Deny and Stop, a revocation, the connection closing, or quit, which deny it and also cancel the client's running calls (`AgentGrantState.cancelCalls(onConnection:)`), which then run no step and open no card; an ended card leaves memory, and the latest 100 outcomes stay. Allow on This Page and Allow on \<site> answer the page's or site's other cards, an allowed step runs only on the page and address it was allowed on, and a sensitive card's Allow and `request_tab`'s Share for an outside client count only after Touch ID or the login password. A call that still waits after 45 seconds answers `approvalPending` with a handle `a1` and goes on, logged as `pending`, and records its own entry and panel step when it ends; `approval_wait` takes its result or answers `approvalPending` again, and is logged as a read. `AgentUserTools.swift` holds `request_tab`, which shares a tab of the grant's Persona that the user picks for reading or acting, `ask_user`, `request_approval` (P15, refused in Read Only), and `hand_to_user`, which shows the tab and waits for Done or Cancel, and the cards for a download (Allow Once, Allow Downloads from This Site, after which the tab asks for the address again; one per tab at a time, the page's other downloads meanwhile not started and reported as an event) and a link to another app, which `NSWorkspace` opens only after Allow. The outside clients' agent panel (`AgentPanel`, `AgentPanelModel`, in `AgentClientPanel.swift`) shows one client's steps, each with a button that shows its tab and a warning when its page text speaks to AI agents, and its cards grouped per page (`AgentApprovalCard`); the service opens it in the front main window's trailing column in place of the page reader when a card waits (`BrowserWindowModel.showAgentPanel(for:)`, `AgentPanelContent.client`, `AgentPanelColumn`), unless the column shows the composer or a session, which stay so that a draft is not lost (`BrowserWindowModel.agentPanel(showingClient:over:)`); the agents indicator's menu then names the client as waiting, and its row shows the client's panel, names the client, its access, and its place as "Persona / Space", and posts "An agent is waiting for you in Arch" with no other content while Arch is in the background, once while a client's cards wait. Cards lead with the step, with a page's names in quotes whose closing quote inside is doubled and "and submit the form" for a submission by Enter, the tab and site, the element marked on a viewport capture, and the exact text or address, then the agent's words under "The agent says:", and a card whose words name a secret leads with `AgentPolicy.secretWarning`. No card has a default button; a sensitive card's Allow, Share, and Done, like the grant sheet's Allow, take only the click the button's tracking loop runs for (`AgentPointerNSButton`), never an accessibility press or `performClick`, and refuse one that a program of the user posted (`AgentPointerCheck`, from `CGEventField.eventSourceUnixProcessID`): the source is read by its owner and running code, and of the user's programs only Apple-signed ones with the signing identifier of Universal Control, VoiceOver, Dwell Control, the accessibility interface server, or Screen Sharing's agent count; a source that cannot be read does not. The activity log records each card's decision.

In-app sessions (`docs/agents.md`, In the browser; In-app sessions: tickets; Site scope and navigation) run on the CLI runner. The app's service registers the `agent` feature (Agent Tasks: trigger user, page, selection, and file data, requirement `agent`) and removes the session folders an earlier run left, except one whose live session holds its `flock` lock (`.arch-lock`, as another Arch's) and a folder without a lock younger than a minute. `AgentService.startSession(_:)` takes the task, the Persona and space (or the Agents Persona's first space, made on first use, with no tab attached), the attached tabs, of which only http and https tabs on sites not excluded from AI are shared, the mode, the model, and the effort, and makes an `AgentSession` bound to that space and Persona with a ticket of 32 random bytes (`AgentSessionTicket`), the attached tabs' handles, and a site scope (`AgentSiteScope`) with their sites and the search engine's results address. It starts the runner through `AIService.startAgentSession(_:)` with the bundled `arch-mcp`, `--session`, `ARCH_AGENT_TICKET`, the tools of the mode's `tools/list` answer, the static harness, and the session's skill folder (`AgentSessionSkills`: `arch-skills` in the session's folder with `.claude-plugin/plugin.json` naming the plugin `arch` and `AgentSkillInstaller.write(AgentHarness.sessionSkills, into:)` for its `skills`), refuses a task that `checkMessage(_:)` refuses, and sends the first message: a short preamble with the attached tabs' handles, then the task, with no title, address, or page text (`AgentSessionPreamble`). A `hello` with a ticket binds the connection to its session when the ticket matches, compared with every live session's in constant time, and the session's running CLI process, as `processDidStart` reported it, is an ancestor of the peer by process ID and start time (`AgentProcessAncestry`); otherwise it is refused with reason `ticket` and logged, and a ticket used from outside a live session's tree adds a notice to the session. On a session's connection, `tools/list` answers the tools of the session's mode among those the session entry serves (`AgentToolHost.sessionTools(for:scopes:)`) and reports a list that differs from the last one to the runner (`toolListDidChange(_:)`), a mode change sends `tools/changed`, and `tools/call` waits for the model's first output for at most 10 seconds, then is refused with code `notReady`, waits while the user has taken the session over for at most 10 minutes, counts against 150 calls per message and 600 per session, runs in the tool host as `AgentToolClient(session:)` through its session entry, which serves `session_info`, `list_spaces`, `list_tabs`, `read_text`, and `guide` until VII.17.1 and whose `session_info` gives the mode, "Persona / Space", the scope, the shared tabs, and the calls left, and becomes a step in the transcript and an activity entry; the runner's idle timer pauses meanwhile. `AgentSession` keeps the transcript (`AgentTranscript`: the user's messages, replies joined from their deltas, thinking, steps, notices, errors with one action, and the model and tokens under each turn), the messages queued during a turn, which start in order after it, the state, the mode, the scope, to which the user's own messages add their addresses, and Take Over and Hand Back. Retry after a session failed before any model held it gives it a new runner; a failure after a model held the conversation offers no Retry, and Start Over With \<model> comes with VII.17.1. Calls wait at the `ready` gate again from each CLI process's start until its own first output (`processReady`), a Stop or a turn's end refuses the calls that waited (`stopped`), a session's `search_history` follows Settings and, for a client's session, its grant's Search History scope, revoking a grant or turning Allow Outside Clients off ends the client's sessions, ending a session sends `failed` to the client that started it, quit returns at its 5-second limit and ends the processes of sessions whose end has not finished, and the composer leaves the model to the `agent` route unless the user picks one (the first preset model, which it shows, when the route has none), offers modes up to Settings' mode, and is switched off with Agent Tasks; it also shows the sentence of a start refused before it opened (`AgentComposerContext.problem`), as for the palette's Ask Agent. `spacesWaitingForUser` gives the spaces of live sessions that wait for the user, and the service republishes a session's waiting as it does its turns. Delete Session closes its connections and ends it with `end(keepingThread:)` under Keep Codex's Copy of Agent Conversations; a deleted Persona deletes its sessions and a deleted space ends its. Stop All Agents stops every session's turn and disconnects every outside client, keeping the grants. `task/start` from an outside client asks for a grant first, requires its Start Agent Tasks scope, and starts the session as `AgentTaskPlan` resolves it: in the grant's Persona and the space it names (or the grant's), with the model it names, at the lower of the grant's level and the Settings default, which the client's mode can only lower, with the client's text adding nothing to the scope and the session marked as the client's (`Origin.outsideClient`, with `--deny-approvals`), and sends the session's `task/event` notifications to that connection; `sessions/list` and `task/stop` reach only the client's own sessions. The sessions open no agent tabs yet and apply the scope at no navigation, since VII.12's tools and VII.13's approvals are not merged (VII.17.1).

`AgentAPISession` runs an API-key session on `AIClient.run` with at most 50 rounds per message (`roundsPerMessage`) of 8,192 output tokens each (`outputTokensPerRound`), no socket, and the conversation in memory, with `AgentHarness.instructions` as the request's instructions. It reports the CLI sessions' `AIAgentEvent`s: `ready` with the model before the first text or tool call, text, the message's usage, and `turnFinished` (`completed`, `stopped`, or `turnLimit`) or `turnFailed`, after which the session stays open, with a notice first when the last reply stopped at the output limit; it pins every later message to the model of `ready`, queues messages sent during a turn, and Stop ends the turn at once, drops the queue, keeps the rounds that finished, and cancels the task of a call the host is running. Its tools come through `AgentSessionTools`, the tool host bound to one session, which VII.17.1 provides (`tools` read at each message, `run(_:)` per call, which withdraws a waiting approval as denied when its task is cancelled); arguments that are not a JSON object never reach the host. A result's text becomes text parts and a page part for each untrusted block with an address (`AgentUntrusted.segments(in:)`, beside `wrap`; a marking that does not parse refuses the result, and the `guide` result goes as text), its images image parts whose source is the result's `structured` `url` or its one page, or a note when neither is known, with "Status: \<status> (\<code>)." first unless it is `ok` and a note without page text. Nothing in the app starts one yet.


### Events module

Live folder refreshes create sidebar rows without posting `tabOpened`. Changes to an item's saved title update its displayed name and post `titleChanged`; neither hooks nor plugins can rename the item. A close from an agent carries the `agent` reason, and a close during Persona deletion carries `spaceDeleted`. Download events keep the source tab kind at download start, so a later takeover or close keeps an agent download out of hooks and plugins.

`Events` holds the browser event bus (`docs/hooks.md`, Browser events). A `BrowserEvent` carries a payload (`tabOpened` with its opener, `tabClosed` with its reason, `pageLoaded` with `isRestore` and `isFirstLoadAfterOpen`, `addressChanged` with the previous address, `titleChanged` with the source of the shown name, `tabSelected` with its window, `downloadFinished` with the file's name, type, size, addresses, and the tab it started from, and `spaceSwitched` with both spaces and Personas), a sequence, a date, and the cause of a change that code made, never page text, titles, or file paths; its payloads' JSON names each value. `BrowserEventBus` delivers each post on the main actor to the subscribers whose filters match, in subscription order, delivers a post made during a delivery once the current event has reached every subscriber, and attaches the cause of the enclosing `withCause` block. `BrowserEventFilter` matches kinds, whole Personas and single spaces (a space switch by the space the window switched to, a favorite's tab and a download by their Persona only), sites and exceptions through `AISitePattern`, engines, and tab kinds, with agent tabs only for a filter that names them; its JSON writes the scope as `"everywhere"` or a list of `{"persona": …}` and `{"space": …}`. `BrowserEvent.view(_:)` makes the script view for hook scripts and script plugins (addresses under `tabs` or a covering site grant, download details under `tabs` or `downloads`, no cause, and nothing from a site excluded from AI for a hook with `agent`, whose excluded previous address also stays out) and the outside view for a grant through `BrowserEventOutsideReader`: handles and reduced addresses for the client's own and shared tabs, watch IDs and hosts for the user's other tabs of its Persona, `excluded: true` without host or address, downloads only from its own agent tabs with the name as an untrusted block, `byThisClient`, window handles, and `spaceSwitched` only with its Persona on one side, the other side as `otherPersona`. `Events` depends on `AI` for site patterns and on `AgentControl` for the agents' address reduction and untrusted blocks. In `Browsing`, `BrowserEventSource` posts every kind to `BrowserSession.events`: it follows the spaces', the favorites', and the small windows' tabs, the windows, and the downloads, and posts `tabClosed` from `closeTab` while the session still lists the tab, with `TabCloseReason` mapped case by case, `spaceDeleted` for a deleted Persona's tabs, and `agent` for an agent tab that the agents' code closes. A popup's `tabOpened` names its opener, which the session sets before the tab joins its list. A tab in an agent folder (`Tab.agentOwnerID`) posts with the kind `agent` from its `tabOpened` on, since the session puts an agent's new tab and the popups of its page in the folder before they join its list, and with its own kind once the user takes it over. Address changes post only for addresses the tab commits (`Tab.committedURL`): one that changes while the page loads, as WebKit reports a navigation's address before it commits, posts once the load or its document's load ends, and none when the tab is back at its earlier address; the tab drops a hook's name at the same point, so the name stays through a navigation that fails, is stopped, or becomes a download, and every event carries the committed address. Title changes follow `Tab.displayTitle`'s inputs with the committed address, wait while the tab's address waits to settle, keeping the cause of a suggested name that code gave meanwhile for the title change that names it, name the input that supplies the shown name (the page's title or host also when another address drops a hook's name, and the user for a hook's name turned down), and none posts while the shown name stays; selections and spaces that restore windows post nothing; `Tab.pendingCause`, which a tab opened inside `withCause` keeps, goes to the tab's next address changes and load, and a load that ends without one, or the user's own navigation from the address field, Home, Back, Forward, the History menu, Reload, the page's context menu, or the crash notice, drops it. `BrowserHooks`, the hook registry (Hooks module), and each Persona's plugin manager (Plugins module) subscribe to the bus; outside programs (VII.30) are to subscribe too.

### Hooks module

The JavaScript hook editor offers Allow Agent Runs with a saved model, instructions, and separate agent permissions for renaming and reading page text. The script’s agent calls are bound to that invocation’s Persona, event tabs, and document addresses. Ending the invocation cancels unawaited calls. See `docs/hooks.md`, Agent run API, for the remaining VII.27 work.

The registry validates a saved hook's agent options against its grant, including the prompt, tool list, tab access, record level, and limits. The usage store can reserve one run per feature against its daily limit across launches, independently of how many model requests a run takes. The API-key hook runner uses the chosen route and checks every candidate against the hook's known providers. It accepts tabs from the hook's Persona and filter, excludes restricted sites, binds suggested names and page reads to the original document, and reports dry-run renames without applying them. At most two runs execute at once. Cancellation, Stop All Agents, and time, tool, and model-round limits stop additional work. A one-round run can apply its final tool calls without another model request. Keep Nothing retains usage only, and only Keep the Session keeps the conversation in memory, at most 20 per feature. A CLI hook uses the same tools over a one-turn ephemeral session, with an MCP ticket bound to its verified process tree and a tool-call gate that opens after the start check. The channel closes and the runner discards the thread when the hook ends. A mixed HTTP/CLI route is refused. Page outlines and activity entries for individual tool calls remain in VII.27.

`Hooks` holds the hook registry (`docs/hooks.md`, Hook registry). A `Hook` has a trigger (`HookTrigger`: a new tab; a close, by its reasons; a page load, its first after the tab opened, one at a new address, ignoring the fragment, or every one, with or without restored tabs; an address change to a new host or any; a title change by its sources; a selection; a finished download; or a space switch, optionally only with a Persona change), a `BrowserEventFilter`, whose kinds are the trigger's and which may not name agent tabs, a script (`HookScript`: JavaScript with its digest and whether the editor wrote it, or a trusted shell script's copy with its source and interpreter), a grant (`HookPermission`'s `tabs`, `tabs.rename`, and `agent`, with an `AgentRunGrant` for `agent`), an agent hook's `AgentRunOptions`, a record level, its timing (delay, timeout, and runs per day, with each trigger's defaults), its state with the pause reason, its origin, and its creation date. `HookStore` keeps `hooks.json`, sealed through `SealedSettingsFile` under its own Keychain item (service `xyz.taeyoon.arch.hooks`), whose key it reads once per launch and only when the file exists or Arch first writes one: a file whose seal does not match, that does not read, whose key is gone, or that is an earlier copy put back is renamed `hooks.json.unreadable`, and a key the Keychain does not give leaves the file as it was and runs no hook until `retryLoading()` reads it. The file is versioned: `HookStore.migrations`, none yet, migrates an earlier version in memory, which is copied to `hooks-v<version>.json` before the next write, and a later version is copied there at once unless a copy is there already, so the copy stays the newer Arch's own file, read as far as this build knows it, and written back with the settings, hook members, and hooks this build does not know unchanged; a hook it cannot read, which in a later version's file includes one with an unknown member inside one of its values, is listed paused with `needsNewerArch` and neither runs, resumes, nor changes. Scripts live in `Hooks/<hook id>/hook.js` or `hook.sh`, mode 0600 in folders of mode 0700, and run only while their SHA-256 matches the digest in the file; one that no longer matches pauses its hook with `scriptChanged` at launch or before a run. `knownProviders` holds the providers `ai.json` lists when Arch first writes the file and each provider the user adds or changes while Arch runs, never one that first appears at a later launch, each by its destination (`AgentRunRequest.destination(of:)`): an HTTP provider's base address, or a command-line tool's executable path with the SHA-256 of its preset, arguments, and environment, so an edit outside Arch to any of them leaves the provider unknown.

`HookRegistry` subscribes to the session's bus and, while Run Hooks is on and the file has been read, matches each event to the active hooks in list order and posts one step for each match to the shared scheduler at order 1,000 plus the hook's place, after the AI hook center's steps for the same event, with the hook's delay and timeout. A hook with `agent` and agent options names its feature `agentHook.<hook id>`, its route, and the tier route in `ai.json` that the route resolves through, so the scheduler applies the feature's checks, budgets, and pause; the registry registers that feature, automatic, `perRequest`, named after the hook, and requiring `agent`, for each hook with `agent`, and unregisters it when the hook loses `agent` or is removed. A tab's runs for an earlier document end when it commits another, and its other runs at its close; a `firstAfterOpen` run so ended lets the tab's next load count as its first, once. A close keeps the closed tab's last shown name and committed address from `HookBrowserHost.closingTab(_:)` in memory with its run, which starts though the tab is gone. Before a run starts, its hook must be active and within its runs for the day, which is checked again once the run holds its slot, its subject must exist and, for page, address, and title events, still show the event's address, ignoring the fragment, and a hook with `agent` gets no event with an address excluded from AI, when the event arrives or after the delay. At most 4 runs run at once, 2 of one hook; waiting runs start in arrival order, and beyond 100 waiting the oldest is dropped and logged as `dropped`. Loop protection skips an event whose cause's hook chain holds the hook or three hooks (`loop`) and runs past 10 for one subject or 60 in all within a minute (`rateLimit`, through `BrowserEventRateLimiter`); an event whose hook chain holds the hook of the chain's running step, such as its rename's title change, joins that chain after its waiting steps instead of replacing it, so a run is never stopped by its own effect (`AIHookScheduler.post(_:kind:subject:event:document:urls:causes:)`); and a hook past a limit logs one skip for it a minute, or a day for `dailyLimit`; a `titleChanged` hook past the subject's cap pauses with `ranTooOften`. Three failed or timed-out runs in a row pause a hook with `failures`; the scheduler's pause after three authentication or provider errors pauses it with `providerErrors`, which `hooks.json` keeps and a change to the tier route its options name lifts, also after a relaunch; an empty tier fails each run with its sentence but toward no pause. `stopAgentHooks()` stops the runs of hooks with `agent` and pauses them with `stopAllAgents`; a deleted Persona or space leaves every filter, and a hook left without a place pauses with `placeDeleted`; Resume clears the hook's counts but keeps it paused while its place, its script, a newer file, or switched-off shell scripts still stop it; Run Hooks off stops every run and leaves the states; `update(_:script:)` keeps a hook's origin, creation date, and state. The daily counts, failure counts, and rate windows are in memory. `test(_:onTab:applying:)` runs a hook once for a tab its filter covers, never an agent tab, as if its event happened, at once and outside the scheduler and the counts, reports actions without applying them unless asked, and gives a close hook that tab as the closed tab, without it among the run's tabs.

Each run gets a `HookInvocation`: the hook, the event's Persona, the event's script view, the cause (the run, with the event's hook chain and the hook), its deadline, whether actions apply, the checked script, the run's tabs (the open tabs the filter covers in the event's Persona, never agent tabs, or the event's tab alone for an outside program's hook), the event's tab, each run tab's document (the event's address for the event's tab of a page, address, or title event, else the tab's committed address as the run starts; none for a tab without one), the closed tab, and the known providers. Scripts run through `HookScriptRunning`, which the registry cancels at a timeout, a newer event, a new document, a close, or a pause; shell scripts get `UnavailableHookScriptRunner`, whose runs fail with `runnerMissing`, until VII.31.

`HookScriptRunner` runs JavaScript hooks (`docs/hooks.md`, JavaScript scripts) in `Plugins`' script runtime: for each run it checks the script once per digest (at most 256 KB of UTF-8 text that parses as a classic script, `HookScriptRunner.problem(in:)`, else `invalidScript`), and runs it in a `ScriptPlugin` made from a package held in memory, whose manifest has the identifier `xyz.taeyoon.arch.hook.<hook id>`, the hook's name, its permissions, and its trigger's kind as `events`, and whose `hook.js` is the checked bytes, so a change to the file after the check cannot run. A hook keeps at most two instances in a Persona, each running one run at a time; a test that comes while both run gets an instance that ends with it, an instance of an older script, name, trigger, permissions, or timeout ends once its run does, and an instance stops after 30 seconds without a run. A run delivers the event through `Plugin.handle(_:)` to the listeners that `arch.events.on(kind, listener)` set, with `hook: {id, name, agent}` added to its script view, and completes with its renames when they settle; a listener that throws or rejects fails the run with its message (`scriptError`), which the bridge's delivery function reports as JSON and `ScriptPlugin.handle(_:)` throws as `PluginError.listenerFailed`, a script without a listener for the kind fails with `noListener` (`PluginError.noListener`), an instance whose web content process ends, or whose document does not load within the hang limit, fails its run with `stopped`, the runner holds the instance it picks (`ScriptPlugin.isHeld`) so that its idle stop cannot end it before the event arrives, and the hook's timeout, which the runner's watchdog enforces with the scheduler, or a cancel ends the instance, so its next run starts the script anew. `HookScriptContext` is each instance's `PluginContext`: it acts only during a run and for that run; `arch.tabs.list()` and `get(id)` give only the run's tabs, without titles and addresses unless the hook has `tabs`, and for a hook with `agent` never those of a tab whose shown address or committed document is on a site excluded from AI; `rename(id, name)` needs `tabs.rename`, refuses another tab with `notGranted`, passes the run's document for the tab and the run's cause to `PluginBrowserHost.renameTab(_:to:showing:cause:applying:)`, only reports in a test, resolves false when the tab no longer shows that document or the user's name or refusal prevents it, and records each name taken as the run's action; storage and secrets hold nothing and refuse writes with `quotaExceeded`, and there are no settings, badges, or clipboard. `HookRunLog` keeps `hooks-activity.json` for 30 days, at most its latest 10,000 entries, and writes it only after an entry or Clear Activity, so a launch without runs leaves none, without content (the hook, the event's and the subject's kinds, the site, the Persona, the outcome with its code or skip reason, the duration, the actions by kind, an agent's model and tokens, and whether a test ran), sets aside a file that does not decode, and logs nothing for a hook at Keep Nothing, whose `lastRuns` keep only the last run's time and outcome; other hooks keep their last 20 runs, read from the log at launch. In `Browsing`, `BrowserHookHost` gives the registry the session's Personas and spaces, its tabs as the bus describes them, with their committed addresses and never agent tabs, the closing tab's shown name and committed address, and a call after Personas or spaces change. `SessionPluginBrowserHost` gives the JavaScript runner and the plugin managers every Persona's open tabs in sidebar order, never agent tabs, and renames a space's tab, not a favorite's, under `BrowserHooks.suggest(_:for:to:applying:)`, the rules of the AI hooks' names (the name's first line, trimmed and cut to 60 characters, for the document the tab still shows, ignoring the fragment, unless the user named the tab or turned the document's suggested name down; nil removes a suggested name and keeps a refusal), inside `withCause` with the caller's cause.

### Terminal module

`Terminal` is the built-in terminal where the user installs and signs in to command-line AI tools. Settings ▸ AI's Open Terminal shows its one window, which runs the user's login shell (`$SHELL`, else the account's shell) in the home folder on a pseudo-terminal from `posix_openpt`. `posix_spawn` starts `/bin/sh` in a new session; it opens the terminal again so the terminal becomes the session's controlling terminal (a terminal that `posix_spawn` opens does not, and neither does the one zsh opens itself), then replaces itself with the shell run with `-l`. Job control, Control-C, passwords read from `/dev/tty`, and window size changes work. Output reaches the screen in batches, one block on the main queue taking everything read since the last, and reading pauses while 64 KB wait, so a program that writes faster than the screen keeps up, such as `cat` of a large file, waits for the terminal and the windows stay responsive. Input that the program in front has not made room for waits in order, retried every 10 milliseconds without holding the write queue; the interrupt, quit, or suspend character typed meanwhile discards that input and the input the program has not read, as the terminal does when it receives the character, so Control-C stops a program that reads nothing after a long paste. The shell gets the app's environment with `TERM=xterm-256color`, `COLORTERM=truecolor`, and a UTF-8 `LANG` when none is set. The screen follows VT and xterm conventions: UTF-8 across reads, C0 controls, cursor movement, erasing, inserting and deleting characters and lines, scroll regions, 16, 256, and direct colors with bold, faint, italic, underline, and inverse, wide East Asian characters and emoji in two columns, combining marks (up to 32 scalars in a cell), the alternate screen, application cursor keys, bracketed paste, cursor visibility, titles, and replies to cursor position, status, and device attribute queries. It keeps 10,000 lines of scrollback, without the blank cells at the end of a line that did not wrap, and does not reflow lines on resize. Keys go through the input method, so Hangul composes at the cursor; Control with a letter sends its control character, also with input sources whose letters are not Latin. Dragging selects text and double-clicking selects a run without spaces, such as an address or a code; Copy, Paste, and Select All work from the Edit menu and the context menu. Paste sends line breaks as carriage returns; with bracketed paste on, the pasted text keeps tabs and carriage returns but no other control character, so an escape sequence in copied text cannot end the paste early and run the rest. Closing the window while a program other than the shell runs in front asks first, then hangs up the terminal; quitting Arch then brings the terminal forward and asks the same way. After the shell exits, the window keeps its output, and Open Terminal starts a new shell. `show(typing:)` shows the terminal and types a command at the shell's prompt without Return, once a new shell's output has paused for 300 milliseconds, and types nothing while a program other than the shell runs in front.

### LiveFolders module

`LiveFolders` keeps live folders up to date as `docs/bookmarks.md` designs them under Live folders; the session uses it for the sidebar's live folders and the New Live Folder sheet (Browsing module, Live folders). `LiveFolderConfiguration` holds a `LiveFolderSource`, a feed address (`https` only, without a user name or password, `http` tried as `https`; an address with a query keeps only the address without it, marked private, while `LiveFolderKeychain` keeps the whole address in a login keychain item per folder, service `xyz.taeyoon.arch.live-folders`) or a `GitHubSearch` (My Pull Requests, Review Requests, Assigned Issues, or a search the user writes, on github.com or another host), with 10, 25, or 50 items and a refresh every 15 minutes, 30 minutes, 1 hour, or 3 hours. `FeedFetcher` loads feeds through an ephemeral `URLSession` with no cookies, cache, or credential storage, 30 seconds and 5 MB per response, redirects only to `https`, and `ETag` and `Last-Modified` sent back, so a 304 keeps the items; 429 and 503 with `Retry-After` postpone the next attempt. `FeedParser` reads RSS 2.0, RSS 1.0, and Atom with `XMLParser` (namespaces on, external entities off) into items with IDs, `http` or `https` addresses resolved against `xml:base`, plain text titles of at most 300 characters, and RFC 822 or ISO 8601 dates, newest first when every item has one, and the feed's `ttl` or syndication interval. `FeedDiscovery` finds up to ten RSS and Atom alternate links in a page's first 512 KB, and `FeedFetcher.discover` tells a feed from a page. `GitHubCLI` finds `gh` in the login shell's PATH once per launch and on Check Again, which reads the login shell again, runs `gh auth status --hostname` for the sign-in state and `gh api graphql --hostname <host> --input -` with a fixed query on standard input, with the minimal environment of command-line tools, the login shell's PATH, `GH_PROMPT_DISABLED`, `GH_NO_UPDATE_NOTIFIER`, `NO_COLOR`, and `GH_CONFIG_DIR` and `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` when the shell sets them to absolute paths, in an empty folder under `TMPDIR`, ending it after 30 seconds or 8 MB of output; exit 4 is signed out, exit 1 shows GitHub's first message or `gh`'s last line of standard error cut at 200 characters, or the other results when every error concerns one result in `search.nodes` and some remain, keeping the read state and dismissals of the results withheld, a failure while `NWPathMonitor` reports no network path counts as offline, and `RATE_LIMITED` waits until `rateLimit.resetAt` or 15 minutes. Pull requests and issues become items with their state, review decision, mergeable state, checks, and the detail line "owner/repo #123 · author". `LiveFolderSchedule` decides when a folder refreshes from its state, configuration, trigger, the time, and Low Power Mode; `LiveFolderStore` keeps each folder's items, validators, fetch state, `viewedAt`, and dismissed items in `live-folders.json`, written half a second after the last change and at quit, moving a file that does not decode to `live-folders.unreadable.json`; `LiveFolderReconciler` plans a folder's tabs from its items as values; and `LiveFolderService`, on the main actor, registers the folders, starts over a folder whose source changed or, through `setPrivateAddress`, whose private feed address changed, refreshes a folder whose item limit changed without validators, counting the older items a raised limit adds as read, records an attempt when it ends, removes other folders' entries and Keychain items after the Keychain work asked for before, runs at most four feed fetches and one `gh` process at once, and checks due folders every 5 minutes through `NSBackgroundActivityScheduler` once started. `swift test --filter LiveFoldersTests` covers it.

### Preferences module

A Settings window (⌘,) uses the system's settings layout, with a toolbar of sections. The window takes each section's title and height. General shows whether Arch is the default browser, with Set as Default… (the system confirms; only an app bundle can register), whether links from other apps open in a small window while Arch is in the background (`ARCHOpensLinksInSmallWindow`, on until the user turns it off; a change applies to the next link), the default engine (it applies to the next new tab; the picker reads the stored value, so a change from the site settings popover shows while Settings is open), the search engine for palette text that is not an address (Google, Bing, or DuckDuckGo, under `ARCHSearchEngine`), the downloads folder (`ARCHDownloadsFolder`), and whether to ask where to save each download (`ARCHAskWhereToSaveDownloads`). Site Search lists keyword shortcuts by name, address, and keyword. Add… and each row's Edit… open a sheet that saves a name, a unique one-word keyword, and an http or https address with `%s`. Delete removes the shortcut. Sidebar has the archive delay and the delay after which pinned tabs and favorites that no window shows unload (`ARCHUnloadAfterHours`, 12 hours by default, 0 for never); Appearance has system, light, or dark, and the space tint's strength (Subtle, Medium, or Strong, under `ARCHTintStrength`). Shortcuts lists the global shortcut and every menu bar command by menu, the Plugins menu's among them, which it lists again each time that menu changes; a plugin command goes without its default shortcut while another command has it (an app command, one the user gave it, or an earlier plugin's command), and without the shortcut the user gave it while an app command or an earlier plugin's command the user gave it has it, since the user may have chosen the two while the menu showed only one of them; its row names that command. Each has a button to record a new shortcut (Escape cancels and Delete removes it) and a button to restore the default. A menu shortcut needs Command or Control unless it is a function key. A shortcut that another command uses moves only after the user chooses Replace, which leaves the other command without one, also when Restore Default would give it back. Arch saves the choices under `ARCHShortcuts`, keyed by the action's selector name with the item's tag or represented string. It applies them to menu items at launch and on each change. A hidden twin such as ⌘= for Zoom In keeps its key only while its command keeps its default. Arch registers the global shortcut, ⌥Space by default, with the Carbon Event Manager's `RegisterEventHotKey`, a public API that needs no accessibility permission. The shortcut brings the last main window forward, or opens one while none is open, and opens the new tab palette there. If the system refuses it, Settings shows why. Plugins lists the plugins of one Persona, web extensions among them, with a Persona picker once there are two, in a scrolling form of fixed height: Install Plugin… and Install from Web Store…, then each plugin's switch with its summary, what its grant lets it do in plain sentences with the API permissions that come with a web extension, its settings as a form (a toggle, text, a number kept within its range, a choice, or a secret field whose value goes to the Keychain on Save and never shows, with Remove while one is saved), which works while the plugin is on, Reload Matching Tabs for an enabled plugin with page scripts, Remove… with a confirmation for an installed plugin, its version and where it came from (built into Arch, the file it was installed from and the date, or the model that wrote it, the date, and the requests), that it is a web extension and where it runs once an engine has checked it ("Runs on WebKit tabs."), why the plugin cannot run on this Mac, why Arch switched it off after repeated failures, because it could not start on any engine it ran on, or because no engine runs its package any more, and, while it is on, why it could not start on an engine. Two web extension plugins that are versions of one extension show as one section at the place of the version installed first, with one switch for both, a row for each version with its engine, version, origin, where it runs or that its engine does not run it, its access, its failures, and Remove Version…, Separate Versions unless the two share an identifier, and Remove…, which removes both. An unpaired web extension plugin has Install \<Engine> Version…, a menu with From a File… and From the Web Store…, for each engine that runs extensions while the plugin runs on another, whatever the checks found, and Join With…, whose sheet chooses another unpaired web extension plugin and the version for each engine, starting where each package runs; while the plugin asks for another engine's version, the footer says so with that menu and Dismiss. From a File… opens a panel on the Settings window for the engine's package types with a message that names the engine and the plugin, and From the Web Store… opens the store sheet; either reviews the package as that version. Install from Web Store… opens the store sheet (`WebStoreInstallSheet`, the first step of `PluginInstallSheet`): a field for a Web Store address or an extension ID whose Continue, available once the text names an identifier, runs `WebStoreInstaller.stage(_:in:)` with a spinner and "Getting the extension from the Web Store…", shows the install flow's failures in red under the field, says "“1Password” 8.12.44.33 is installed, the Web Store's current version." with Done, or puts the package's review in its place. Cancel, closing the sheet, and deleting its Persona cancel the task, which leaves nothing staged. While the Persona has extensions installed from the store, Check for Updates follows those buttons, with "Checking for updates…", "Last checked at 10:42." (with the date for a check on another day), or why the last check failed in red; the section also checks once per Persona per run as its list first shows the Persona. A plugin, or a pair's version, for which the check found a newer version says "Version 8.12.0 is available from the Web Store." with Review Update…, which opens the store sheet without its field, titled "Update “1Password” from the Web Store" and saying that it downloads the version that the last check found without asking the Web Store for a newer one, to download that version and put its review, as an update of that plugin, in its place, also where two versions of a pair share its identifier. The site settings popover and the palette reach the section through `PreferencesWindowController.showWebStoreInstall(of:in:space:)` and `showPlugin(_:in:space:installingVersionFor:)`, which bring Settings forward on Plugins with a `PluginsRequest` for the Persona; once the list the section shows has no sheet, open panel, or staging of a chosen package under way, so that a download or a review in progress stays, the section shows the request's Persona, and its list answers the request with the store sheet started for the identifier or with the version's open panel. Install Plugin… opens a panel on the Settings window for a folder or `.zip` archive of a script plugin or a web extension, or a `.crx` file, stages it, and shows a review sheet with the plugin's name, version, source, summary, whether it is a web extension, where the package runs and each engine's refusal, its permissions in plain sentences, a web extension's other API permissions in one sentence, a switch for each site it asks for, and its files; an update marks what the installed version did not ask for as New and starts each site that version asked for as the user left it, and an unsigned update of a plugin whose `.crx` file was signed says "The installed version is signed by its developer; this package is not." The subtitle and the plugin's Settings footer name a Web Store install "from the Web Store". A web extension that names the same extension as an installed plugin, by identifier or by name with a matching developer field, and that can join it for an engine, says it may be that engine's version and offers Install as the \<Engine> Version for each such engine and Update or Install as a Separate Plugin, starting on the version when each package runs on an engine that refused the other; a version's review, titled "Install the Chromium Version of “Reader”?", has one Install button, installs on or off as the plugin is, warns when the name or developer differs, and offers Cancel only, with why, for a package that the engine refused or with another installed plugin's identifier. An update of a version of a pair is titled with its engine, names each engine whose tabs lose the plugin, lets the user choose the version where the package fits both, and offers Cancel only for a package that would leave the version on no engine. With two or more Personas, every review that the section opens names the Persona it installs in: "Installs in the Persona of “Work / Research” for all its spaces." for an install that starts from a tab or the palette, with the tab's or the window's space, and "Installs in “Work” for all its spaces." for one that starts in the section, New Feature with AI… among them (`PluginInstallSheet.personaSentence(persona:space:among:)`, Persona rule 7). After a new web extension that asks for another engine's version installs, the sheet's last step says so and offers the Install \<Engine> Version… menu, which reviews the chosen package or puts the store sheet in the sheet's place, Don't Ask Again, and Done. Install, Install and Enable, or Update installs with the chosen sites into the shown Persona, after Touch ID or the login password when they include every website or the grant includes native messaging; Cancel deletes the staged package, and deleting that Persona closes the sheet. While a provider can serve Plugin Writer, which Settings registers when its window opens, New Feature with AI… opens a sheet for a description of the feature. Write Plugin shows the review sheet of the plugin that AI wrote, whose subtitle names the model, with the requests and every file's source, and Ask for Changes… goes back to the field for a change, whose version replaces the one under review. Stop ends a request, the AI interface's errors about models, keys, and limits offer Open AI Settings, and closing the sheet discards the version under review. Websites lists sites with remembered permission answers in a scrolling form of fixed height. A menu switches each answer between Allow and Don't Allow. A button removes it so the site asks again. The footer says that websites can't show notifications in WebKit tabs. Switching a site's Notifications to Allow asks macOS while it has not asked, and while a site is allowed and Arch's notifications are not on, a group at the top offers Turn On Notifications… or Open System Settings (Permissions, sheets, and dialogs). Settings ▸ AI has eight pages: Providers (Open Terminal, and Add Provider for an HTTP API, a command-line tool, or the Claude Code or Codex preset, which finds `claude` or `codex` in the login shell's PATH, shows its version and sign-in from the tool's status commands with Refresh and, while it is signed out, Sign In, which types the tool's sign-in command at the built-in terminal's prompt without running it, and keeps only its environment, models, and limits, with a Check that runs the status command and Arch's probes but no model; an HTTP provider has its name, format, base URL, key, non-secret headers, models with input limit, price, and capability overrides, Refresh from the provider with the typed key as Save stores it, without surrounding spaces and line breaks, and limits, a price's currency in uppercase, as costs count under it, and replacing or removing a saved key, or saving a new base URL that the saved key would go to, asks for Touch ID or the login password; a command-line tool has its name, executable (Find looks a name up in the login shell's PATH, Choose… picks the file), arguments, environment, plain text or JSON lines output with its field paths, Runs without its own tools with its explanation, models with the tool's default model, limits, Open Terminal, and Check, which sends a short request and shows the reply or the error), Features (the default model and, for each registered feature with a stored route, its switch, model, two fallbacks, what it sends, and its last error, with a Test Prompt feature that streams a reply, names the model that answered, and stops when another page or section shows), Model Tiers (Fast, Standard, and Deep, each with the same model menus starting at None, every connected model, and a sentence on the tier's purpose that names no feature, under a note that the features under Features use the models chosen there), Sites (the exclusion list), Usage (this and last month by feature, a `perRequest` feature by its name, and by model, the daily automatic limit, and the monthly spending limit), Activity (the last 50 requests with Show Content for those that recorded content), and Agents (the agent sessions' New sessions start in, Let In-App Agents Search History, and Keep Codex's Copy of Agent Conversations, Allow Outside Clients for `arch-cli` and `arch-mcp` with what it lets programs do, the command-line tools (the bundle's `Contents/Helpers`, the links into an Arch bundle in the login shell's PATH, each folder once, Install Command-Line Tools…, which picks the folder through `AgentLinkInstaller`, `~/.local/bin` first, shows the tools' folder and both links before it writes them, and then names each file that PATH reaches first by a link's name and a terminal runs instead, Remove, which removes only links into an Arch bundle, the Claude Code command and Codex's `config.toml` table from `AgentMCPConfiguration` for the bundle's `arch-mcp`, each with Copy, a sentence that the links could not be checked, with the bundle's `arch-cli links install` and `links remove`, when the login shell's PATH cannot be read, and, in an app outside a bundle, a note that it has no tools; while macOS runs the bundle from an App Translocation folder, which changes at each launch, the section shows neither Install nor the configurations but says to move Arch to the Applications folder and open it again), the skills (for Claude Code and for Codex, whose row uses only `~/.agents/skills`, the `arch-browser` folder that `AgentSkillInstaller` names from the login shell's `HOME` and `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, with its state, Install… and, for an outdated copy, Update…, which show the `SKILL.md` path and the `arch-cli` it names before writing, and Remove for Arch's unchanged copy; a copy changed after Arch wrote it and a folder Arch did not write show that Arch leaves them alone, with no buttons, a variable that is not an absolute path leaves its row without a folder, a row for the folder under the login shell's `CODEX_HOME` appears with Remove only while it holds a skill of that name, as `arch-cli skills install --target codex-home` writes it, and Codex's row offers no Install meanwhile, since Codex would list a second copy twice, a translocated bundle offers only Remove, and when the login shell's environment cannot be read, the section names the bundle's `arch-cli skills install` and `skills remove` to run in a terminal), the grants with their client, path, access, Persona, space, scopes, expiry, last use, and whether the client is connected, with Revoke and Revoke All, and the activity log by day, newest first and at most 300 entries, with filters by client and site and Clear Activity, and notices for an `agent.json` set aside, a key the Keychain did not give, with Try Again, a socket that cannot listen, with Try Again, and a failed write), and Agent Hooks, below. Open AI Settings in an AI feature's error opens Settings on its AI section.

In Settings ▸ Plugins, the footer of a web extension plugin whose grant has `nativeMessaging`, on or off, or of a pair with such a version, adds the lines of `NativeAppGuide` (Extensions module) after its failures and before the note that asks for another engine's version; the section's first showing in a run starts the run's pass for native apps when none ran. `Preferences` depends on `Extensions` for them.


Settings ▸ AI ▸ Agent Hooks (`AgentHooksPage`) has Run Hooks, the notices about `hooks.json` (a file set aside, a key the Keychain did not give with Try Again, a file from a newer Arch, a change not saved), New Script Hook…, and every hook in run order: its switch, name, situation, places as Persona rule 7 names them (`BrowserSession.displayedPlace(ofPersona:space:among:)`: "Persona" and "Persona / Space", or, with one Persona, a space's name alone and the Persona's place as Everywhere, and "Deleted Persona" or "Deleted Space" for a place that is gone), sites, an agent hook's model and record, its last run, the program that added it, a paused hook's reason with Resume, or Review for a changed script (an unknown provider's Review comes with VII.27, which pauses hooks for one), and a menu with Edit…, Run on Current Tab (`HookRegistry.test(_:onTab:applying:)` on the front main window's selected tab), Duplicate, and Delete…, which asks first; dragging reorders rows, and a row opens to its last runs. `ScriptHookSheet` edits a JavaScript hook's name, situation with its options, places (with one Persona its spaces alone, so a hook for the only Persona opens as Everywhere), sites, engines, tab kinds, Read Tab Titles and Addresses (`tabs`), Rename Tabs (`tabs.rename`), and script, starting from a template for the situation, and checks the script with `HookScriptRunner.problem(in:)` at Add, or Save for a hook that exists; Review opens it on the script as the file holds it, and Save records its digest and resumes a hook that the change paused. Edit… of a file changed outside Arch opens it for review too, and Duplicate is off for a hook paused with `scriptChanged` and refuses a changed file (`HookScriptActions`), so the bytes another program wrote run only once the user has read and saved them. The last tab kind left on cannot be turned off, since an empty set means every kind. `HookRegistry.places` names the places and `scriptFile(of:)` reads a script whatever its digest, says whether it matches, and pauses an active hook whose file no longer matches with `scriptChanged`.

### App shell

Before anything reads a preference or starts an engine, `BundleIdentifierMigration` (in `Browsing`) moves the data macOS keeps under the previous bundle identifier, `local.arch.browser`, to the bundle's: it copies the preferences the new domain lacks, except `ARCHAskedDefaultBrowser`, so that Arch asks again to become the default browser, and removes the old domain, renames `~/Library/WebKit/<identifier>` (the default WebKit store and each Persona's), `~/Library/HTTPStorages/<identifier>`, and `<identifier>.binarycookies` beside it while the new name is free, deletes `~/Library/Caches/local.arch.browser`, and adds each AI key of the old Keychain service under `xyz.taeyoon.arch.ai`, and WebKit's WebCrypto master key, which wraps the CryptoKey objects that sites keep in IndexedDB, under the account `com.apple.WebKit.WebCrypto.master+xyz.taeyoon.arch`, before deleting the old item. Old data is removed only once its new copy exists, so a run that stopped partway finishes at the next launch, and later runs find nothing to move.

`PluginIdentifierMigration` (in `Plugins`) follows by the same rules and moves the plugin data that earlier builds kept under identifiers that start with `local.arch.`: in each Persona's folder it renames the folder of each plugin whose identifier starts with `local.arch.browser.` (Page Address's `local.arch.browser.page-address` becomes `xyz.taeyoon.arch.page-address`) or `local.arch.generated.` (plugins that AI wrote, whose identifiers start with `xyz.taeyoon.arch.generated.` now) while the new name is free, after writing the new identifier into its package's `plugin.json` (a folder whose `plugin.json` it cannot write keeps its name until a later launch), and renames the identifiers of the choices and installs in `plugins.json`, where an entry under the new identifier wins; it adds each plugin secret of the services `local.arch.browser.plugin.<plugin id>` under `xyz.taeyoon.arch.plugin.<new plugin id>` unless the new service has the account, then deletes the old item, and once every item has moved sets `ARCHPluginSecretsMoved`, so that later launches do not list the Keychain's items again, and until an item has moved, removing its plugin or deleting its Persona deletes it by the service it moves to, so that it does not come back; and it renames the plugin identifiers in the command identifiers of the user's shortcuts (`ARCHShortcuts`), where a shortcut under the new identifier wins. Staging refuses packages with those identifiers, so they do not come back.

`Arch` then registers both engines and creates the session, which keeps its files and stores in the app's Application Support folder (`userDataDirectory`, the folder `PluginIdentifierMigration` also takes), starts the default engine (or the other when the default fails), and starts the other engine when a tab first needs it; passes the built-in plugins (Page Address) to the session when it creates it, for every Persona's manager, the Chromium engine's CEF version (`extensionRuntimeVersion`, read without starting CEF), whose Chromium version the Web Store's requests carry, and the host manifest folders of other browsers (`BrowserSource.nativeMessagingHostFolders`), which the native app links read, and the session to Settings, whose Site Search, Websites, and Plugins sections use it; passes the Agents Persona that `agent.json` records, read without the key, to the session when it creates it; creates the agents' service (Agent module) with a `BrowserAgentHost` over the session before the windows open and gives it to Settings ▸ AI ▸ Agents and to the session, for the agent panel, with the closure that opens the built-in terminal with a preset's sign-in typed; creates the hook registry (Hooks module) on the session's bus with a `BrowserHookHost` and `AIService.shared`'s hook scheduler before the windows open, with `HookScriptRunner` on the session's `SessionPluginBrowserHost` and the AI interface's site check for JavaScript hooks and a runner that refuses shell scripts until VII.31, and gives it to Settings ▸ AI ▸ Agent Hooks; builds the menu bar (File with New Window (⌘N), Close Window (⇧⌘W), New Agent Task, hidden until a Claude Code or Codex model can run a session and acting on the active window, a small window included, Stop All Agents, enabled while an agent acts, Share, Import from Browser, Import Bookmarks and History, Export as PDF, and Print; Edit; View with Actual Size (⌘0), Zoom In (⌘+, also ⌘=), and Zoom Out (⌘-), enabled while a tab is selected and no internal page covers it, Capture Viewport, Capture Full Page, and Capture Region… (enabled likewise), Show or Hide Page Reader (hidden until a provider can serve the reader, enabled over a tab that no internal page covers), Show or Hide Agent Panel (hidden like New Agent Task and acting on the same window), and Developer (Show or Hide Developer Tools, the side they dock to, and Show Tab Close Log) and Appearance submenus; Tab with Open in Split View (⌥⌘=), enabled while the selected tab can open as a pane and no internal page covers it, and Go Back to Saved Page, Use Current Page as Saved Page, and Edit Saved Address… (Saved pages); Spaces, with New Persona…, Rename Persona…, and Delete Persona… for the sidebar target's Persona, disabled while that window shows a sheet and the last also for the default Persona; Bookmarks, which lists the favorites and the spaces' pinned folders and tabs for the sidebar target whenever it opens (Bookmarks menu); History, with Home (⇧⌘H), which lists the selected tab's previous ten pages whenever it opens and has Show All History (⌘Y) for the History window, Clear History…, which opens the History window with its range sheet, and Clear Site Data…; Plugins, before Window, with the Plugins menu items of the enabled plugins of the active window's Persona, or of the default Persona while no window is open, one section per plugin under its name, each with its command's shortcut, hidden while empty and disabled while no browser window is open; Window with Minimize (⌘M), Zoom, Move to Main Window (⌥⌘N, enabled while a small window is in front), and Bring All to Front, after which AppKit lists the browser windows and the windows of undocked developer tools; docked tools are no window), hands the menu bar to the Preferences module's shortcut settings, which apply the user's shortcuts and register the global shortcut, then adds the Plugins menu, whose items the shortcut settings list each time they change, and only then makes the Window menu AppKit's windows menu, so that Settings ▸ Shortcuts lists none of AppKit's items, has the session open the saved windows, opens a main window on a Dock click while none is open, gives Settings ▸ AI the closures that open the built-in terminal, as it is or with a command typed at its prompt, opens links sent by other apps (`CFBundleURLTypes` for http and https) in a small window while Arch is in the background and Settings ▸ General allows it, and as tabs of the main window otherwise, prompts once to become the default browser and offers a menu item for it (registering `http` also makes Arch the handler for `https`; the menu item is disabled once Arch is the default), confirms a quit, also the one that closing the last main window starts, while downloads are in progress or paused, which quitting stops and the next launch does not list, and while a program runs in the built-in terminal, asking before it answers the tabs' prompts so that Cancel leaves their cards showing, ends the agent sessions before quitting (`terminateLater` while one lives, for at most 5 seconds, then the process groups that are left end at once), and saves the session and finishes the history store's writes at quit, when the agents' service also closes its grant sheets as Don't Allow, writes `agent.json`'s last-use dates and the activity log, and removes its socket. Close Tab (⌘W) closes another of the app's windows when it is key and is disabled while a sheet shows on that window, such as Settings' provider editor or History's Clear History…. `ARCH_START_URL` opens a page at launch.

It answers AppleScript through `Arch.sdef`: the standard suite, `open location`, `active tab` (the selected tab of the browser window that last became main), and the `tabs` element in sidebar order (`BrowserSession.tabsInSidebarOrder`, which the agents' `list_tabs` reads too: the favorites' open tabs, then each space's pinned tabs and its open tabs, then the small windows' tabs), each tab with its `id`, `name` (the title the sidebar shows), and `URL`. AppKit delivers `open location` to `application(_:open:)` like links from other apps. Arch opens only http and https addresses from either, so a file, `mailto:`, or other address opens nothing. It reads text without a scheme as the address field does, first with AppKit's escapes decoded, then as it came, so that an escape the script wrote, such as a query's `%20`, stays, and ignores text that is not a web address (`BrowserSession.webAddress(forOpened:)`).

### Build

`scripts/setup_cef.sh` downloads the pinned CEF distribution, release `arch-8078-6` of `taeyoon0137/arch-chromium`, with the GitHub CLI. The bridge needs the experimental DevTools, picture-in-picture, extension runtime, notification, and password AutoFill APIs of the engine changes under Engine Plan, which `arch-8078-6` carries; `ARCH_CEF_ARCHIVE` installs a local build of `arch-8078` instead, to try it before its release. `Package.swift` builds the CEF wrapper and the bridge against CEF's experimental API version (`CEF_API_VERSION`), which holds the API Arch's engine adds. `swift build` builds every target (Xcode 27, Swift 6.4); `scripts/bundle_app.sh` assembles `build/Arch.app` with the bundle identifier `xyz.taeyoon.arch` (the helper apps `xyz.taeyoon.arch.helper` and `xyz.taeyoon.arch.helper.<gpu, renderer, plugin, alerts>`), the CEF framework, records the installed SDK version in the executable with `vtool` (SwiftPM writes the deployment target there, which makes macOS run the app with that older system's design), the helper apps, `arch-cli` in `Contents/Helpers` with `arch-mcp` as a relative link to it, the app icon from `resources/assets/sym_arch.png` (`scripts/opaque_icon.swift` fills its transparent corners so that macOS clips it to the system shape instead of placing it on a gray plate), usage descriptions, URL types, and the scripting definition (`Arch.sdef` in Resources, named by `OSAScriptingDefinition` with `NSAppleScriptEnabled`), then signs it with the user's own individual team: the newest Developer ID Application identity on the Mac, then the newest Apple Development identity, whose certificate names its holder as its organization; without one, the first Apple Development identity, or ad hoc (`ARCH_SIGN_IDENTITY` overrides the choice). With a provisioning profile that suits the identity (for `xyz.taeyoon.arch` on macOS, not expired, listing the identity's certificate, and of its kind: Developer ID, or development listing this Mac), the newest in Xcode's profile folder or the one at `ARCH_PROVISIONING_PROFILE`, which must suit, the script embeds the profile as `Contents/embedded.provisionprofile`, signs the helper apps and the framework without entitlements, and then signs the app alone with the entitlements it writes to `build/Arch.entitlements` from those the profile grants: the application and team identifiers, the iCloud containers with CloudKit and their environment (Production for Developer ID, as Xcode's export chooses, and Development otherwise) when the profile grants a container and CloudKit, and the push environment when it grants push. It names the container, the CloudKit environment, and the push environment the app got, and warns when the profile grants no container. Without one, it signs as before, with no entitlements. `scripts/provision_app.sh` gets both profiles through Xcode's automatic signing of a stand-in project with the bundle identifier and those entitlements, which it writes to a temporary folder: a development profile through a build, which registers the Mac, and a Developer ID profile through an archive exported with the `developer-id` method. Xcode signs in with its account or an App Store Connect API key, and the script reports each profile's state without the team, explains a failed sign-in or a profile without the iCloud container, and removes the distribution logs that the export adds to the Darwin user temporary folder (`getconf DARWIN_USER_TEMP_DIR`, whatever `TMPDIR` says), which name the team and the certificate's holder. Command-line automatic signing turned on iCloud and push for the App ID but did not assign the container `iCloud.xyz.taeyoon.arch`: Xcode's first development profiles listed no container, and its builds failed because the profile did not match the entitlements file's `com.apple.developer.icloud-container-identifiers`. Once the container was assigned to the App ID, the next profiles listed it. The Mac now has both profiles, each listing a certificate on the Mac and granting the container, CloudKit, and push: the development profile lists the Mac and grants development push until 2027-11-14, and the Developer ID profile grants production push until 2044-11-09. `bundle_app.sh` embeds the Developer ID profile by default: the signed bundle passes `codesign --verify --deep --strict`, its entitlements list the container, CloudKit with the Production environment, and production push, and it launches through `open` with its helpers. With `ARCH_SIGN_IDENTITY` set to the Apple Development identity, it embeds the development profile and the bundle lists the Development environment and development push; that bundle was not launched, since the Keychain items Chromium created belong to the Developer ID identity and another identity makes macOS ask for them. Gatekeeper rejects the bundle as an unnotarized Developer ID app, which `open` from the build folder does not check. `scripts/install_app.sh` copies a `build/Arch.app` signed by a team into `/Applications` as a real bundle for native apps that accept only browsers there, replacing only an earlier Arch or a link and keeping it until the copy, whose signature it verifies first, is in place, and refuses one signed ad hoc or unsigned (`docs/password-managers.md`, Installing Arch into /Applications). macOS ties permissions (camera, microphone, Bluetooth, location, notifications, and automation) and the default browser setting to the bundle identifier, so they did not move from `local.arch.browser` and the user grants them again. The app is run from the bundle; a bare `.build/debug/Arch` can point at CEF through the environment variables under Build Procedure, which is unverified on the current Mac. There are no third-party Swift dependencies. `swift test --filter ARCHEngineChromiumTests` runs the Chromium adapter's picture-in-picture tests (Swift Testing): where the source view sits below the page, an IOSurface wrapped as a sample buffer without a copy, with a clean aperture for a smaller content rectangle and a format description kept while frames match it, and which stops pause the video or select its tab, the adapter's check of extension packages, and its rules that need no engine: cookies into CEF's store and back (host-only domains and insecure `SameSite=None` cookies), the Caches counterpart of the profiles' root, where a grant lets an extension run without a click and which of its sites go to Chromium with a click setting, when a first load keeps Chromium's copy, when a crashed page reloads, the key events its trusted input sends for a press and for text, with the text it refuses, and the file chooser request it makes of CEF's mode, types, and default path; and the context menu parity tests, which build WebKit's menus for thirteen situations, nine from the context menu prototype's records and four assumed from them (a misspelled word without guesses, a password field with and without a selection, and a link around an image), take Chromium's input in each from Blink's source, since the prototype recorded none, and check that the Chromium adapter hands the tab the same items, in the same order and enabled state, as the WebKit adapter keeps from them, apart from the caret difference that row 49 lists, when Chromium's Copy shows outside a field, what each of Chromium's items runs, and which view AppKit is asked about.
