### Documentation

Engine design in `docs/engine-protocol.md`, the sync design in `docs/sync.md`, the plugin system design in `docs/plugins.md`, the AI interface design in `docs/ai.md`, the agents design in `docs/agents.md` (its control channel library, the endpoint with grants, the sealed settings, the activity log, agent tabs, the tool host with the reading and navigating tools, the CLI runner in `AI`, in-app sessions with their composer and panel, the API-key loop with `AgentAPISession`, `arch-cli`'s commands with `task` and `sessions`, and `arch-mcp` are built; its Facts hold the checked Claude Code and Codex behavior and the bundle's signing facts), the hooks design in `docs/hooks.md` (browser events, the hook registry, the script runner, agent runs with model tiers, the agent hook editor, and outside programs; browser events, the shared hook scheduler, model tiers, the hook registry, and JavaScript hook scripts are built), the design for multiple windows, the small window, and Personas in `docs/windows-personas.md`, the browser import design in `docs/import-browsers.md`, the Web Store install design in `docs/web-store.md` (package proof, the client, staging, the entry points, Chromium's store button, and updates built), the password manager design in `docs/password-managers.md` (host discovery, the proof gate, installing into `/Applications`, and the status in Settings built; the checks with the app and the user's steps not done), the bookmarks and live folders design in `docs/bookmarks.md` (saved pages, unloading, nested folders with their dragging, the Bookmarks menu, the `LiveFolders` module, live folders in the sidebar, and the New Live Folder sheet built), the feature list in `docs/features.md`, and `README.md` generated from `resources/README.preset.md` with the hero image from `resources/assets`.

## Repository Layout

```
Package.swift                               Package manifest (maintained by hand)
Packages/ARCHKit/Sources/ARCHEngine         Engine protocols, value types, registry, selection, favicon loader, camera and microphone access, context menu presentation
Packages/ARCHKit/Sources/ARCHEngineWebKit   WebKit adapter (secondary and debug engine)
Packages/ARCHKit/Sources/ARCHEngineChromium Chromium adapter using the Chromium Embedded Framework (CEF), with picture in picture through AVKit and web extensions through Arch's engine
Packages/ARCHKit/Sources/ARCHPrivateAPI     Private API registry, availability checks, and flags
Packages/ARCHKit/Sources/CEFBridge          Objective-C++ bridge over the CEF C++ API: runtime, request contexts, browsers, extensions, and the helper's renderer side, with a header they share; copied CEF wrapper sources are ignored by Git
Packages/ARCH/Sources/Browsing              Session, Personas, window model and controller, sidebar, small window, command palette, find bar, split view, previews, page reader, downloads list, plugin items and page scripts in tabs, the browser event source, AI hook events and actions, the hook registry's and the plugins' browser hosts, web extension windows and actions, the agents' browser host, agent panel placement and indicator, session store
Packages/ARCH/Sources/History               SQLite visit store and suggestions
Packages/ARCH/Sources/Preferences           Settings window and appearance setting, and Settings ▸ AI ▸ Agent Hooks with the script hook sheet
Packages/ARCH/Sources/Import                Bookmark and history import from exported files, and readers of other browsers' files
Packages/ARCH/Sources/AI                    AI client, service, routing, page access checks, hook center and shared hook scheduler, HTTP formats, command-line tools, Claude Code and Codex presets and agent sessions, the API-key tool loop, Keychain storage, ai.json, usage
Packages/ARCH/Sources/Terminal              Built-in terminal: pseudo-terminal, parser, screen, view, and window
Packages/ARCH/Sources/LiveFolders           Live folder sources, feed parsing, discovery, and fetching, the GitHub CLI, private feed addresses in the Keychain, the schedule, live-folders.json, the tab plans, and the refresh service
Packages/ARCH/Sources/Extensions            Web extensions as plugins: manifest.json, identifiers, the runtime per Persona, access questions and engine prompts, the popup presenter, and native messaging with native app status
Packages/ARCH/Sources/AgentControl          Agents' control channel: framing, JSON-RPC, hello, methods, tool calls and results, task messages, socket location, code identity checks, tool catalog, limits, untrusted wrapper, exit codes, harness, command line, and the client
Packages/ARCH/Sources/Events                Browser event bus, events and their causes, filters, and the delivery views for scripts and outside programs
Packages/ARCH/Sources/Hooks                 Hook registry: hooks, the sealed hooks.json and script folders, matching and runs on the shared hook scheduler, limits, pauses, and the run log; the JavaScript hook runner and its script context
Packages/ARCH/Sources/Agent                 Agents' service: control endpoint, client identity, grants and the grant sheet, sealed settings files (agent.json, and hooks.json's seal), activity log, the browser host protocol, the tool host and its rules, the agents' page script, the cards that wait for the user with the outside clients' panel, in-app sessions with their tickets and site scope, the composer and panel views, the API-key session, and the agent run's values
Packages/ARCH/Sources/Plugins               Plugin manifests, match patterns, shortcuts, contributions, page scripts, storage, Keychain secrets, manager, context, events, the browser host protocol, the built-in Page Address plugin, script plugins: packages, plugin.json, sandbox, bridge, and runtime, .crx files, the runtimes of other kinds, and the composer of plugins that AI writes
Packages/ARCHKit/Tests/ARCHEngineChromiumTests Chromium adapter tests (Swift Testing): picture-in-picture source view, sample buffers, and stops, extension package checks, the cookie, profile root, site access, and kept copy rules, context menu parity with the WebKit adapter, trusted input's key events, and file chooser requests
Packages/ARCHKit/Tests/ARCHEngineTests      Favicon rule, favicon loading, capture access, key and wheel input, file chooser rule, and context menu presenter tests (Swift Testing)
Packages/ARCHKit/Tests/ARCHEngineWebKitTests WebKit adapter tests (Swift Testing)
Packages/ARCHKit/Tests/ARCHPrivateAPITests  Private API flag and availability check tests (Swift Testing)
Packages/ARCH/Tests/AgentControlTests       Control channel, catalog, command line, harness, and client tests (Swift Testing)
Packages/ARCH/Tests/AgentTests              Endpoint, grant, seal, identity, activity log, tool host, policy, approval, WebKit page script, API-key session, and agent run value tests (Swift Testing) with Fixtures
Packages/ARCH/Tests/AITests                 AI, API-key loop, model tier, agent session, and plugin composer tests (Swift Testing) with a URLProtocol stub and shell scripts as command-line tools, and recorded Claude Code and Codex streams in Fixtures/Agent
Packages/ARCH/Tests/BrowsingTests           Session file loading, migration, Persona, Agents Persona, agent tab, sidebar order, window placement, small window, hook action, browser event source, `BrowserHooks`, cookie copy, Web Store entry point, developer tools dock, and file chooser panel tests (Swift Testing)
Packages/ARCH/Tests/EventsTests             Browser event payload, filter, delivery view, and bus tests (Swift Testing)
Packages/ARCH/Tests/ExtensionsTests         Web extension tests (Swift Testing) and a sample web extension in Fixtures/
Packages/ARCH/Tests/HistoryTests            History store suggestion, import, and removal tests (Swift Testing)
Packages/ARCH/Tests/HooksTests              Hook store, registry, run log, and JavaScript runner tests (Swift Testing) and hook scripts in Fixtures/
Packages/ARCH/Tests/ImportTests             Import reader tests on synthetic browser files and databases (Swift Testing)
Packages/ARCH/Tests/LiveFoldersTests        Live folder tests (Swift Testing) with a URLProtocol stub and shell scripts as gh and the login shell
Packages/ARCH/Tests/PluginsTests            Plugin tests (Swift Testing) and a sample script plugin in Fixtures/
Packages/ARCH/Tests/PreferencesTests        Shortcut settings, provider key, review Persona sentence, preset, and hook text tests (Swift Testing)
Packages/ARCH/Tests/TerminalTests           Terminal parser, screen, paste, and pseudo-terminal tests (Swift Testing)
Packages/ARCHApp/Sources/Arch               App target with the application delegate, menu bar, Plugins menu, and AppleScript support
Packages/ARCHApp/Sources/ArchHelper         Executable for CEF helper processes
Packages/ARCHApp/Sources/ArchCLI            arch-cli, which runs as arch-mcp under that name; bundle_app.sh copies it into Contents/Helpers
Frameworks/                                 CEF distribution (not committed)
build/                                      Assembled app bundle (not committed)
docs/                                       Design documents
resources/assets/                           Hero images and the app icon source
resources/                                  README source and hero wrapper
scripts/readme_update.sh                    README generation
scripts/setup_cef.sh                        Downloads the pinned CEF distribution; copies wrapper sources into CEFBridge
scripts/bundle_app.sh                       Bundles the CEF framework, helper apps, arch-cli, and icon into build/Arch.app
scripts/provision_app.sh                    Gets the provisioning profiles that bundle_app.sh embeds
scripts/install_app.sh                      Copies a build/Arch.app signed by a team into /Applications for native apps
scripts/opaque_icon.swift                   Makes the icon opaque for the system icon shape; run by bundle_app.sh
scripts/check_markdown.py                   Finds Markdown that GitHub renders differently from the text; --fix escapes placeholders
```

## Working Rules

1. Write new code for each feature, with interfaces, names, and layout designed for Arch.
2. Maintain `Package.swift` by hand. Add one target per new module.
3. Route every AI call through the AI interface. No feature calls a provider directly.
4. Write documentation, comments, and commit messages in English.
5. Document the current state only. Do not include a change history. When a queue task finishes, remove its row and fold the result into Current State and `docs/features.md` in the same commit.
6. Stage by path (`git add <paths>`) and keep one purpose per commit. Queue tasks use the `Task(<ID>):` subject; other work uses the prefixes in `.githooks/commit-msg`.
7. Work on `main`. Branches named `cloud/…` or `sync/…` move work between machines; delete them after use.
8. Never record the code-signing identity, its team identifier, or a signed bundle in the repository, the documents, or a report. `build/` stays ignored.
9. Before the final `Docs:` commit of a batch, have Codex rewrite the batch's added comments and document text through its CLI (`codex exec` without a model flag; confirm the model and reasoning level in its startup output). Ask it to add no information, preserve meaning, and remove empty wording, then review its changes.
10. Report a check as passed only after running it.

## Build Procedure

```bash
./scripts/setup_cef.sh      # once per checkout, and again after the pin changes
swift build
./scripts/bundle_app.sh     # writes build/Arch.app
open build/Arch.app
```

- `scripts/setup_cef.sh` needs the GitHub CLI signed in with access to `taeyoon0137/arch-chromium`. It keeps the archive in `Frameworks/cef-download` only once its checksum matches the pin, so a run after an interrupted download fetches it again. `ARCH_CEF_ARCHIVE=<path to the archive> ./scripts/setup_cef.sh` installs a distribution from a local archive instead, to try a build of `arch-chromium` before its release.
- `./scripts/provision_app.sh` gets the provisioning profiles that give the bundle the iCloud and push entitlements, and `--dry-run` prints its steps and the installed profiles without running them. Xcode signs in with the Apple Account in Xcode ▸ Settings ▸ Accounts, or with an App Store Connect API key when `ARCH_ASC_KEY_ID` and `ARCH_ASC_ISSUER_ID` are set; `ARCH_ASC_KEY_PATH` defaults to `~/.appstoreconnect/private_keys/AuthKey_<key id>.p8`, and the key stays there. `ARCH_PROVISIONING_PROFILE` gives `bundle_app.sh` a profile of its own.
- `ARCH_SIGN_IDENTITY` overrides the signing identity; `ARCH_START_URL` opens a page at launch; `ARCH_CEF_FRAMEWORK_PATH` and `ARCH_CEF_HELPER_PATH` let a bare `.build/debug/Arch` find CEF.
- State lives in `~/Library/Application Support/Arch`: `session.json` (beside it, `session.unreadable.json` for a file that did not decode and `session-v<version>.json` for one from a newer build), `permissions.json`, `history.sqlite`, `site-search.json` (beside it, `site-search.unreadable.json` for a file that did not decode), `imports.json` (beside it, `imports.unreadable.json` for a file that did not decode), `CookieSync/<persona id>.json` (what the Persona's two cookie stores agreed on at their last copy, without cookie values), `Plugins/<persona id>/`, `NativeMessagingHosts/` (the host manifests of the native apps that web extensions may reach), and `Cache/Chromium`, which holds the default Persona's Chromium profile in `Default` and each other Persona's in `Persona-<UUID>`. Chromium keeps each profile's HTTP and code caches in `~/Library/Caches/Arch/Cache/Chromium`, where the removal pass also deletes a deleted Persona's directory, and WebKit keeps the default Persona's store, the other Personas' stores (in `WebsiteDataStore`), and the web extensions' data (in `WebExtensions`) in `~/Library/WebKit/xyz.taeyoon.arch`. The default Persona's WebKit store also keeps its cookies and HTTP storage in `~/Library/HTTPStorages/xyz.taeyoon.arch` and `xyz.taeyoon.arch.binarycookies` beside it, and its caches, among them HSTS, in `~/Library/Caches/xyz.taeyoon.arch/WebKit`, beside the app's URL cache. Delete these folders and files for a first-launch test. The Keychain items (AI keys under `xyz.taeyoon.arch.ai`, plugin secrets under `xyz.taeyoon.arch.plugin.<plugin id>`, and `Chromium Safe Storage`) and the preferences (`defaults delete xyz.taeyoon.arch`) stay unless deleted as well. `defaults delete xyz.taeyoon.arch ARCHAskedDefaultBrowser` brings back only the default-browser prompt.
- For a scripted check, run `build/Arch.app/Contents/MacOS/Arch 2> /tmp/arch.log &`, then inspect `session.json`, `sqlite3 … history.sqlite 'select * from visits'`, and the log. `open -a build/Arch.app https://example.org` sends a link to the running app. `pkill -9 -f "Arch.app/Contents/Frameworks/Arch Helper.app/Contents/MacOS/Arch Helper --type=renderer"` crashes every page's renderer; a bare `type=renderer` pattern would also kill other Chromium-based apps' renderers.
- For password manager checks, run `./scripts/install_app.sh` after `./scripts/bundle_app.sh`, then `open /Applications/Arch.app` (`docs/password-managers.md`, Installing Arch into /Applications). The script copies only a bundle signed by a team, replaces only an earlier Arch or a link, and refuses while Arch runs from `/Applications`; `ARCH_APP_SOURCE` and `ARCH_INSTALL_DIR` change the source and the destination folder.
- Launch through `open` or the Finder for any check that involves a system permission prompt. A binary started from a shell is attributed to the shell's parent, so the camera prompt never appears and the page reports a denied request.

Regression checklist, run from the bundle on the Chromium engine with a fresh Application Support folder:

1. ⌘F opens the find bar focused; typing shows "n of m"; Return and ⇧Return move through matches; Escape closes the bar and the page keeps keyboard focus, so Space scrolls it; switching tabs closes it.
2. ⌘⇧J and the library button toggle the library, which pushes the sidebar aside and dims its first 60 points; only one library button shows at the bottom left while the library is open, with none in the dimmed part; clicking that part or swiping brings the sidebar back, and swiping past the first space brings the library in; a file download shows its progress on the button and appears in the library; a page that opens two file addresses in new tabs at once, as with two `window.open` calls, downloads both files on both engines, and each of those tabs closes once its own download appears; pause, resume, and cancel change the row, and on a WebKit tab Resume clicked at once after Pause continues the download from its bytes so far; a download that finishes with the library closed adds to the button's badge until the library opens; Clear removes finished rows. ⌘Q while a download is in progress or paused, and closing the last main window then, ask first; while the question shows, a Chromium download's progress keeps moving and a video on a Chromium tab keeps playing; Cancel keeps Arch, the download, and a sheet that showed. With Settings ▸ General ▸ Ask where to save each download on, a download opens a save panel sheet in the chosen downloads folder; with it off, the file saves to that folder.
3. After visiting three pages, ⌘L lists them under the typed text; choosing one navigates; a reload does not raise its visit count. With a PDF dropped from the Finder onto the tab list, ⌘L and Return reload the file and search for nothing.
4. `open -a build/Arch.app https://example.org` at launch, or while Arch is active, opens a tab in the main window; while Arch is in the background, it opens a small window in front, and ⌥⌘N there moves the tab into its destination space: the last main window's current space when the small window opened. With Settings ▸ General's small window toggle off, a link sent while Arch is in the background opens a tab in the main window, and turning the toggle on again applies to the next link.
5. The first launch asks to set the default browser; Set as Default shows the system confirmation; afterwards the menu item is disabled.
6. A page requesting the camera gets Arch's card over its pane, then the system prompt; Allow reaches the page and is remembered, so the next request shows no card; after its answer is removed in Settings ▸ Websites, Don't Allow reports a denied request. On both engines, with the camera switched off for Arch in System Settings ▸ Privacy & Security ▸ Camera, the request is denied without Arch's card.
7. Killing the renderer helper reloads the page once; a second crash on the same page shows the notice with Reload, Reload brings the page back, and the app keeps running.
8. On both engines, after a tab loads three pages, the History menu lists the two earlier pages, most recent first; choosing the older one loads it.
9. Dragging the sidebar's trailing edge resizes it between 240 and 400 points in every window; the width persists after relaunch. ⌘S slides the sidebar and the window buttons away without showing the card. Moving the pointer to the window's leading edge slides in the card with the window buttons. The card hides when the pointer leaves. The card's sidebar button docks the sidebar in place while only the page resizes.
10. Dragging a tab leaves a dimmed slot that moves with a light haptic at each new position. The lifted tab follows the pointer over the page and returns to the slot on release. Dropping it among pinned tabs pins it. Double-clicking a tab renames it; the name persists after relaunch. After ⌘W, ⌘Z reopens the tab, including when a Chromium page has focus.
11. Holding ⌘ alone for 0.2 seconds shows each tab's ⌘-number in place of its close button. Releasing ⌘ immediately hides the numbers. Pressing ⌘L or ⌘-clicking and keeping ⌘ held shows no numbers until ⌘ is released, and ⌘2 while the numbers show keeps them.
12. ⌥⌘I opens the developer tools of a Chromium tab inside the page area to the right of the page, which narrows, and `NSApp.windows` gains no window; the Developer menu docks them below or to the left; dragging the margin between the page and the tools resizes them, and closing the tools with ⌥⌘I while the pointer rests on the margin leaves no resize pointer over the page; after the tools are dragged as wide as a maximized window allows, tiling the window to half the screen keeps the sidebar, a page at least 200 points wide, and the margin in the window, and dragging the margin past the window's edge stops there; selecting another tab hides them until it returns, with their panel kept; ⌥⌘I again closes them, also while the focus is inside the tools; on a WebKit tab it opens WebKit's Web Inspector inside the page, also on a tab opened while Use private APIs on `flags://` was off and switched on afterwards, and with that switch off the item is disabled for a WebKit tab. Inside the tools, ⌘W closes the tab, ⌘L focuses the address pill, and the tools' own ⌘F searches the tools. The sidebar card, menus, and popovers draw over docked tools, and VoiceOver reads them. Undock moves them into a window titled after the page, such as "Developer Tools – Example Domain" for https://example.com; Dock to Right brings the same tools back with the console's history kept; the window's close button closes the tools, and closing the tab while they float closes the window. With the tools undocked and another tab selected in the browser window, a WebKit tab among them, the Developer menu in the tools' window reads Hide Developer Tools and offers every side, ⌥⌘I there closes those tools and opens no others, and Dock to Right there closes the window and docks the tools beside their page once their tab is selected again. Sources ▸ Workspace ▸ Add folder declines access without a crash, with the tools docked and with them undocked and focused. Ending the tools' renderer process in Activity Monitor closes them and widens the page. Quitting with the tools docked, undocked, just closed, and just opened before their front-end has loaded leaves no crash report in `~/Library/Logs/DiagnosticReports`, also when ⌘Q is pressed while the page or an extension popup has the keyboard focus; then Arch quits within a second, as it does from the menu bar.
13. On both engines, a page element's fullscreen fills the screen and Escape returns; on Chromium the sidebar and window come back as they were, also when Escape comes while the window is still entering full screen.
14. On both engines, ⌘+ and ⌘- zoom the page through the same steps and ⌘0 restores it; another tab of the same host follows, a page of another host stays at actual size, and after relaunch the host's page opens at its remembered zoom once it finishes loading.
15. On each engine, ⌘P shows the system print panel as a sheet with a paginated preview, and File ▸ Export as PDF… saves a paginated PDF named after the tab.
16. After visiting three pages, ⌘Y opens the History window with them under Today, newest first; typing in its search field filters by title or address; double-clicking a row opens it in a new tab and brings the browser window forward; Delete removes the selected rows and they leave the ⌘L suggestions; Clear History… with The Last Hour removes only the pages visited in the last hour; History ▸ Clear History… brings the window forward with the same sheet, and while the sheet shows, ⌘W closes nothing; ⌘W closes the window and keeps the browser's tabs.
17. The share button and File ▸ Share… open the system picker below the button, or from the top of the window with the sidebar hidden or the library open; both are disabled on `flags://` and with no tab.
18. `osascript -e 'tell application "Arch" to open location "example.org"'` opens https://example.org in a new tab while Arch is active, or in a small window while Arch is in the background and Settings ▸ General allows it; text that is not an address, `open location "file:///etc/hosts"`, and `open location "mailto:someone@example.org"` open nothing, and `open location "example.org/search?q=a%20b"` opens that address with its `%20`; `get {name, URL} of active tab` returns the selected tab's title and address; `get name of every tab` lists every tab in sidebar order, then the small windows' tabs. Reading properties needs the sender's automation permission the first time.
19. ⌘, opens Settings with General, Site Search, Sidebar, and Appearance in its toolbar, and the window takes each section's title and height; choosing Bing as the search engine sends palette text to Bing; Choose… sets the downloads folder and the next download on each engine saves there; Set as Default… shows the system confirmation, and the row then shows Arch. With Settings open, choosing WebKit as the default engine in the site settings popover shows WebKit in General. With a provider's Edit… sheet open in Settings ▸ AI ▸ Providers, ⌘W closes neither the sheet, Settings, nor a tab.
20. With Arch in the background, a finished download posts a notification that names the file, and clicking it shows the file in the Finder; a finished download asks for notification permission only while macOS has not asked, a fresh launch does not ask, and while Arch's notifications are off in System Settings a finished download neither asks nor posts. With a website's notification shown first on Chromium, a download notification still posts, its click still shows the file, and it stays in Notification Center after Arch quits.
21. On both engines, after selecting three tabs in turn, a quick ⌃Tab returns to the previous tab; holding Control and pressing Tab shows the switcher in most-recent order, Tab and ⇧Tab move the highlight, releasing Control selects it with its page focused, and Escape closes the switcher without switching. Closing a tab with ⌘W, a page's own `window.close()`, and a file-only tab appear in View ▸ Developer ▸ Show Tab Close Log as User, Page, and Download.
22. Choosing Set Current Page as Home in a space's menu, loading another page, and pressing ⇧⌘H loads the home page in the same tab; after relaunch ⇧⌘H still does, and after Clear Home, History ▸ Home is disabled. A page playing sound shows a speaker on its row. Open Link in New Tab from the page's context menu adds a row with a dot that goes away once the tab is selected. Hovering a row shows its title and address.
23. On both engines, View ▸ Capture Viewport, Capture Full Page, and Capture Region… each save a PNG to `~/Pictures/Arch Captures` named after the tab and the time and select it in the Finder; the full-page file holds the whole page from its top, the region file holds only the dragged rectangle, Escape cancels the region overlay, and all three are disabled on `flags://` and with no tab. On Chromium, ⌘W right after Capture Full Page on a long page closes the tab without a sheet, and with no other tab of the site open, its `Arch Helper (Renderer)` process ends within a few seconds.
24. Sign in to a site in a Chromium tab and a WebKit tab, and to a second site; History ▸ Clear Site Data… and Clear reload both tabs of the first site signed out, they stay signed out after a reload and an engine switch, local storage set on the site is empty on both engines, and the second site stays signed in. Clear followed at once by ⌘Q quits within about two seconds, after the tabs close, with no Arch crash report in `~/Library/Logs/DiagnosticReports`, and after a relaunch the site's local storage on a Chromium tab is empty.
25. Typing `yt cats` in the palette shows Search YouTube for "cats", and Return opens its results for cats; `yt` without a space searches the default engine. In Settings ▸ Site Search, a keyword in use or an address without `%s` disables Add; adding keyword `ex` with `https://example.com/?q=%s` makes `ex hello` open `https://example.com/?q=hello`; editing and deleting the shortcut change `site-search.json`.
26. File ▸ Import Bookmarks and History… with an exported bookmark HTML file adds its bookmarks to the favorites and reports how many it added; importing the same file again adds none and reports them as already in favorites. A Chromium-format History database chosen while its browser runs adds its pages to the History window and the ⌘L suggestions; while a large one imports, ⌘L suggestions and ⌘Y keep answering, and the summary shows once its pages are in; a text file reports that it cannot be read.
27. On each engine, a page that plays audio with Media Session metadata shows its title, artist, and artwork in Now Playing; the play/pause key pauses and resumes it once; next and previous track are enabled only when the page set handlers, and run them; with two tabs playing, the selected one shows, and pausing it shows the other; closing the last tab with media clears Now Playing.
28. In Settings ▸ Shortcuts, recording ⌃⌘N for New Tab makes ⌃⌘N open the palette, also with a Chromium page focused, and File ▸ New Tab show it; the row's restore button brings ⌘T back; recording ⇧⌘T for New Tab asks before taking it from Reopen Last Closed Tab; a changed shortcut stays after relaunch. After recording ⌃⌘W for Close Tab, closing three tabs with it and pressing ⌘Z three times reopens all three. With another app in front, ⌥Space brings Arch forward with the new tab palette; after another global shortcut is recorded, only the new keys do.
29. On Chromium, `Notification.requestPermission()` from the console shows the card with the note that Arch remembers the answer; after Allow, Settings ▸ Websites lists the site with Notifications set to Allow; switching it to Don't Allow makes `Notification.permission` return `denied` and the next request fail without a card; removing it makes `Notification.permission` return `default` and the next request show the card. Closing a tab whose permission card waits leaves nothing in `permissions.json`. With a test Persona whose Chromium profile has not started since launch, removing the Allow that its Chromium tab gave a site, then opening the site on Chromium in that Persona, shows the card again. On a WebKit tab, the site settings popover says that websites can't show notifications in WebKit tabs. VI.7: under a bundle identifier macOS has not seen, the card says that macOS asks after Allow, and macOS's prompt follows Allow, and switching a site's Notifications to Allow in Settings ▸ Websites, but not a remembered Allow or a fresh launch; a page that runs `new Notification('t')` right after `await Notification.requestPermission()` shows no banner for it while macOS asks, and one it shows after macOS's Allow shows. With Arch's notifications off in System Settings ▸ Notifications, the card for a site's notifications says so and has Open System Settings, which opens Arch's entry there, leaves `Notification.permission` at `default` afterwards, and leaves nothing in `permissions.json`; once a site has Notifications set to Allow, Settings ▸ Websites shows the group with Open System Settings, which goes away after notifications are turned on and Arch is in front again. A notification card that waits on a background tab while macOS's answer changes the permission shows the permission as it is when it shows, and a card that shows while the user turns Arch's notifications on in System Settings loses its note and Open System Settings once Arch is in front again. With Arch's notifications on, `new Notification('t')` from an allowed https or localhost page shows a banner named Arch, `log show --predicate 'process == "usernoted"'` has no `Legacy client` error for `xyz.taeyoon.arch`, and `reg.showNotification('t', {requireInteraction: true})` from a service worker shows from Arch too, with no `Arch Helper (Alerts)` process running; record in Current State whether `usernoted` still logs `Failed to source application bundle` for `Arch Helper.app` at launch. A website's notification offers no Settings action in its actions menu, and clicking one brings Arch forward; record whether it leaves Notification Center. With another website's notification still in Notification Center, clicking a service worker's notification that has a tag, showing a new one with the same tag, and clicking that one fire `notificationclick` for each click and never `notificationclose`, and `registration.getNotifications()` lists the new notification until it is clicked. After Arch quits, Notification Center keeps a download notification and no website's notification. VI.8: clicking a page's notification whose `click` handler calls `window.focus()`, or a service worker's whose `notificationclick` handler calls `client.focus()`, while another tab or window is in front, selects the page's tab and brings its window and Arch forward; one whose handler calls `clients.openWindow(url)` opens the address in a new selected tab of the page's Persona, in front, and the promise resolves with a window client for it. With two main windows of different Personas, the other Persona's in front, `clients.openWindow(url)` from a site of the back window's Persona opens the tab in the back window's current space and brings that window forward, and `client.focus()` for a tab in the back window's current space that it does not select selects it there; the front window keeps its Persona / Space in both.
30. In a space's menu, Theme ▸ Automatic tints the window with the system accent color; changing the accent color in System Settings retints it without a relaunch, and the space stays Automatic after relaunch. In the dark appearance with Settings ▸ Appearance ▸ Space tint set to Strong, a Green, Teal, or Orange space shows dark sidebar text and icons while a Blue space keeps light text; Subtle restores the faint wash.
31. Dragging a favorite over another reorders the favorites, and dragging a space symbol over another reorders the spaces; both orders persist after relaunch. Dragging a tab onto another space's symbol lights the symbol and, on release, moves the tab to the end of that space's list; a selected tab takes the sidebar to that space. On both engines, a link dragged from a page, a file from the Finder, and the text `example.org` dropped onto the tab list each open in a new tab, and other text opens nothing. With the sidebar hidden, dragging a link to the window's leading edge shows the card, and dropping it on the tab list opens it.
32. ⌘, ▸ AI: after adding a provider with its key and choosing its model for Test Prompt, Send streams a reply and names the model; a wrong key shows "The provider did not accept the API key."; with a failing primary model and a working fallback, the fallback answers and its name shows; switching to Sites while a reply streams stops the request, and Features then offers Send; `ai.json` holds no key, and removing the provider asks for Touch ID or the login password. After `security lock-keychain` and a relaunch, cancelling the keychain password prompt that Send brings up shows that the provider is unavailable because the key could not be read from the Keychain, Settings stays responsive while the prompt shows, and cancelling the prompt that Remove Provider brings up keeps the provider listed with its routes. With a hosted `chatCompletions` provider whose reasoning model refuses `max_tokens` and asks for `max_completion_tokens`, Send with that model for Test Prompt streams a reply, and a second Send does too; a reply that the 512-token limit cuts before any text, as when the model thinks through the whole limit, shows "The reply reached its length limit before it was complete." in place of an empty answer. Settings ▸ AI ▸ Model Tiers shows Fast, Standard, and Deep at None, each with its sentence, and the page picker shows every page's name in full; a model and a fallback chosen for Fast and a model for Deep stay after a relaunch, and `ai.json` holds them under `tier.fast` and `tier.deep` with `isEnabled` true.
33. Typing `copy` or `url` in the palette lists Copy Page Address right after the primary row, with Page Address at the row's end; clicking it closes the palette, and pasting gives the selected tab's address on both engines. On `flags://` and with no tab open, it copies nothing. ⇧⌘C and Plugins ▸ Copy Page Address copy the selected tab's address, also with a Chromium page focused; right-clicking a page away from links, images, media, selected text, and fields shows Copy Page Address under Page Address after Reload, and right-clicking a video shows Reload without it; while a favorite's preview panel (item 38) covers the tab, or with a favorite of another Persona selected, ⇧⌘C copies nothing; a tab row's context menu ends with Copy Address, which copies that row's address. In Settings ▸ Plugins, Copy as a Markdown link makes them copy `[title](address)`; switching Page Address off removes its palette row, its menu items, and the Plugins menu, and it stays off after relaunch. Settings ▸ Shortcuts lists Plugins ▸ Page Address ▸ Copy Page Address with ⇧⌘C; recording another shortcut for it works in the menu, also after relaunch.
34. With a provider connected, View ▸ Show Page Reader on a Chromium tab and on a WebKit tab narrows the page, streams a summary, and names the model and its tokens under it; Stop ends a reply at once, also while a busy page is still being read; a follow-up question with text selected in the page answers about that text; a page of another site in the same tab starts a new conversation. Typing `ask` in the palette offers Ask About This Page, and Ask About Selection in the context menu of selected text opens the popover below the find bar, which answers a question and closes on Escape; a link in a reply in the reader or the popover opens as a selected tab in the same window, and in a small window as another small window of the small window's Persona, also after the main window moved to another Persona's space; selected text in a field offers no question, and after Select All on a page with an editable area, such as a mail draft, a follow-up question carries no selection. While a preview panel covers the tab, typing `ask` offers no Ask About This Page, and opening a preview closes the popover. With a model whose input limit is 20,000 characters, follow-up questions with a long selection end in "The request is too long for the model." with Start Over, which leaves only that question, answered. On an excluded site, the panel and the popover show Allow AI on This Site and send nothing until it is chosen, and the site settings popover's switch adds and removes the site in Settings ▸ AI ▸ Sites. With the provider's Requests per minute at 1, a second question within the minute waits; switching Allow AI on this site off meanwhile ends it with Allow AI on This Site once the minute passes, and Settings ▸ AI ▸ Usage counts one request. Without a provider, no page reader item, palette command, context menu item, or site switch appears. The reader, the popover, and Ask About Selection work the same on a favorite's tab.
35. Clicking a favorite opens its own tab with a dot below the tile and no row in the space's list; after scrolling and following a link in it, switching spaces selects that space's tab, and clicking the favorite there shows the same page at the same position with Back enabled. The tile's Close ends the tab and removes the dot, and ⌘Z does not reopen it. Quitting with two favorites open, one of them selected, shows the selected one at once after relaunch and keeps the other's dot; its first click loads the page it showed. Remove from Favorites closes its tab.
36. New Folder in a space's menu adds a folder among the pinned tabs with its name field open; dragging an open tab onto the folder's row lights the row and, on release, pins the tab into the folder; clicking the row collapses and expands it; a tab's Move Out of Folder moves it up one level, here out of folders, and Delete Folder keeps the tabs; folders, names, collapsed state, and membership persist after relaunch, and a `session.json` from before folders still loads every tab.
37. On both engines, with two tabs in a space, ⌥⌘= shows them side by side with a tinted ring on the selected one; clicking the other pane moves the ring, and the address pill, ⌘R, and ⌘F act on it. A tab row's Open in Split View adds a third pane, and dragging a fourth tab onto a pane's leading half lights that half and opens the tab there; with four panes the item is disabled. Selecting a tab outside the split view shows it alone, selecting a pane's row brings the split view back, and the split view persists after relaunch. A pane's close control removes the pane while its tab stays in the sidebar, and ⌘W on the focused pane selects the pane beside it. With a split view of two tabs and a second window on its space that selected the second tab, the first window's close control on the first tab's pane leaves the second window's tab as it is. On Chromium, a video's fullscreen in a pane fills the window and Escape restores the panes; ⌥⌘I docks the focused pane's tools beside the split view; Capture Region… and `flags://` cover only the focused pane.
38. On both engines, holding Shift with the pointer on a favorite shows its preview panel, and Shift-clicking a link in a page, including one with `target=_blank`, shows the link's page there instead of loading it. Escape, ⌘W, and a click on a tab row each close the panel, and the click still selects the tab. Open in Tab opens the page the panel shows as a selected tab. A previewed page does not appear in the History window, the ⌘L suggestions, or `session.json`, and a site that asked for notifications in a Chromium preview still asks in a tab.
39. ⌘, ▸ AI ▸ Providers ▸ Open Terminal shows the login shell: `tty` prints a device, `stty size` follows the window size, Hangul typed with the Korean input method composes at the prompt, Control-C stops `sleep 30`, also after many lines pasted into it, `cat` of a file of several megabytes keeps the browser windows responsive, `less` returns to the prompt's screen when it quits, text copied from a page whose copy handler puts `ESC [201~` and a second line in it pastes at the zsh prompt without running anything, ⌘Q while `sleep 30` runs there brings the terminal forward and asks (Cancel keeps Arch and `sleep` running), and a command-line AI tool installs and signs in there. Add Provider ▸ Command-Line Tool… with the tool's name and Find fills its path; with the tool's non-interactive arguments, Check shows its reply, and Test Prompt routed to the tool streams a reply. With only that tool, not marked Runs without its own tools, the page reader shows that the tool gets no page data.
40. ⌘N opens a second window cascaded from the first, on its space, with the New Tab palette and no tab, while the first keeps its tab. On both engines, clicking in the second window a tab that the first shows moves it with its page state, and the first selects its previous tab; a Chromium tab's docked developer tools move with it into the second window's page area, and Developer ▸ Dock to Bottom moves the tools of both windows. Each window keeps its own find bar, palette, renaming, and ⌃Tab order, and an `alert` shows in one window while the other shows its own sheet. ⇧⌘W on the second window keeps its tabs in the first window's sidebar. The Window menu lists both windows by tab title and not the docked tools, Minimize, Zoom, and Bring All to Front work, and Settings ▸ Shortcuts lists New Window, Close Window, Minimize, Zoom, and Bring All to Front without window names. With Settings open, closing every browser window keeps the app running, and a Dock click opens a main window.
41. Two windows with different frames, spaces, selected tabs, and sidebar visibility return after a relaunch, with the same window in front. A window in full screen returns windowed at its earlier frame, and one left on a display that has since been disconnected returns centered on the main screen. Closing the last window quits, and the relaunch restores it. ⌘Q with two windows and an open sheet quits without a crash report.
42. In a small window, on both engines: an `alert` shows as a card over its page, and Window ▸ Move to Main Window moves the tab with its card to the main window; text typed into a field survives the button's Move to Main Window; ⌘F finds in its page, ⌘L opens the palette with its address, and ⌘T opens the New Tab palette in the main window, which comes forward; a link with `target=_blank` or a `window.open` from a click opens another small window whose page keeps `window.opener` and takes the keys, so Space scrolls it, and that window closes within a second when its page calls `window.close()`, also from a timer while another app is in front; Open Link in New Tab adds an unread row to the destination space. The main window's sidebar, ⌃Tab, ⌘-numbers, palette, and Close Other Tabs leave the small window's tab out, and the archive timer does not close it. Closing the small window puts its tab in the closed list, ⇧⌘T reopens it in the main window, and quitting with it open brings its page back as an open tab of its destination space. Settings ▸ Shortcuts lists Move to Main Window with ⌥⌘N. The small window's bar is thinner than the main window's title bar: a compact title bar with its row on the traffic lights' line and the page 4 points below it.
43. New Persona… in a space's menu, named Work, adds a space named Personal after a divider in the bottom bar and shows it, with "Work / Personal" on one line at one size in its header, "Work" in regular secondary text and the slash in tertiary before "Personal" in semibold primary, "Work" truncating before "Personal" as the sidebar narrows, a double click on "Personal" renaming the space alone, no favorites, " — Work / Personal" after the title in the Window menu, or "Work / Personal" alone with no tab selected, and "Work / Personal" as the tooltip of its symbol in the bottom bar; the default Persona's spaces read "Default / \<space>" in their headers and tooltips, and renaming Work changes all three. While Work and Personal are renamed for a moment to Engineering Platform and Research Projects, the header at the default sidebar width shows the whole space name after a truncated Persona name, and double-clicking the space's name leaves the header in place with the whole name in the field. On both engines, a site signed in in the default Persona is signed out in Work, also after switching the tab's engine; after signing in to another account in Work, both Personas keep their accounts after a relaunch, and Clear Site Data in Work says "…in the Persona of “Work / Personal”…", also on a favorite's tab, and leaves the default Persona signed in. A second window on Work shows Work's favorites while the first shows the default Persona's, and a favorite added in Work stays out of the default Persona. In Settings ▸ Plugins, whose picker lists Default and Work alone, with Work chosen, switching Page Address off removes Copy Page Address from Work's palette, Plugins menu, and context menus and leaves it in the default Persona's windows. A default-Persona tab dragged onto a Work space symbol neither lights it nor moves; Move Left on Work's first space is disabled; Next Space from the default Persona's last space enters Work. Delete Persona… on Work, from a Work space's menu or from the Spaces menu while the window shows a Work space, asks "Delete the Persona of “Work / \<that space>”?", then moves the windows on it to the default Persona and puts its tabs in the closed list, and a Chromium download in Work interrupted beforehand, as by turning Wi-Fi off during it, leaves no `.crdownload` file in the downloads folder; after a relaunch, its `Persona-<UUID>` directories under Application Support and `~/Library/Caches`, its WebKit store, and `Plugins/<UUID>` are gone, and `session.json` lists no `removedPersonaIDs`. The default Persona's Delete Persona… is disabled.
44. On both engines, ⌘W on a tab that plays a video closes the tab and its sound stops at once; switching a playing tab's engine stops the old page's sound too.
45. Settings ▸ Plugins ▸ Install Plugin… with `Packages/ARCH/Tests/PluginsTests/Fixtures/Sample`, and again with a `.zip` of it made by the Finder's Compress, shows the review sheet with Sample 1.0, "Write text to the clipboard", "Run scripts on pages from example.org" with its switch, and the three files; Cancel leaves `Plugins/<persona id>/Staging/` empty. Install and Enable adds Remember Page to the palette (`save`), the Plugins menu with ⌃⌥R, a Chromium and a WebKit page's context menu, and a tab row's context menu as Remember, and a Remembered row below the favorites, whose click sets its badge to 1, also with a Chromium page focused. Activity Monitor shows one more WebKit web content process while the plugin runs, which goes about 30 seconds after the last command (whether WebKit ends it at once needs verification), and the badge stays. On both engines, https://example.org/ shows its tab's title with " · Sample" added once the plugin answers the page script, and https://example.org/private/ keeps its title. Wait Forever beeps after 10 seconds; the third within five minutes switches the plugin off, and its section shows why until it is switched on again. Changing its Label setting and quitting with ⌘Q at once keeps the new label after relaunch. Installing the folder again shows Update, with example.org's switch as the last install left it; a copy with version 2.0 that also asks for `<all_urls>` marks that site New and asks for Touch ID or the login password before it installs. With `plugins.json` moved aside and a folder put at its path, installing the folder again shows the write's error in the review sheet, which stays open, while the section keeps the installed version and `Staging/` holds the package; switching the plugin off then shows the error in red with Try Again under Install Plugin…, and once the folder goes and the file is back, Try Again clears it and the plugin stays off after relaunch. Remove… deletes `Plugins/<persona id>/org.example.sample/` and the plugin's rows, and it stays gone after relaunch.
46. With a provider connected, Settings ▸ AI ▸ Features lists Tab Names and Download Names switched off with no model. After turning Tab Names on with a model, a page opened in a new tab on each engine gets a short name in its row about two seconds after it loads, and Activity lists the request under Tab Names; a tab renamed by double-clicking keeps its name after a reload; following a link to another page replaces the name; on a WebKit tab whose page stops answering once it loads, as with `onload = () => setTimeout(() => { for (;;) {} })`, loading another site in the tab within 30 seconds names the tab for that site a few seconds after the first page's hook reaches its 30-second timeout; a page whose frames keep loading, such as one with rotating ads, gets one request per document on each engine; renaming a named tab to nothing shows the page's title, which stays after a reload and a relaunch; after relaunch the tabs keep their names and Activity lists no new request. After turning Download Names on, a finished download on each engine gets a descriptive name with its extension in the Finder and the library, Restore Original Name puts the first name back, and a download from an excluded site or from a page on one, or one saved through the save panel with Ask where to save each download on, keeps its name; a `.tar.gz` download keeps both extensions. With a failing key, Tab Names' row shows the error and, after three page loads, Resume; with the daily limit for automatic requests at 1, the second page load's row says the limit was reached. A tab that moves to another address within its document during Tab Names' 1.5-second wait, as a single-page site's link does, and a tab closed during the wait send no request for that page (Activity).
47. Once Settings has opened, Settings ▸ AI ▸ Features lists Plugin Writer, with or without a provider; Settings ▸ Plugins shows New Feature with AI… only while a provider is connected, and not while Plugin Writer is switched off in Settings ▸ AI ▸ Features. Writing "Copy the tab's title and address as a Markdown link from the tab's context menu" shows the review sheet with the model in its subtitle, the request, "Read the titles and addresses of your tabs" and "Write text to the clipboard", and the sources of `background.js` and `plugin.json`, and Activity lists the request under Plugin Writer. `Plugins/<persona id>/Staging/` then holds one package, and neither the palette, the menus, nor Activity Monitor shows anything of the plugin. Ask for Changes… with "Add the shortcut ctrl+opt+m" shows a new version with the shortcut, and `Staging/` still holds one package. Discard leaves `Staging/` empty. Writing the plugin again and choosing Install adds it switched off, with its footer naming the model, the date, and the request; once its switch is on, a tab row's context menu copies the Markdown link on both engines. Stop during Write Plugin keeps the description, and with a wrong API key saved for the provider, Write Plugin shows the error with Open AI Settings, which opens Settings ▸ AI. Remove… deletes `Plugins/<persona id>/xyz.taeyoon.arch.generated.<UUID>/`, and the plugin stays gone after relaunch.
48. Settings ▸ Plugins ▸ Install Plugin… with `Packages/ARCH/Tests/ExtensionsTests/Fixtures/Sample`, and again with a `.zip` of it made by the Finder's Compress, shows the review sheet with Sample Extension 1.0, "A web extension." and "Runs on WebKit and Chromium tabs.", "Read the titles and addresses of your tabs", "Use the extension APIs storage", "Run scripts on pages from example.org" with its switch, and the five files. Install and Enable shows the extension's section with the same access and "Runs on WebKit and Chromium tabs."; `plugins.json` gives its entry `engineChecks` with WebKit's and Chromium's answers, WebKit's framework version, and the CEF version. On a WebKit tab, https://example.org/ gets `data-sample-extension="ran"` on its root element (the browser bundled with macOS can inspect the tab), and the site settings popover lists Show Sample Popup with the badge 1; clicking it closes the popover and shows the popup from the site settings button with the tab's address, also with the sidebar hidden, from the top of the window; Escape or a click outside closes it. On WebKit tabs, an installed extension with an options page and a frame that its content script puts in pages, such as a password manager, shows the options page in a new tab after `runtime.openOptionsPage()` from its popup's console, a link with `target=_blank` there to a web page opens that page in another tab, and the frame shows in the page. On a WebKit tab, a link without a new window on the options page and a web address typed in the address field there each load in the same tab, whose row stays in place with Back disabled; `tabs.update({url: runtime.getURL("popup.html")})` from the popup's console loads that page in the web page's tab, and a `tabs.onReplaced` listener added in the background's console (the browser bundled with macOS inspects it) logs the new and the old tab ID; after a relaunch with the options page open in a tab, the tab shows the options page once the extension has loaded. Check 52 covers Chromium tabs. Typing `sample` in the palette lists Show Sample Popup, which shows the popup on a WebKit tab. A second window on another space and Next Space change which tab the popup reports as active. With a window of another Persona in front of one on the extension's space, `tabs.create({windowId, active: true})` from the popup's console with that space's window ID selects the new tab in the back window and leaves the front window on its space, and `tabs.create({index: 0})` while a favorite's or pinned tab comes first in the space opens the tab after the active tab. Switching the extension off removes its row and palette command and stops its content script from the next load; installing the folder again offers Update; Remove… deletes `Plugins/<persona id>/extension.<identifier>/`, and after a relaunch nothing of it returns. With a host manifest for an app that echoes its input in `NativeMessagingHosts/`, an extension with `nativeMessaging` asks for Touch ID or the login password at install and receives the app's reply to `runtime.sendNativeMessage`; one without the grant, or not in `allowed_origins`, receives an error.
49. On a Chromium tab of a site that changes pages with `history.pushState`, such as a code hosting site, following links within the site keeps the tab row's icon; a link to another host shows that site's icon once its page loads, and going back shows the first site's icon again.
50. On Chromium, with a local page whose `<video controls>` logs `enterpictureinpicture`, `leavepictureinpicture`, and `pictureInPictureWindow`'s size: the video controls' picture-in-picture item and `video.requestPictureInPicture()` open the system's picture-in-picture window, which grows from the video and plays smoothly, with no Chromium window in the window list, and the page logs the start. The window's play and pause button and the page's controls stay in step, and a page with Media Session `play` and `pause` handlers receives them instead. The close button pauses the video, while a camera stream from `getUserMedia` keeps playing; the return button selects the tab in its window and brings the window forward, also from another space and out of the Dock, and the video keeps playing. Switching tabs and spaces, minimizing the window, and hiding the app keep frames coming. `document.exitPictureInPicture()`, removing the video, navigating, closing the tab, a renderer crash, and a video in another tab entering picture in picture each close the window, also while another tab is selected and while the app is hidden, and the other tab's video then opens its own; a second video in the same tab takes the first one's place at its own shape. Quitting with the window open does not crash. While the selected tab's video is in picture in picture, Escape in the find bar, closing the command palette, and the ⌃Tab switcher leave keyboard focus in the page, so Space scrolls it.
51. On Chromium, the rest of the picture-in-picture design's tests (`picture-in-picture.md` in `arch-chromium`, Testing 1 and 6 to 9, Risks 4 to 6, 11, and 12): on check 50's page, videos at 24 and 60 frames per second, in H.264 and in VP9, each with an on-screen frame counter and a full-height bar that moves each frame, show no tearing in the system window. A video in a cross-origin iframe opens the window (note where the opening animation starts), and so do a site that streams with Media Source Extensions and a live stream. An Encrypted Media Extensions page with Clear Key and a Widevine page show their video in the window, not black. Activity Monitor and `powermetrics` give the CPU of the app and GPU processes and the dropped frames, against a WebKit tab's picture in picture and Chromium's own window in a stock distribution, and the app's IOSurface count stays level over 10 minutes of playback. Frames keep coming while a sheet shows and while the sidebar scrolls. The return button selects and brings forward a favorite's tab, a tab of another Persona, a tab of a space that no window shows, and a tab in a small window. Switching the video's tab to WebKit in the site settings popover closes the window. With another tab selected, `document.exitPictureInPicture()` from a timer and closing the video's tab from the sidebar each close the window within a second (note where the stop animation ends); this is the design's stop recheck with the screen unlocked (Order of work step 1).
52. On Chromium, the VI.1 checks (`chrome-style-extensions.md` in `arch-chromium`, How to test, with the app's spaces and small windows as the extensions' windows), with Arch's engine from `arch-8078-6` or a local build through `ARCH_CEF_ARCHIVE`: the sample extension of check 48 installs once and runs on Chromium tabs: https://example.org/ gets `data-sample-extension="ran"`, the site settings popover lists Show Sample Popup with the badge 1, and the popup shows the tab's address from the site settings button, sized to its content, takes the keys typed into it, and closes on Escape, a click outside, and the page's `window.close()`. An extension's `tabs.create` from its popup opens a Chromium tab in the same space after the current tab, a `target=_blank` link in the popup opens one too, `runtime.openOptionsPage` opens the options page as a tab, `tabs.remove` closes a tab into the closed list, and `windows.create({url})` opens a small window with the page, on both engines, whose window in the call's answer lists only that tab, fires `windows.onCreated`, and closes when the extension removes that tab, while `windows.create` with two addresses fails and opens nothing. `windows.getAll({populate: true})` lists the Persona's spaces with their Chromium tabs in sidebar order, and each small window with its tab; with the window in full screen, minimized, and moved or resized without a tab change, `windows.getCurrent()` on a Chromium tab gives the same `state` and bounds as on a WebKit tab; making another window key fires `windows.onFocusChanged`, moving a tab to another space fires `onDetached` and `onAttached`, and in a split view the focused pane is the active tab. With the sample's site switched off at install, its row offers Allow on This Site, after which the content script runs; an extension without an action whose `matches` name two sites, one switched off at install, runs its content script on a Chromium tab of the other without Allow on This Site and has no row there, while on a Chromium tab of the one switched off its script does not run and its row offers Allow on This Site, after which the script runs there too; after a relaunch it runs on both without a row, and an update whose review switches the first site off and the second on runs it only on the second, while the first offers Allow on This Site again; with a `document_start` script kept from running, the action asks to reload, and Reload runs it; `permissions.request()` for an optional site shows Arch's sheet, whose Allow grants it, and for an extension whose `host_permissions` has `<all_urls>` and whose site was switched off at install, `permissions.request({origins: ['<all_urls>']})` asks for Touch ID or the login password after Allow. An extension installed before VI.1 runs on Chromium tabs after the next launch, its installed `manifest.json` has gained a `key`, and `runtime.id` is the same on both engines. A package that only WebKit runs, such as one with `background.scripts` alone, asks for its Chromium version in the install sheet's last step, its Settings section, and on Chromium tabs, and the version chosen there joins it and runs on Chromium tabs; a Chromium package with the `key` of an installed WebKit package that both engines run installs from its review or from Install Chromium Version… as the plugin's Chromium version, while the WebKit package keeps WebKit tabs, and warns, for a package from another place, that it takes over the data that the WebKit package keeps on Chromium tabs; a `.crx` file installs under its key's identifier. With two installed extensions, Join With… pairs them as one section with a row per version, Remove Version… leaves the other in its place, and Separate Versions makes two plugins again; in a pair whose WebKit version, installed first, has no `action` and whose Chromium version has one, typing the Chromium version's name in the palette lists its action once, with the WebKit version's name, and choosing it on a Chromium tab opens the popup that the pair's row in the site settings popover opens; a version's request for access at run time names it in its sheet. After a relaunch, an extension switched off before it stays off on Chromium tabs from the start, also when it was the Persona's only enabled extension and Arch was ended with `kill -9` within ten seconds of switching it off, a switched-on one keeps its `chrome.storage` data, and removing an extension deletes its Chromium storage; an extension with `chrome.storage` data on Chromium tabs that is removed in a launch whose Chromium application does not start, as when `build/Arch.app/Contents/MacOS/Arch` runs with `ARCH_CEF_FRAMEWORK_PATH=/nonexistent`, leaves `Plugins/<persona id>/webExtension.json` naming Chromium and the extension's identifier, and after a relaunch with Chromium, also with no other extension installed, the file is gone, the extension no longer appears in Chromium's profile, and installing it again finds no `chrome.storage` data on Chromium tabs; with two switched-on extensions whose content scripts run on a restored Chromium tab, both scripts have run on it after the relaunch without a reload, and neither background has started twice. An extension switched off at launch, updated from a package of the same version with a changed content script, and switched on again runs the new script on Chromium tabs. A second Persona does not list the extension until it is installed there, and after that Persona is deleted and Arch relaunches, its Chromium profile and its extensions' storage are gone. With an echo host manifest in `NativeMessagingHosts/`, `runtime.sendNativeMessage` from a Chromium tab gets the host's reply through `Cache/Chromium/NativeMessagingHosts`. `identity.launchWebAuthFlow({url, interactive: true})` opens no window, the log says that Arch closed a window that Chromium opened, and a quit right after it ends at once. No Chromium window or views dialog appears, the developer tools open and dock as before, a link in a PDF still navigates, and `tabs.highlight`, `tabs.group`, `tabs.createSplit`, `tabs.discard`, and `tabs.move` into a window return errors while the app keeps running. A content script that matches `<all_urls>` runs on https://example.org/ and not on a page opened from a `file:` URL, also after the extension reloads itself, after a relaunch, and at the first launch with this engine of a profile that an earlier engine wrote, and the extension's `tabs.create({url: 'file:///…'})` opens nothing; an extension whose `matches` are `http://*/*`, `https://*/*`, and `file:///*` runs on web pages without Allow on This Site once both sites of its install sheet are switched on, and with only `https://*/*` switched on, it runs on HTTPS pages without a click, while an HTTP page offers Allow on This Site, after which it runs there and still runs on HTTPS pages.
53. In the default Persona, a site signed in on a Chromium tab stays signed in after the tab switches to WebKit. Signing out on the WebKit tab signs out a Chromium tab of the site after its next reload, and the WebKit tab stays signed out after a relaunch. A cookie that a Chromium page changes with `fetch` keeps its new value when a WebKit tab then changes another cookie, and reaches WebKit after the Chromium tab's next load. Across launches, with a test Persona that is deleted again afterwards, signed in to the site on both engines and holding another site's cookies as well: when the site signs out on a WebKit tab in a launch in which none of the Persona's tabs uses Chromium, a Chromium tab of the site in the Persona is signed out after a relaunch, at the latest once the WebKit tab has loaded again and the Chromium tab reloads; when the site renews its sign-in cookie on a Chromium tab in a launch in which none of the Persona's tabs uses WebKit, both engines hold the renewed value after a relaunch once tabs of both have loaded; when Arch ends with `kill -9` a few seconds after the site signs in on a WebKit tab, a Chromium tab of the site is signed in after a relaunch, once tabs of both engines have loaded, whenever the WebKit tab is. `CookieSync/<persona id>.json` names the cookies without their values and is gone after the launch that follows Delete Persona.
54. With `ARCHArchiveAfterHours` at 1, a tab selected right after launch and left selected until Arch quits 25 minutes later, before the archive timer first runs, comes back selected with its back list when Arch opens again 40 minutes after quitting, both when ⌘Q quits and when closing the last main window with its close button quits.

On both engines, a page from a local web server whose `<link rel="icon">` names `file:///dev/zero` shows no icon while Arch's memory stays flat in Activity Monitor; an icon served as an endless response stops loading at 1 MB, and one served a byte every few seconds after 30 seconds, both without an icon; a page with an ordinary icon still shows it.
56. On Chromium, with two main windows of one Persona, the front one on one space and the back one on another, and in the back window's space a tab that it does not show, whose page has a `beforeunload` handler and an edited form, Clear Site Data… for that site from another of its tabs in the front window moves the front window to that space, selects the tab, and asks Leave this page? there.
57. On Chromium, with the helpers in the macOS sandbox: no `Arch Helper` process runs with `--no-sandbox`, and each runs with `--seatbelt-client` (`ps -ax -o args`); checks 1, 2, 6, 7, 12, 23, 24, and 44 pass on Chromium tabs; pages with a VP9 video, WebGL, IndexedDB, Cache Storage, and workers, and a PDF, work, with no `Sandbox:` deny lines for `Arch Helper` in `log show` and no helper crash reports; downloads, uploads through the file chooser, printing, PDF export, the camera and the microphone, and Capture work on Chromium tabs, and an H.264 MP4 video with AAC sound plays with its sound after a click.
58. With 40 tabs of both engines in a space, each on a site of its own, among them a WebKit tab with three pages in its back list and a Chromium tab, a split view of two other tabs, and a second window on another space, quit and relaunch: with no extension installed, Activity Monitor shows at most one `Arch Helper (Renderer)` process more than the Chromium tabs that the two windows show, and the other rows keep their titles and, for sites that serve `/favicon.ico`, their icons. Until a tab is selected, every tab record in `session.json` matches a copy taken before the quit, apart from the shown tabs' activation dates, also after ⌘Q and another relaunch. Selecting the WebKit tab loads its page with Back enabled and its earlier pages in the History menu, the Chromium tab loads its last address, and selecting a pane's row shows the split view with both pages. A tab closed from its row before it was selected closes at once, and ⇧⌘T reopens it at its address. With an extension installed afterwards, its `tabs.query({})` lists a restored tab only once a window has shown it.
59. On both engines, with more tabs in a space than fit: scrolling the list fades the rows out under the address field and above the bottom bar instead of cutting them off, and at the top or bottom of the list that end stays sharp; closing a tab with ⌘W, its row's button, and Close Tab fades its row out while the rows below slide up, a new tab's row fades in, and dragging a tab within the list still moves the rows as before.
60. With the Mac on its power adapter and Low Power Mode off, the address pill shows only its share and site settings buttons after the site. Turning Low Power Mode on in Control Center shows a battery symbol with a yellow level before the share button of the address pill in every main window without a relaunch, also in a hidden sidebar's card, and before the share button of a small window's top row, the site's name truncating to make room; its outline stays plainly visible in light and dark appearance and in a space with a light, strong tint such as yellow; its help says pages may animate more slowly, a click and its context menu's Low Power Mode Settings… each open the Battery pane directly (Energy on a Mac without a battery), and the mode stays on; VoiceOver moving through the address pill reaches the site, then Low Power Mode, then Share; turning the mode off hides the symbol. To read the frame rate, open on a Chromium tab and on a WebKit tab a page whose script shows how many `requestAnimationFrame` callbacks run each second, such as a file `fps.html` holding `<p id=f></p><script>let n=0,t=performance.now();requestAnimationFrame(function r(x){n++;if(x-t>=1000){f.textContent=n;n=0;t=x}requestAnimationFrame(r)})</script>` opened by its `file://` address, and read it with the mode off and then on, still on the power adapter so that Chromium's Energy Saver, which starts only on battery below 20 percent, stays out. With the mode off, the Chromium tab shows the display's full rate (120 on a ProMotion display). With the mode on, 30 on the Chromium tab is macOS's limit described under Decisions, and the full rate there means that only Energy Saver slows Chromium on this macOS, which Decisions and row 69 of `docs/features.md` then record; the WebKit tab's rate falls to about half of its rate with the mode off.
61. On a Chromium tab and a WebKit tab of the same local page, with a paragraph, an empty text field, a text field with text and one with a misspelled word, a username field, a password field, a text link, an image, a link around an image, and a video, right-clicking each shows the same menu on both engines, AppKit's items included: AutoFill ▸ with Contact…, Passwords…, and Credit Card… at the bottom in the empty field and in a field with text; Services ▸ in a field with a selected word and on both links, with the link's services such as Add to Reading List; and none of AppKit's items on the page background, a selected word of the paragraph, the image, or the video. On the background, the text link, and a field of each engine, a quick right click opens the menu once and it stays open, and holding the right button for half a second before letting go, dragging onto an item and letting go, which runs it, and right-clicking elsewhere while the menu is open, which closes it and opens one there, behave the same on both engines, with no menu that closes and opens again by itself. On Chromium, AutoFill ▸ Passwords… in the username field fills it once macOS has asked for Touch ID or the password; a service that returns text, such as Convert Text to Full Width, replaces a field's selection; a service on either link receives the link's address; and Escape closes the menu. On Chromium, the password field's menu shows AutoFill ▸ with Passwords… and neither Services nor Writing Tools, also with its text selected; choosing a password fills the field once macOS has asked for Touch ID or the password, and afterwards typing in the field with a Korean or Japanese input source still enters only Latin characters, as before (VI.9). The known difference remains: a right click past a field's text, where its caret is, selects the word before it only on WebKit. On a Mac with Apple Intelligence on, a field's menu shows Writing Tools on both engines (VI.10).

Package proof: Settings ▸ Plugins ▸ Install Plugin… with a `.crx` file of `Packages/ARCH/Tests/ExtensionsTests/Fixtures/Sample` that a Chromium-based browser's Pack Extension made shows the review with "from Sample.crx" and installs it; `plugins.json` names its file with `signedSource` and has `"proof": "developer"` and no `source`, also after a relaunch. A copy of that file with one byte near its end changed is refused with "The signature of “Sample.crx” does not match its contents." and leaves `Staging/` empty. Web Store entry points (`docs/web-store.md`, Checks with the app 1 and 2), on both engines: Settings ▸ Plugins ▸ Install from Web Store… keeps Continue disabled until the field holds a store address or an extension ID, then shows the spinner and the review with "from the Web Store"; Cancel during the spinner closes the sheet and leaves `Plugins/<persona id>/Staging/` empty, and the same identifier again after the install says it is installed with Done. A plugin's Install \<Engine> Version… menu, the footer note's, and the install sheet's last step offer From a File… and From the Web Store…, which reviews the store's package as that engine's version. On a store page, the site settings popover's extensions section starts with Install This Extension…, or "“…” is installed." once it is, and ⌘L lists Install Extension from This Page; a pasted identifier in ⌘T lists Install from the Web Store below the primary row; each opens the same review in Settings ▸ Plugins in the tab's or the window's Persona, also after Settings ▸ Plugins was opened and closed once, as the popover's Install \<Engine> Version… opens its open panel. With no action in the section, the store row's note is "Opens the extension's review in Settings before it installs." or, once the extension is installed, "Manage it for this Persona in Settings ▸ Plugins." With two Personas, those reviews say "Installs in the Persona of “Work / Research” for all its spaces." and Install from Web Store… says "Installs in “Work” for all its spaces."; deleting the Persona while its store sheet downloads closes the sheet and leaves its `Staging/` empty; a start for “Home” while “Work”'s store sheet downloads or its review shows waits until that sheet closes, then shows “Home” with its store sheet; and a start while Install Plugin…'s open panel shows or its package stages opens only after that package's review closes. New Feature with AI…'s review says "Installs in “Work” for all its spaces." On a Chromium tab, the store's own install button, if the store offers it on Arch's engine, opens the same review in the tab's Persona, also in a Persona without any extension, naming "the Persona of “Work / Research”" with two Personas, and Chromium lists no extension of its own until the review's Install.
64. Web Store updates (`docs/web-store.md`, Checks with the app 3), with an extension installed from the store in a test Persona: Settings ▸ Plugins shows Check for Updates only while the shown Persona has a store install, and opening the section checks once, saying "Last checked at …" with nothing offered for a current extension. After the `version` in the installed copy's `manifest.json` is lowered while Arch is not running, opening the section checks and the plugin's section says "Version … is available from the Web Store." with Review Update…, whose store sheet downloads it and whose review, titled "Update “…”?", updates the plugin, after which the line goes; showing another Persona and back asks nothing again, and Check for Updates asks again. Offline, Check for Updates shows the system's message in red and keeps an update found before. Deleting the Persona while its check runs leaves nothing for it, and the remaining Personas' results stay.

Native app links, with `Messenger`, a copy of the folder `Packages/ARCH/Tests/ExtensionsTests/Fixtures/Sample` with `"nativeMessaging"` added to its permissions, and `Messenger.crx`, which a Chromium-based browser's Pack Extension made from that copy as in check 62 (the fixture keeps its permissions, since check 62 and the tests install it without Touch ID), and with a test host manifest `org.example.echo.json` whose `path` names a script that runs `exec cat` and whose `allowed_origins` lists the identifier of `Messenger.crx` and that of the `Messenger` folder, which has no `key`, installed with Install Plugin…, placed in a listed user folder that holds nothing else (the fifth of `BrowserSource.nativeMessagingHostFolders`, empty on this Mac on 2026-11-13) and removed afterwards. Each install asks for Touch ID or the login password, as `nativeMessaging` needs.
